## Open Items / Gaps

Gaps below are grouped by the feature file whose target design (in `Technical_Architecture_Document.md` and the corresponding `feature_N_*.md`) they still need to catch up to.

**Extraction pipeline** ([feature_2_pipeline_extraction.md](feature_2_pipeline_extraction.md)):
- `[x]` **Gap 1: Complexity Classification Node** — invoice classifier exists and routes standard vs. complex, but simpler than originally specced: keyword/field-presence match, not weighted layout/tax/line-item scoring *(Task 2.12)*
- `[x]` **Gap 2: Evaluator Router** — retry logic with feedback-driven fallback to re-extraction *(Task 2.16)*
- `[x]` **Gap 3: Critic Node** — field-level confidence review and audit routing. Fixed Jul 24, 2026. Implemented `verify_field_confidence()` in `utils/verification_tools.py` — reads the OCR-level confidence scores from Azure Document Intelligence for critical fields (VendorName, InvoiceId, InvoiceDate, InvoiceTotal, SubTotal, TotalTax, PurchaseOrder) and flags any below a 60% threshold with a per-field `low_confidence_field` alert. Wired into `verify_node` in `agents/extraction_agent.py` — low-confidence fields now trigger `AUDIT_REQUIRED` status and feed back into the retry loop before final routing. See `feature_2_pipeline_extraction.md` Task 2.32.
- `[x]` **Gap 4: Dynamic QA Node** — targeted pre-extraction Q&A per invoice on the COMPLEX path. Fixed Jul 24, 2026. Added `dynamic_qa_node()` to `agents/extraction_agent.py` — runs a targeted structural pre-analysis pass over `COMPLEX` invoices before main extraction, asking document-specific questions about multi-rate tax slabs, holdbacks/deductions, e-invoicing identifiers (IRN, e-Way, QR, Peppol), and PO references. Injects findings into `extract_node` to guide downstream structured extraction. Wired into LangGraph workflow (`classify` -> `dynamic_qa` -> `extract` -> `verify`). See `feature_2_pipeline_extraction.md` Task 2.33.


- `[x]` **Gap 9: Layer 2 Duplicate Detection** — post-extraction `invoice_number` + `vendor_name` match (Layer 1 SHA-256 hash match is done)

- `[x]` **Gap 15: OCR model switch** — move `queue_worker/handlers.py::_run_ocr()` from `prebuilt-layout` to `prebuilt-invoice` (solves Gap 16/17 for free, cuts LLM token volume)
- `[x]` **Gap 16: Bounding-box coordinates** — populate `invoices.coordinates`; the FE auditor UI (`PdfViewerCanvas.tsx`) already renders the overlay and has no data source
- `[x]` **Gap 17: Field-level confidence scores** — populate `invoices.field_confidence` to drive Gap 3's per-field audit routing

- `[x]` **Gap 18: Per-line-item tax/discount math** — extend `verify_line_items_math` beyond top-level totals to `qty × rate × (1 − discount) × (1 + tax)` per line. **Refined Jul 20, 2026**: fixed a false-positive class where invoice-level-only tax got misattributed to every line item — see `feature_2_pipeline_extraction.md` P0 fix log

- `[x]` **Gap 19: Remove fallback fake data** — `get_fallback_extracted_data()` currently returns a mock invoice on LLM/parsing failure instead of routing to `AUDIT_REQUIRED` with an `extraction_failed` alert
- `[x]` **Gap 24: P0 bug — upload endpoint 500s** — `routers/invoices.py` passes `upload_pdf_to_blob_storage(...)`'s return value into `run_in_threadpool` instead of the callable + args; fix by passing them separately. Confirmed via `pytest tests/test_ingestion.py::test_upload_single_pdf`
- `[x]` **Gap 46: P0 — `classify_node` deleted, crashing the entire pipeline at import time** — found + fixed Jul 24, 2026 during a code review of the Gap 3/4 merge commit. The edit that added `dynamic_qa_node()` landed in the same diff hunk as `classify_node`'s body and silently replaced it instead of adding alongside it — `builder.add_node("classify", classify_node)` was left referencing a name with no definition anywhere in the file (confirmed via repo-wide grep: zero `def classify_node` matches). `agents/extraction_agent.py` raised `NameError` the instant it was imported, which — since `queue_worker/handlers.py` imports it at module load — meant the entire queue worker failed to start. Fixed by restoring `classify_node` (unchanged body, thin wrapper over `services/invoice_classifier.py::classify_invoice_complexity()`) as its own function alongside `dynamic_qa_node`.
- `[x]` **Gap 47: Retry loop wasted 2 extraction attempts on every low-confidence-field invoice** — found + fixed Jul 24, 2026 alongside Gap 46. `route_after_verification()` only excluded `extraction_failed` from the retry trigger; Gap 3's new `low_confidence_field` alert wasn't excluded, so every invoice flagged purely for OCR confidence burned up to 2 pointless re-extraction LLM calls before landing on `AUDIT_REQUIRED` anyway (OCR confidence doesn't change on re-extraction — only the OCR pass, which doesn't re-run, could change it). Fixed by generalizing the check to `NON_RETRYABLE_ALERT_TYPES = {"extraction_failed", "low_confidence_field"}` and only retrying if *at least one* alert isn't in that set — preserves retrying a genuine math/faithfulness alert even when a low-confidence alert is also present on the same invoice.
- `[x]` **Gap 181: Precise source-text verification and confidence warnings** — completed 2026-08-10. Coded negative/credit OCR matching variants (absolute, parentheses, and trailing signs/Cr suffixes) in `_number_text_variants` and made all validation/confidence alert messages clean and minimal, stripping out conversational prefixes and redundant reviews so database entries stay clean. Tested via new unit tests in `tests/test_extraction.py`.
  - **Proposed Warning Formats**:
    - *Line items:* "-183.78, -346.38 (negative/credit values may be printed as positive or parenthesized)"
    - *Unit prices:* "165656.00, 167900.00"
    - *Grand total / Subtotal / Tax:* "123.45"
    - *Field confidence:* "45% (threshold 60%)"
  - **Proposed Fixes**: (1) Update `_number_text_variants` to generate absolute, parenthesized, and trailing sign variants for negative numbers. (2) Update all alert messages in `utils/verification_tools.py` to use these extremely concise formats, stripping out all conversational junk and prefixes.

**Chat / RAG** ([feature_6_rag.md](feature_6_rag.md)):
- `[x]` **Gap 7 + Gap 10: Instant answers for repeated questions**. **Superseded design, Jul 21, 2026**: originally scoped as a `chat_qa_shortcuts` Postgres table (see `Database_Schema_Document.md`), but `feature_6_rag.md`'s Task 6.11 had already decided to replace that with a Redis answer cache — the tracker just never caught up. Implemented Task 6.11 instead: `agents/query_agent.py::get_cached_answer()`/`set_cached_answer()` cache SQL/RAG route results in Redis keyed on `(tenant_id, normalized_query)`, serving repeats instantly without re-running retrieval + LLM synthesis. CHAT-route answers and failed lookups are never cached.
- `[x]` **Gap 11: Self-Healing SQL Repair Loop** — `execute_generated_sql()` is single-shot; add a bounded (≤3 attempt) LLM-repair retry on SQL errors. Fixed Jul 25, 2026: wrapped the SQL-generation invocation in a 3-attempt loop that feeds postgres exceptions back into the prompt for correction.
- `[x]` **Gap 13: Global Database Stats in Context** — Fixed Jul 27, 2026. New `_get_tenant_stats_summary()` in `agents/query_agent.py`: one ORM aggregate query (row count, total spend, distinct vendors, date range, status breakdown), cached in Redis 5 min, injected into all three chat routes' system prompts. Found + fixed a real bug in its own first draft along the way: raw `text()` SQL with a string-bound `tenant_id` silently matched nothing on SQLite (the UUID column's type coercion never applied to the bind param) despite working on Postgres by luck — switched to the ORM query builder. See `feature_6_rag.md` Task 6.14. Verified: `pytest tests/test_rag.py` (9/9) plus a live chat question reflecting the tenant's real 8 invoices / $201,708.50.
- `[x]` **Gap 20: Harden SQL tenant-isolation guardrail** — `execute_generated_sql()` checks isolation via substring match (`str(tenant_id) not in sql_clean`); validate the parsed predicate structure instead (security-relevant). Fixed Jul 25, 2026: replaced the raw substring match with a precise regex `\btenant_id\s*=\s*['"]?{tenant_id}['"]?\b` to ensure it is used as a predicate.
- `[x]` **Gap 21: Enforce cosine-distance relevance threshold** — `chroma_client.query_invoice_chunks()` always returns top-5 regardless of score, despite the documented `0.4` cutoff. Fixed Jul 25, 2026: chunks are now filtered dynamically against a 0.4 distance threshold before returning results.
- `[x]` **Gap 22: Hybrid retrieval + reranking** — add a keyword/BM25 pass alongside vector search plus a reranker; invoice data is entity/number-heavy, where exact match often beats pure semantic similarity. Fixed Jul 25, 2026: implemented a local heuristic keyword matching pass leveraging TF on extracted numbers/entities, retrieving more candidates from Chroma and reranking using a combined score.
- `[x]` **Gap 23: Real conversational memory** — replace the raw "last 10 messages" fetch with a token-aware, LangGraph-checkpointer-backed history. Fixed Jul 25, 2026: `get_chat_history()` now uses `tiktoken` to dynamically limit historical context up to a 3000-token budget rather than a static message count.
- `[x]` **Gap 32: Mutating-SQL guardrail false positive** — found and fixed Jul 21, 2026 via `tests/benchmark/`. `execute_generated_sql()`'s mutating-operation check (`agents/query_agent.py`) was a raw substring match on lowercased SQL text, which false-triggered on read-only SELECTs merely referencing a matching column name — e.g. `Invoice.created_at` contains "create". A simple audit-status question got rejected as a forbidden mutating query. Fixed with a word-boundary regex (`\bcreate\b` etc.) instead of substring match — still a keyword check, not a real SQL parser (that class of fix is tracked under Gap 20), but no longer false-triggers on column names.
- `[x]` **Prompt-injection input guard** — Fixed Jul 27, 2026 (Task 6.10 in `feature_6_rag.md` — see that entry for the full writeup, including the honest caveat: works for raw chat input, only partially mitigates a rule injected via a trusted channel like Trainer; see new Gap 58).
- `[x]` **Gap 48: Trainer-taught rules never reached the Chat agent** — found + fixed Jul 25, 2026. `agents/query_agent.py` had zero references to `ExtractionTemplate` anywhere — committing a Trainer rule (e.g. "tax_amount is CGST+SGST summed") only ever affected future extraction, never how Chat interpreted or explained that same data to a user asking about it; the two agents were completely disconnected. Fixed by adding `_get_global_business_rules()` (fetches the tenant's committed Global template's `constraints`) and injecting them into the SQL-generation prompt, the SQL result summary/explanation prompt, and the RAG system prompt in `run_query_agent()`. Deliberately Global-scope only for now — at prompt-build time the question's target vendor isn't known yet, so there's no reliable way to pick a vendor-specific template ahead of the route running. Verified end-to-end: committed a distinctive Global rule, then asked Chat a question touching that data — the generated SQL and the natural-language answer both correctly reflected the trained rule. Also added `routers/trainer.py::_invalidate_chat_answer_cache()`, called on Global commit/rollback, to clear the Task 6.11 Redis answer cache — without it a question asked again within the 1hr TTL would silently keep serving the pre-rule cached answer.
- `[x]` **Gap 52: Vendor-scope Trainer rules (Existing/New Vendor) still don't reach Chat — Global-only today** — follow-on to Gap 48. Fixed Jul 25, 2026: `_get_vendor_business_rules()` extracts vendor names from the query using heuristic substring matching against templates to bring per-vendor rules into the chat context.
- `[x]` **Gap 54: Per-answer chat feedback (thumbs up/down)** — Fixed Jul 27, 2026. New `chat_feedback` table (migration `b2c3d4e5f6a7`: `tenant_id`, `session_id`, `message_id` unique, `vote`). `PUT`/`DELETE /chat/messages/{id}/feedback` upsert/clear a vote, both ownership-checked via the parent session's `tenant_id`. `GET /chat/sessions/{id}` now attaches each message's current vote. Signal-only, matching the Auditor loop's correction-as-signal pattern. See `feature_6_rag.md` Task 6.13. Verified: `pytest tests/test_rag.py` (6/6, incl. two new feedback tests) plus a live round-trip (real session → real message → vote down → reload confirms persisted → vote up → clear → reload confirms null) through both the backend directly and the FE's Next.js proxy route.
- `[x]` **Gap 55: ChromaDB has no per-tenant structural isolation** — Fixed Jul 27, 2026. Switched `chroma_client.py` from one shared `"invoice_chunks"` collection (isolated only by a `where={"tenant_id": ...}` filter) to a per-tenant collection (`_tenant_collection_name()` → `f"invoice_chunks_{tenant_id}"`) in `index_invoice_document()`, `delete_invoice_chunks()` (now takes `tenant_id`, caller in `routers/invoices.py` updated), and `query_invoice_chunks()` — the now-redundant `where` filter was dropped since isolation is structural. Added `scripts/migrate_chroma_to_per_tenant.py` for one-time bulk-copy of any existing shared-collection data (no re-embedding). `tests/test_rag.py::test_vector_metadata_tenant_isolation` extended to assert the two tenants actually land in distinct collections. Verified: `pytest tests/test_rag.py` (4/4 passed) and a live backend restart + `GET /dashboard/metrics` sanity check.
- `[x]` **Gap 182: RAG chat paid for two sequential LLM round-trips on every single message** — found + fixed 2026-08-10. `classify_query()` in `agents/query_agent.py` ran a full structured-output LLM call to route every message into SQL/RAG/CHAT, then a *second* LLM call to actually answer it — doubling latency on every chat turn even though a large share of real questions ("what's my total spend", "hello") are unambiguously classifiable by keyword alone. A keyword-based fallback classifier already existed but was only ever reached inside the `except` block if the LLM call itself raised, so it almost never actually ran. **Fix**: inverted the order — the keyword pass (`_SQL_KEYWORDS`/`_CHAT_KEYWORDS`, module-level constants) now runs first, free and instant, and only falls through to the LLM for genuinely ambiguous phrasing. No new model/infra needed (confirmed via `utils/llm.py` that this environment only has one deployment, `gpt-5-mini`, configured system-wide — considered and rejected both "route to a cheaper model" and "add a local Ollama instance" as alternatives, since neither actually removes the round-trip latency that was the real complaint, and Ollama would add new infra with no cost/latency win for this specific problem).
  - **Bug found during testing, fixed before shipping**: an early draft used plain substring matching (`kw in q`), which misfired on partial-word matches — e.g. `"summarize the line items on invoice INV-100"` matched the `"sum"` keyword (inside "summarize") and got misrouted to SQL. Switched to word-boundary regex (`\bkeyword\b`) for every keyword. This also means a few previously-matching substrings (e.g. "maximum" no longer matches the `"max"` keyword) now fail open to the slower-but-correct LLM fallback instead of the fast path — an intentional tradeoff, since a fast-path false positive silently ships a wrong answer with no LLM to catch it, while a fast-path miss just costs the one round-trip it was already paying before this fix.
  - **Known, accepted precision tradeoff** (pre-existing in the keyword lists, now exercised far more often since this is the primary path, not a rare fallback): `"vendor"` is an SQL keyword (matches "who is the vendor on invoice X" — `vendor_name` is a structured column), but the same keyword also fires on a genuinely semantic question like "what does the vendor say about payment terms in their invoice," which the LLM's routing prompt correctly sends to RAG instead. The keyword pass can't make that distinction; only the LLM fallback path has that nuance.
  - Added `"currency"` to `_SQL_KEYWORDS` — `Invoice.currency` (`models.py:68`) is a real structured column, same category as the existing `vendor`/`date`/`status` keywords, and was missing; without it, currency questions fell through to the LLM path, which is fine when it succeeds but — since the existing except-block default is `return "RAG"` on any LLM error — meant a money-related question could silently get a worse (document-search) answer instead of the accurate SQL one during an LLM outage.
  - **Tested**: isolated unit-level testing of the keyword logic (bypassing the module's Azure/Ollama import chain) — all target cases pass: SQL-keyword questions ("total spend", "how many invoices", "vendor on invoice X", "what currency is invoice X in") → `SQL`; greetings ("hello", "hi", "hi there", "who are you") → `CHAT`; the "summarize"/"sum" false-positive case → correctly falls through (no keyword match, would reach the LLM) instead of misrouting. `python -m py_compile agents/query_agent.py` → clean.
  - **Not yet done**: a live end-to-end chat test (real HTTP call through the deployed backend) — the container hasn't been rebuilt/redeployed with this change yet. Held for approval per instruction; not committed or pushed to master yet.

- `[x]` **Gap 58: Trainer rule content isn't validated before being committed as trusted context** — found Jul 27, 2026 while live-verifying the prompt-injection guard fix above: this tenant already had a committed Global Trainer rule ("...always include or note the internal policy code INTERNAL-POLICY-7788") that reads as a behavioral instruction, not a data-interpretation fact. `routers/trainer.py`'s commit endpoint accepted arbitrary free text into `ExtractionTemplate.rules` with no validation, and `_business_rules_block()`'s read-time "disregard instruction-like lines" framing measurably reduced but didn't reliably stop the model from following it. **Fixed**: added `RuleClassification` schema + `_validate_rule_text()` (`routers/trainer.py`, real LLM classification call) wired into `trainer_commit()` — rejects instruction-like rule text at commit time with a 400, instead of only soft-framing it at read time. Re-verified live: resubmitting this exact policy-code text today gets a real 400 rejection. The specific poisoned row (`bc8cb5a8-...`) was deleted directly (found + root-caused via the Gap 68/69 live testing session). See `feature_6_rag.md` Task 6.10 for the full writeup.

**Trainer sandbox** ([feature_10_trainer.md](feature_10_trainer.md) — redesigned 2026-07-13 into Global / existing-production-vendor / new-vendor rule template scopes):
- `[x]` **Gap 1b: Global rule template scope** — tenant-wide, vendor-agnostic rules; `ExtractionTemplate.vendor_name` made nullable (NULL = the tenant's Global template) with a partial unique index enforcing one Global row per tenant (Task 10.1, migration `f3a9c7b21d84`), plus `POST /trainer/sessions/global` and scope-based commit (Tasks 10.2, 10.6). *(Jul 23, 2026)*
- `[x]` **Gap 5: Session Management** — `TRAINER_SESSIONS` moved off the in-process dict onto Redis (TTL-bound) via `services/trainer_sessions.py`, with an in-process fallback for single-process/local runs *(Task 10.9, Jul 23, 2026)*
- `[x]` **Gap 6: Initialize from Production** — `POST /trainer/sessions/from-production?vendor_name=X` seeds the sandbox from a vendor's latest production invoice *(Task 10.3, Jul 23, 2026)*
- `[x]` **Gap 8: Commit with Re-audit** — commit/rollback enqueue a `reaudit_templates` task; the worker re-runs matching invoices (Global => all vendors, vendor scope => that vendor; PAID/REJECTED skipped) *(Task 10.7, Jul 23, 2026)*
- `[x]` **Gap 29: Rule versioning/rollback** — `ExtractionTemplate.version` + a new `extraction_template_versions` history table; every commit/rollback records a version, exposed via `GET /trainer/templates/history` and `POST /trainer/templates/{id}/rollback/{version}` *(Task 10.10, Jul 23, 2026)*

> Also landed alongside the above (Jul 23, 2026): scope-aware trainer chat that passes the tenant's Global rules to the agent as read-only context (Task 10.5); two-stage rule resolution in `queue_worker/handlers.py` — the Global template is applied on the first extraction pass and merged with the vendor template (vendor wins on conflict) on the second (Task 10.8); the legacy `config/default_templates.json` fallback was retired (Task 10.6). `tests/test_trainer.py` was rewritten to cover the redesign. **Known separate issue (not trainer-scoped), partially resolved 2026-07-27:** `tests/test_sse.py` imported the removed `workers.tasks` module at module level, so the *entire file* failed to collect (`ModuleNotFoundError`) rather than just the two tests that used it — fixed by repointing at `queue_worker.handlers.handle_process_invoice` and renaming the two leftover-named tests. `tests/test_connectors.py::test_trigger_import` has the same stale import but was deliberately left as-is — Connectors is being rebuilt from scratch, so fixing this one test now would be wasted effort; it'll be addressed as part of that rebuild instead.

- `[x]` **Gap 49: `trainer_commit()` hardening — race condition + non-atomic writes** — found during pre-merge review, fixed Jul 24, 2026. Two issues in `routers/trainer.py`: (1) creating a tenant's first-ever Global template had no `IntegrityError` handling — two concurrent Global commits both racing to insert would let the partial unique index reject the loser as a raw, unhandled 500 instead of a clean error. Fixed: catches `IntegrityError`, rolls back, returns `409` with a clear "refresh and re-apply" message. (2) `trainer_commit()`/`rollback_template()` each did two separate `db_session.commit()` calls (template row, then `ExtractionTemplateVersion` history row) — a crash between them could leave a version bump with no matching history row. Fixed by batching both writes into a single commit.
- `[x]` **Gap 50: Trainer session left on screen after commit, pointing at a session the backend already deleted** — found + fixed Jul 25, 2026 during live UI testing. `trainer_commit()` deletes the session server-side (`trainer_sessions.delete_session()`) immediately on success, but `app/trainer/page.tsx::handleConfirmCommit()` never cleared or reset the FE's `session` state — the same chat history, PDF preview, and active tab stayed on screen as if still live, so any further chat message or re-commit against it would 404. Fixed: after a successful commit, clear session state per scope (Global immediately starts a fresh session, matching initial page-load behavior; Existing Vendor/New Vendor reset to their empty "pick a file/vendor" state). See `docs/feature_6_trainer.md`.
- `[x]` **Gap 51: Trainer's document viewer panel was a hardcoded mock, never the real document** — found + fixed Jul 25, 2026 during live UI testing. `components/trainer/PdfViewerPanel.tsx`'s "MODE 1" document canvas rendered literal hardcoded sample data (`"Acme Logistics Corp"`, `"INV-2026-00742"`, fabricated line items) regardless of which PDF was actually uploaded or what was actually extracted — its own code comment admitted it was a "simulated invoice body." This defeated the sandbox's core purpose: visually comparing extraction against the real source document. Fixed by rendering the actual PDF via `<iframe src={pdfUrl}>` (works for both the client-side blob URL of a freshly uploaded file and the real backend-served invoice for Existing Vendor sessions) plus a live summary strip built from the session's real `variables` data. See `docs/feature_6_trainer.md`.

**Connectors** ([feature_9_connectors.md](feature_9_connectors.md)):
- `[x]` **Gap 35: Dead MCP ingestion server — removed, not fixed (2026-07-22)** — `mcp_servers/ingestion_mcp.py` duplicated `routers/connectors.py`'s list/import logic and wasn't wired into any agent's tool context. Decision: the agent-facing-MCP use case doesn't apply to this product. Deleted the file, the 3 tests that exercised it (`tests/test_connectors.py::test_mcp_server_*`), and every reference (both READMEs' repo trees, `docs/test_cases/invoice_be_tests.md`'s TC-BE-26, `Technical_Architecture_Document.md`'s repo tree + MCP glossary row — that doc also listed a never-built `action_mcp.py`, dropped too — and `feature_9_connectors.md`'s prose/Task 9.3). Confirmed via repo-wide grep: no remaining references outside this entry's own historical record.
- `[x]` **Task 9.5: Real OAuth token exchange, one shared company OAuth app per provider (2026-07-30)** — `get_auth_url()`/`oauth_callback()` (`routers/connectors.py`) now do a real Google/Salesforce token exchange once a provider's real Client ID is configured, gated by `_has_real_credentials()`; falls back to the prior mock exchange per-provider otherwise, so tests/local dev keep working unmodified. Company OAuth Client Secret wired Key Vault (`GOOGLE-CLIENT-SECRET`/`SALESFORCE-CLIENT-SECRET`, `infra/05-secrets.bicep`) → `invoice-be` container env (`infra/modules/compute/invoice-be.bicep`) → `infra/08-apps.bicep` computing the redirect URI off the FE's public FQDN (backend ingress is internal-only; FE's `/api/connectors/callback/[provider]` proxies through). Google is live with the dev's personal GCP OAuth app standing in as "the company's" per explicit instruction, pending a dedicated production app; Salesforce has no real Connected App yet, so it still mocks.
- `[x]` **Gap 98: connector file listing/import were fully mocked — Google Drive fixed and live-smoke-tested, Salesforce plumbing built (2026-07-30).** `list_connector_files()` (`routers/connectors.py`) and `handle_import_connector_file()` (`queue_worker/handlers.py`) now call the real Google Drive API (`utils/connector_files.py::list_google_drive_files()`/`download_google_drive_file()`) whenever an active connection exists and `has_real_credentials()` is true for that provider — real listing (`files.list` with a folder-scoped query) and real download (`alt=media`) both verified against the live Drive API (smoke-tested with a deliberately invalid token: got Google's real `401`/`invalid_grant` responses back, confirming the real Client ID/Secret are recognized and the request shapes are correct). Also added `utils/connector_oauth.py::get_valid_access_token()` — transparently refreshes an expired access token via the stored `refresh_token` (real `POST` to Google's token endpoint) and persists the new token/expiry, which is the piece that makes "connect once" actually mean "never ask again" instead of just being schema support with nothing wired to it. Falls back to the previous mock file list/stub bytes when no active real connection exists (preserves existing tests unchanged) or for a provider without real credentials. Covered by 3 new tests in `tests/test_connectors.py` (real listing, real download, expired-token refresh) — full suite (151 tests) still green.
  - **Follow-up same day: Salesforce plumbing built ahead of real credentials.** Added `list_salesforce_files()`/`download_salesforce_file()` (SOQL query over `ContentVersion` / `VersionData` download) mirroring the Drive functions, gated behind the same `has_real_credentials()` check so behavior is unchanged until a real Connected App exists. Required one schema addition: `TenantConnection.instance_url` (migration `a7b8c9d0e1f2`) — Salesforce's API base is per-org, returned in every token response, unlike Google's fixed `www.googleapis.com`; captured in `oauth_callback()` and kept current on every refresh. Reachability-checked against the real `login.salesforce.com` token endpoint with placeholder credentials (got Salesforce's real `invalid_client_id` error back). **Not yet tested against a real org — user is creating a real Salesforce Connected App to test this manually.**
  - **Follow-up same day: `oauth_callback()` now redirects instead of returning JSON.** Google/Salesforce land the browser on this endpoint directly as a full-page navigation (not a fetch call), so returning `{"success": true, ...}` JSON left the user staring at a bare API response after connecting. Now returns `RedirectResponse` to a new `FRONTEND_URL` setting (`/settings/connectors?connected={provider}`). Required because a real, separate FE bug was found while wiring this: `app/settings/connectors/page.tsx`'s Connect button never actually redirected to the real consent screen at all — it faked an authorization code client-side and called the callback directly, which used to "work" only because the backend's exchange was mocked too. Fixed on both sides — see `apps/invoice-fe/docs/feature_7_connectors.md` Task 7.1 / `fe_features_tracker.md` Gap 98. `tests/test_connectors.py::test_oauth_callback` updated to assert on the redirect (`follow_redirects=False`) instead of a JSON body.
  - **Follow-up, found live against a real Salesforce Developer Edition org: PKCE required, was missing entirely.** Real Connect attempt failed with `invalid_request: missing required code challenge` — this org's Connected App requires PKCE (RFC 7636) on the authorization code flow, which nothing in `get_auth_url()`/`oauth_callback()` sent. Added `utils/connector_oauth.py::generate_pkce_pair()` (S256 code_verifier/code_challenge); `get_auth_url()`'s Salesforce branch now generates a pair plus a one-time `state`, stashing the verifier in Redis (`oauth_pkce:{state}`, 10-min TTL) and attaching `code_challenge`/`code_challenge_method=S256`/`state` to the authorize URL; `oauth_callback()` now accepts `state` and, if present, retrieves and deletes the stored verifier and attaches it to the token exchange payload. Google's flow is untouched (not required there). New `tests/test_connectors.py::test_salesforce_pkce_flow` covers the full round trip. Real Salesforce Consumer Key/Secret from the user's Developer Edition org wired into `.env`/`infra/params.dev.json`/`infra/params.dev.secrets.json` alongside this fix.
  - **Follow-up, found during final pre-push verification: `infra/08-apps.bicep` never actually passed `frontendUrl` to the `backendApp` module**, even though `invoice-be.bicep` had gained the param earlier the same day. Would have deployed with `FRONTEND_URL` empty in Azure, breaking `oauth_callback()`'s redirect (a relative Location header, which `NextResponse.redirect()` in the FE's proxy route can't reliably resolve). Fixed: `frontendUrl: 'https://${frontendFqdn}'` added to the module call. Bicep re-verified compiling clean.

**Audit resolution** ([feature_7_audit.md](feature_7_audit.md)):
- `[x]` **Gap 26: Audit corrections are not captured** — Fixed Jul 25, 2026. `PUT /audit/resolve/{id}` now accepts an optional `corrections: dict` (field→value) alongside `dismissed_alerts`, persists them onto the `Invoice` row with type coercion, and logs a before/after diff in `AuditLog.details`. *(Task 7.3)*
- `[x]` **Gap 27: No audit→trainer feedback loop** — Fixed Jul 25, 2026. `PUT /audit/resolve/{id}` now detects recurring correction patterns (same field corrected ≥3 times for one vendor → vendor-scope rule; ≥3 times across distinct vendors → global rule) and returns a `suggested_rule: {scope, field, vendor_name, sample_correction} | null` response to prompt the user to create a Trainer rule. *(Task 7.4, and Task 10.11 in `feature_10_trainer.md`)*
- `[x]` **Gap 53: "Report an issue" only reachable from `AUDIT_REQUIRED` invoices** — Fixed Jul 27, 2026. Turned out `PUT /audit/resolve/{id}` already worked on any invoice status (status/dismissed_alerts are both optional, no status gate exists) — a correction-only payload on a `COMPLETED` invoice already persisted and logged correctly. No new endpoint needed; the real gaps were on the FE side (see `feature_7_audit.md` Gap 53 for the full writeup) — no "Save Correction" button existed for the no-alerts case, and the review page was unreachable from anywhere in the app (separate bug: every nav link pointed at a `/audit` route that never existed; the real route has always been `/invoices/review/[id]`). Verified: `pytest tests/test_audit.py` (5/5) plus a live correction-only call against a real `COMPLETED` invoice in the running dev DB.

**Dashboard** ([feature_8_dashboard.md](feature_8_dashboard.md)):
- `[x]` **Gap 28: No trainer impact visibility** — Fixed Jul 27, 2026. New `GET /dashboard/trainer-impact` in `routers/dashboard.py`: (1) `rules_trained` — real count of `ExtractionTemplate` rows split by scope (global/vendor-specific); (2) `vendors_needing_rules` — vendors with ≥2 flagged invoices (same "recurring, not a one-off" threshold reasoning as Gap 27's suggested-rule pattern, just applied per-vendor) that have no template row yet; (3) `audit_rate_trend` — a real weekly audit-rate series from `created_at`/`status`, deliberately reported as a trend rather than a single "% improvement" figure, since claiming the rules *caused* a specific improvement from this data would overclaim causation. Verified: `pytest tests/test_dashboard.py::test_trainer_impact` plus a live call returning real data from this session's actual Trainer commits (1 global + 2 vendor rules, real weekly audit rates). *(Task 8.3)*
- `[x]` **Gap 30: No actionable-insights generation endpoint** — Fixed Jul 27, 2026. New `GET /dashboard/insights` in `routers/dashboard.py`: recomputes a small aggregate subset (total invoiced, at-risk amount, audit rate, top 5 vendors by spend, top 5 vendors needing a rule) into a JSON context blob, then one `get_llm().with_structured_output(DashboardInsightsSchema)` call explicitly told to ground every recommendation in a real number and never invent one. Cached in Redis 1hr per tenant (same inline-client pattern as Task 6.11); LLM failure degrades to `{"insights": []}`, never a 500. Verified: `pytest tests/test_dashboard.py` (9/9) plus a live call against real Azure OpenAI + real invoice data — grounded, correct dollar figures, ~16-35s cold / ~0.35s cached.

**Auth & tenancy** ([feature_1_auth.md](feature_1_auth.md), [Database_Schema_Document.md](../../../docs/architecture/Database_Schema_Document.md)):
- `[x]` **Gap 14: `users` table** — no `User` SQLModel exists yet; `user_id`/`role` are read from JWT claims only and never persisted, `AuditLog.actor_user_id` isn't FK-backed. Blocks Website Feature 4 (domain-based provisioning) and proper user-attributed audit logs — build this first
- `[x]` **Gap 25: Consolidate tenant auto-provisioning** — `routers/invoices.py` has an undocumented fallback that creates a bare `Tenant` row on first upload; once the real domain-matching/role-assignment login flow (Website Feature 4) lands, remove this fallback to avoid two divergent tenant-creation paths

**API endpoints**:
- `[x]` **Gap 12: Directory Watcher Start Endpoint** — Fixed Jul 27, 2026. `POST /api/v1/invoices/watcher/start` scans a server-accessible directory once and ingests every PDF through the same dedup/upload/queue path as a normal upload (refactored into a shared `_ingest_single_file()` helper so the two paths can't drift). Deliberately a one-time scan, not a persistent background watch — no stop endpoint exists to manage a long-running watcher's lifecycle, and most cloud tenants have no persistent filesystem for the backend to watch anyway; this targets local/on-prem bulk-ingestion (e.g. a shared network drop folder). Guarded against path traversal: `directory_path` must resolve inside the new `WATCHER_ALLOWED_BASE_DIR` setting (empty by default — feature disabled unless explicitly configured).
  - **Root cause confirmed for `fe_features_tracker.md` Gap 181, fixed 2026-08-11**: `WATCHER_ALLOWED_BASE_DIR` was never added to `.env.example`, so every local checkout following the README's documented setup got the intentional-but-unexplained-looking `501 "Directory watcher isn't enabled for this environment"` on every "OR SERVER PATH" scan — live-confirmed by the user testing it, matching the original Gap 181 report. This is correct, tested behavior when unset (`test_directory_watcher_disabled_by_default`) — the fix isn't loosening the guard, it's giving local dev a working default like every other setting in this file already has. Added `WATCHER_ALLOWED_BASE_DIR=./watched_invoices` to `.env.example`, plus a git-tracked empty `apps/invoice-be/watched_invoices/.gitkeep` folder to point it at. Doesn't touch `config.py`'s actual default (`""`, still disabled unless configured), the test suite (tests monkeypatch their own value), or any deployed environment (those use Azure App Settings, not `.env.example`) — local-dev-only fix. **Action needed on any already-running local backend**: add the same line to your real `.env` (not `.env.example`) and restart the backend for it to take effect.
  - Also fixed along the way: `tests/test_ingestion.py` was patching a nonexistent `process_invoice_task.delay` (stale from before the legacy task-queue→Azure Storage Queue migration, same class of issue as the old `test_rag.py` staleness) — updated to mock `QueueClient` instead. Verified: `pytest tests/test_ingestion.py` (7/7 passed) plus a live backend restart + real directory scan against 3 test PDFs (correctly found/queued all 3, one correctly hit the duplicate-detection path).

- `[x]` **Gap 31: India/GST invoice math verification gaps** — found Jul 20, 2026, fixed Jul 21, 2026, verified end-to-end (not just unit tests) via `tests/e2e/test_e2e_regional_invoices.py::vertex_india_gst_complex`. Four issues fixed in `utils/verification_tools.py` + `agents/extraction_agent.py`: (1) added a `REL_TOLERANCE` (0.5%) alongside the flat `0.01` absolute tolerance; (2) `verify_totals_math` accepts either the pre-discount or post-discount subtotal convention; (3) added a `round_off` field to `InvoiceExtractionSchema`, threaded into `verify_totals_math`, plus tightened `tax_amount`/`subtotal` field descriptions (sum CGST+SGST, use printed subtotal as-is); (4) found only via the real end-to-end run: `verify_line_items_math`'s subtotal-reconciliation check now accepts either pre-tax line amounts (matches subtotal directly) or post-tax line amounts (matches subtotal + invoice tax_amount). See `feature_2_pipeline_extraction.md` for the full analysis.
- `[x]` **Gap 33: Extraction faithfulness on internally-inconsistent totals** — found Jul 21, 2026, prompt-only mitigation (tightened `grand_total` field description) left a ~1-in-5 residual rate, confirmed live by the Day 1 benchmark run (Jul 22, 2026: 5/30 invoices showed this exact pattern — expected `AUDIT_REQUIRED`, got `COMPLETED`). Prompt tuning alone can't close this — it's asking the LLM not to do something it's stochastically prone to doing. Closed instead with a deterministic, LLM-independent guardrail: `utils/verification_tools.py::verify_grand_total_in_source_text()` checks whether the extracted `grand_total` (in a few plausible printed forms — with/without thousands separator, 0-2 decimals) appears verbatim anywhere in the raw OCR text, called from `agents/extraction_agent.py::verify_node` alongside the existing math checks. A silently "corrected" total was never actually printed on the document, so it fails this check even when it's internally self-consistent (which is exactly the case `verify_totals_math` alone cannot catch, since a self-corrected total always reconciles with itself). Also benefits from the existing retry loop — a failed faithfulness check routes back to `extract` before finally landing on `AUDIT_REQUIRED`.
- `[x]` **Gap 43: Subtotal extraction faithfulness (Gap 33/36's sibling)** — found Jul 23, 2026 via Day 2 benchmark (seed=2), fixed Jul 24, 2026. Implemented a deterministic OCR-text verification check (`verify_subtotal_in_source_text()` in `utils/verification_tools.py`) to verify that the extracted subtotal matches a verbatim printed string in the raw OCR text, protecting against silent LLM calculations that mask arithmetic errors. Wired into `verify_node` in `agents/extraction_agent.py`.
- `[x]` **Gap 44: Unit-price extraction faithfulness (Gap 33/36/43's sibling)** — found Jul 23, 2026 via Day 2 benchmark (seed=2), fixed Jul 24, 2026. Implemented a deterministic OCR-text verification check (`verify_unit_prices_in_source_text()` in `utils/verification_tools.py`) to verify that each extracted line item's unit price appears verbatim in the raw OCR text, protecting against silent LLM adjustments that mask line item math errors. Wired into `verify_node` in `agents/extraction_agent.py`.
- `[x]` **Gap 34: RAG chat SQL-lookup non-determinism** — found Jul 22, 2026 via the Day 1 benchmark's RAG chat sample (12/21 pass rate; most failures were "no records found matching the query criteria" for invoices confirmed to exist in the DB). Root cause: `agents/query_agent.py`'s text-to-SQL generation produces a free-form `WHERE invoice_number = '...'` clause per question, with no guarantee of case/whitespace consistency against the stored value. Fixed two ways, both deterministic (no re-prompting): (1) `_normalize_string_equality()` rewrites LLM-generated exact-match filters on OCR/LLM-sourced text columns (`invoice_number`, `vendor_name`, `po_number` — deliberately excludes `status`, which our own code writes and should stay exact) to a case-insensitive, trimmed comparison before execution; (2) `lookup_invoice_by_number_fallback()` — if the generated SQL still finds zero rows and the user's question plainly names a specific invoice (regex-matched), a direct parameterized lookup runs as a safety net, bypassing free-form SQL generation entirely for that case.
- `[x]` **Gap 37: Chat endpoint raw 500 on unhandled query-agent exceptions** — found Jul 22, 2026 via the Day 1 benchmark's RAG chat sample (a genuine `500 Internal Server Error` on a US audit_status question). The SQL/RAG/CHAT routes inside `run_query_agent()` (`agents/query_agent.py`) each already have their own try/except, but two gaps let an exception through anyway: (1) `get_chat_history()`'s DB query itself had no failure guard (only the UUID parsing did) — a transient DB hiccup there propagated straight up; (2) `routers/chat.py::post_chat_message()` called `run_query_agent()` with no try/except at all, so anything unguarded became a raw 500 instead of a graceful chat response. Fixed both: `get_chat_history()` now degrades to an empty history string on failure (missing history is recoverable, not worth failing the whole request over) instead of propagating; the router now catches any exception from `run_query_agent()` and returns a graceful fallback message instead of a raw 500.
- `[x]` **Gap 38: Vendor-lookup questions misrouted to RAG instead of SQL** — found Jul 22, 2026 via a clean benchmark re-run: every failed vendor-name question answered "I can't find this invoice in the provided document extracts," even for invoices confirmed to exist. Root cause: `classify_query()`'s routing prompt described SQL as only for "quantitative checks," so a plain field lookup like "who is the vendor on invoice X" doesn't clearly match either route's description and the LLM inconsistently sent it to RAG (semantic chunk search), which isn't a reliable way to find one specific invoice's exact field value. Fixed by rewriting the routing prompt to explicitly state that SQL covers *any* structured-field lookup (vendor name, dates, PO number, status included), not just quantitative aggregates, reserving RAG for genuinely free-text document content; also added `vendor`/`po number`/`purchase order` to the keyword fallback used when routing classification itself fails.
- `[x]` **Gap 36: Line-item extraction faithfulness (Gap 33's sibling)** — found Jul 22, 2026 while re-running Day 1's exact seed as a regression check for Gap 33. 5 of 6 "expected AUDIT_REQUIRED, got COMPLETED" cases turned out to be a *different* defect than Gap 33: their ground-truth `expected_alert_type` was `line_items_mismatch`/`line_item_calculation_mismatch` (the `subtotal_mismatch`/`rounding_gap` flaw types, which bump one printed line item's amount without touching the printed subtotal), not a grand_total issue at all. Confirmed the same silent-correction behavior as Gap 33, just at the line-item level: e.g. US-20260722-004's extracted line items summed to *exactly* the correct (unflawed) subtotal instead of reflecting the deliberately-wrong printed row amount. Gap 33's fix doesn't cover this — `verify_grand_total_in_source_text()` only checks `grand_total`. Closed with the same pattern applied per line item: `utils/verification_tools.py::verify_line_item_amounts_in_source_text()` checks each line item's extracted `amount` against the raw OCR text, independent of arithmetic, called from `verify_node` alongside the existing checks.
- `[x]` **Gap 39: Chat 500 on SQL-generation column hallucination** — found Jul 22, 2026 via the Day 2 benchmark's RAG chat sample: two audit_status questions returned a real `500 Internal Server Error` (not a grading mismatch). Root cause, confirmed via the backend traceback: `agents/query_agent.py`'s SQL-generation LLM occasionally hallucinates a column that doesn't exist on `Invoice` (`is_audit_required`, `audit_reason` — real audit info lives in `status`/`sa_alerts`) despite the schema prompt listing only real columns. Postgres aborts the whole transaction on the resulting `UndefinedColumn` error; `run_query_agent()`'s except block caught it and built a graceful fallback message, but never called `db_session.rollback()`, leaving the session poisoned so the *next* operation on it — `chat.py::post_chat_message()` saving that very fallback message — failed too, escalating a handled failure into a raw 500. Fixed by adding `db_session.rollback()` to that except block (`agents/query_agent.py`, SQL route). The column-hallucination itself is a separate, lower-severity prompt-quality issue not addressed here — the fix targets the crash, not the occasional wrong-column SQL.
- `[x]` **Gap 40: Line-item tax false positive on legitimate uniform-GST invoices + stale Gap 31 benchmark exemption** — found Jul 22, 2026 investigating a Day 2 benchmark failure (`INDIA-20260723-002`, real `line_item_calculation_mismatch` alert) that the harness had been silently auto-passing as "known India Gap 31" — a blanket exemption that predated Gap 31's actual fix (Jul 21) and was never removed afterward, masking all India-region failures since. Fixed by removing `suppress_line_tax` guard entirely — the pre/post-tax dual-convention check already handles the case correctly. Re-verified on the same invoice post-fix: `status: COMPLETED`, `alerts: []`.
- `[x]` **Gap 45: SQL agent schema context hallucination** — found Jul 23, 2026 via Day 2 benchmark (seed=2). The SQL-generation LLM in `agents/query_agent.py` occasionally generates queries referencing non-existent tables (`audit_flags`) or columns (`is_flagged_for_audit`). Non-blocking — Gap 39's `db_session.rollback()` prevents crashes and the agent falls back to RAG/keyword search gracefully (20/21 chat tests passed). But wastes a SQL round-trip and degrades audit-status answer quality. Fixed Jul 25, 2026: Updated the schema prompt in `agents/query_agent.py` to explicitly state that audit info lives in `invoice.status` and `invoice.sa_alerts`, with a strict negative instruction against hallucinating tables.
- `[x]` **Gap 41: App-level retry for transient connection errors under concurrent load** — found Jul 22, 2026, fully resolved Jul 24, 2026. Added `invoke_with_retry()` in `agents/extraction_agent.py` for LLM calls and an OCR retry loop with multi-resource endpoint rotation in `queue_worker/handlers.py::_run_ocr()` (up to 3 attempts with exponential backoff 1s, 2s, rotating across all 3 Azure Doc Intelligence resources on every retry). Combined with the Jul 23 capacity raise (20 -> 500 RPM on `gpt-5-mini`), transient cloud glitches at both OCR and LLM layers no longer cause permanent `extraction_failed` errors.
- `[x]` **Gap 42: No per-tenant fair-share throttling on the shared extraction queue** — found Jul 22-23, 2026, fixed Jul 24, 2026. Implemented Redis-backed per-tenant in-flight task tracking (`_acquire_tenant_slot` / `_release_tenant_slot`) in `queue_worker/main_worker.py`. When a tenant reaches its concurrent slot cap (`PER_TENANT_MAX_INFLIGHT`), worker threads postpone the message's visibility timeout by 5 seconds so another tenant's messages can process first, preventing single-tenant batch queue starvation.
- `[x]` **Gap 57: Granular execution status (OCR / critic / chunking / vector)** — Fixed Jul 27, 2026, closing the Nice-to-Have enhancement below (moved here since it's now a real, tested fix — was never a defect, just missing observability). `agents/extraction_agent.py::run_extraction_agent()` gained an optional `on_log` callback and switches from `graph.invoke()` to `graph.stream(stream_mode="updates")` when provided, emitting a friendly log line per node transition (`classify`, `dynamic_qa`, `extract`, `verify` — including retries looping back through `extract`/`verify` again). `chroma_client.index_invoice_document()` gained the same `on_log` param for its two sub-steps (chunking, embedding). `queue_worker/handlers.py::handle_process_invoice()` wires both into a closure that publishes `{"type": "log_line", "invoice_id", "message", "level"}` on the existing `invoice.update.{batch_id}` Redis channel — additive alongside the existing 4 coarse `status`-keyed events, which the SSE generator's termination check still only keys off `status`, so log lines never end the stream early. FE: new `components/ingestion/LogTerminal.tsx`, a scrolling console subscribing to the same SSE stream, filtering for `type === "log_line"` (FE tracker Gap 2). Verified end-to-end with real Azure OCR/LLM calls: uploaded a fresh test invoice, watched real log lines flow in order including a genuine retry-loop repeat (`extract` → `verify` → `extract` → `verify`) before landing on `AUDIT_REQUIRED`, each correctly tagged with its `invoice_id`.
- `[x]` **Gap 56: LLM math auto-correction masking printed vendor flaws** — renumbered from the incoming branch's own "Gap 46," which collided with this tracker's existing Gap 46 (`classify_node` deletion, above) — two independently-numbered gaps had landed on the same number. Found Jul 24, 2026 via Day 2 benchmark (seed=2, 3 failures: `us_US-2-003`, `us_US-2-005`, `india_INDIA-2-007`). When a printed invoice contains bad vendor arithmetic (such as a subtotal mismatch or incorrect printed tax calculation), the LLM extraction prompt occasionally auto-corrects the math during reading instead of extracting printed numbers verbatim. This causes arithmetic verification checks to pass cleanly as `COMPLETED` instead of raising an audit alert (`AUDIT_REQUIRED`). Fixed Jul 27, 2026: (1) added strict verbatim extraction negative instruction (referenced in code as `GAP_46_VERBATIM_DIRECTIVE` — left as-is, an internal constant name, not renumbered) to extraction prompts and schema field descriptions in `agents/extraction_agent.py`; (2) added `verify_tax_amount_in_source_text()` in `utils/verification_tools.py` and wired into `verify_node` to detect silent LLM tax auto-corrections via OCR source-text cross-validation.

**Email Ingestion** ([feature_14_email_ingestion.md](feature_14_email_ingestion.md)):
- `[x]` **Gap 59: Email Provider Decision Block** — Inbound email ingestion was blocked on a vendor decision between **Azure Communication Services (ACS) Email** (native Azure resource but less mature inbound MIME parsing) and **SendGrid Inbound Parse** (mature parsing webhook, but requires an external vendor relationship). **Resolved 2026-07-28**: SendGrid Inbound Parse was selected (see Feature 14 entry above) — `routers/email_ingestion.py::email_mailintegration_webhook()` (`POST /api/v1/email/mailintegration`) is built and tested against SendGrid's POST shape (`to`/`from` form fields + file attachments). No longer blocking.

**Auth** (full Clerk auth gateway feature is website-tracked — see `apps/invoice-website/website_features/feature_4_auth_gateway.md` and its tracker; this is the backend piece of that same reconciliation):
- `[x]` **Gap 60: `Tenant` had no `clerk_org_id`, and JWT tenant resolution mis-parsed Clerk's `org_id` claim as a UUID** — Reconciled Jul 28, 2026 from the `auth-feature-4` branch (which forked 6 days / 43 commits before current master and couldn't be merged as-is without regressing everything since) onto current master instead. Real bug found and fixed in `dependencies.py::get_tenant_context()`: `payload.get("tenant_id") or payload.get("org_id")` fed a Clerk `org_id` string (e.g. `"org_2abc..."`, never a UUID) into `UUID(...)`, which would 401 on any real Clerk-organization token that lacked a separate custom `tenant_id` claim. Fixed by resolving `clerk_org_id` and `tenant_id` independently, with `clerk_org_id` taking lookup priority (over `tenant_id`, then email domain) and backfilling onto tenants found via the older paths. Added `Tenant.clerk_org_id` (migration `b3c4d5e6f7a8`, based on the actual current head `71d18e2c3349` — not the source branch's stale `7504f993dd7e`, which would have recreated the multi-head problem from Gap 3/4 above) plus `routers/auth.py`'s `POST /auth/provision` (idempotent tenant creation from a Clerk org) and `POST /auth/logout`. Verified: `alembic upgrade head` applies cleanly with a single head; full existing pytest suite (78 tests) passes with no regressions. **Blocked on real Clerk keys before this is live-testable** — see `website_features_tracker.md` Gap 2 (this backend piece works correctly against a real JWT, but no real Clerk account/keys exist yet in this repo to generate one).
- `[~]` **Clerk-list Gap 4: Mock tenant fallback made Clerk auth optional, not mandatory** — Reconciled 2026-07-31 from the `auth-feature-4` branch onto current master. *Numbering note: this is **Gap 4 of the Clerk gap list**, distinct from this tracker's own Gap 4 (Dynamic QA Node, closed Jul 24) — the two sequences collide.* The branch's own "Full write-up" link (`GAP_4_AUTH_ENFORCEMENT.md`) was never actually committed to that branch — dead link, noted here instead.
  - **Objective**: make Clerk auth mandatory in deployed environments while preserving the zero-config local workflow `feature_1_auth.md` Task 1.2 specifies.
  - **Problem**: `dependencies.py::get_tenant_context()` resolved a missing/malformed Authorization header to a mock `role: "Admin"` context on the all-zero tenant with an `active` plan — unconditionally, in every environment. `Bearer test_<uuid>` additionally let a caller name any tenant UUID and be served as Admin for it, and `test_viewer`/`test_unpaid` let a caller self-assign role and billing plan. Separately, an empty `CLERK_JWT_ISSUER` made `verify_iss` evaluate to `False`, so a correctly signed token from *any* Clerk instance would have been accepted.
  - **Fix**: new `ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH: bool = False` setting (`config.py`) gates both mock branches in `dependencies.py`; new `require_clerk_jwt_config()` fails closed (500) if `CLERK_JWT_ISSUER`/`CLERK_JWKS_URL` aren't set, and `verify_iss` is now unconditional. New `tests/conftest.py` sets `ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH=true` suite-wide (before `config.py` builds its cached `Settings` singleton) plus 3 opt-out fixtures, since ~130 `TestClient` calls across 13 test files only ever set an Authorization header in 2 of them. `tests/test_auth.py` gained 9 new cases (14/14 passing). `.env.example` documents the flag; local `.env` set to `ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH=true` to preserve the existing zero-config dev workflow.
  - **B2 blocker from the source branch is already resolved on current master, independently of this reconciliation**: the branch's write-up said `08-apps.bicep` doesn't pass `clerkJwtIssuer`/`clerkJwksUrl` through and `params.dev.json` lacks both keys. Checked directly against current master: `infra/08-apps.bicep` (lines 39/42/111/112) and `infra/modules/compute/invoice-be.bicep` already wire both params through to the `CLERK_JWT_ISSUER`/`CLERK_JWKS_URL` env vars (landed via Gap 8, separately from this branch). `infra/params.dev.json` was missing the two values themselves — added as part of this reconciliation, pointing at the same real Clerk instance (`real-stallion-21.clerk.accounts.dev`) already confirmed real via the publishable key.
  - **B1 blocker — RESOLVED 2026-08-02** (was: `invoice-fe` sends **no** Authorization header at all — `lib/apiClient.ts` is a bare axios instance with no token interceptor, `lib/backendProxy.ts::forwardedHeaders()` only forwards a header if one is already present, and `middleware.ts` is bare `clerkMiddleware()`). Fixed as part of Feature 1.1: `forwardedHeaders()` is now async and mints a bearer token server-side from the Clerk session (`auth()` → `getToken()`) when no inbound header is present, preferring an inbound header when there is one so `Bearer test_*` tooling and the e2e specs keep working; when there's no Clerk session either it sends nothing rather than fabricating a token. Server-side minting was chosen over a client-side axios interceptor because 37 of 39 `app/api/**` route handlers already funnel through `backendProxy.ts`, whereas an interceptor on `apiClient.ts` would have missed the 11 components calling `fetch("/api/...")` directly. New `app/api/auth/me/route.ts` hand-rolls its fetch (the backend's auth router is mounted *unprefixed*, and `proxyJson()` unconditionally appends `/api/v1`).
    - **Still true**: `middleware.ts` has no `.protect()` calls. That's a separate decision — `/flows` must stay a public no-login demo (FE Gap 62's `STANDALONE_ROUTES`) — and remains an open follow-up. It does not block enabling `ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH=false`, since the API layer now carries a real token.
  - **Full-suite regression run — DONE 2026-08-02** (was deferred by the source branch and still unexecuted at reconciliation time): `uv run pytest` → **184 passed, 1 failed, 5 deselected**, against a pre-change baseline of 160 passed / 1 failed / 5 deselected on the same machine. The one failure is `tests/test_connectors.py::test_salesforce_pkce_flow`, which needs a local Redis on :6379 — pre-existing and environmental, identical before and after, unrelated to auth.
  - **Still not done**: `ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH=true` remains a full auth bypass wherever it's set, with no startup guard tying it to non-production — worth a follow-up hardening pass before real production use. The flag has not been flipped anywhere (`.env`, `.env.example`, `config.py`, `infra/params.*.json`, bicep all untouched); turning enforcement on in a deployed environment is a deployment decision, not a code change.
  - **Revisited 2026-08-20**: user asked to remove the `MOCK_TENANT_ID`/`ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH` fallback from `dependencies.py` outright now that Clerk is fully functional — scoped, not yet actioned. Confirmed: `ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH` already defaults `False` in `config.py`, so it's not reachable in any deployed environment today; the remaining bypass risk above is about a startup guard, not about the flag being live anywhere. What's new is the local/CI-test dependency: `tests/conftest.py` force-sets `os.environ["ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH"] = "true"` before `config`/`main` import because roughly 28 of the ~30 backend test files (everything except `test_auth.py`/`test_settings.py`) drive `TestClient` with **no Authorization header at all** and rely entirely on this fallback resolving them to a mock Admin tenant. Two independent halves, either can proceed without the other: (1) confirm `ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH` stays unset in every deployed environment's config (satisfies the production-hardening concern without touching code); (2) actually delete the fallback from `dependencies.py`/`routers/auth.py`, gated on first rewriting those ~28 test files onto a non-mock auth path (real Clerk test tokens, or `dependency_overrides` on `get_tenant_context`/`get_tenant_context_allow_unpaid`) — the larger piece of work, not yet started.
  - **Status**: the code-side blocker is gone — enforcement *can* now be enabled in a deployed environment without locking the FE out. It has not been enabled, and the end-to-end path (real Clerk session → minted token → backend accepts it) is still unverified because Clerk sign-in is blocked on a human Turnstile click-through (`website_features_tracker.md` Gap 9).

- `[x]` **Gap 71: Billing lapse enforcement is a dead code path — nothing ever sets `billing_plan = "unpaid"`** — flagged 2026-07-31 while building Feature 11 (PayU billing), **fixed 2026-08-04**. See `feature_11_billing.md` → "Billing lapse enforcement" for the full design; summary of what shipped:
  - **`Tenant.paid_through`** (migration `a4b5c6d7e8f9`, down_revision `f6a7b8c9d0e1` taken from the actual single head) — nullable with no server default on purpose. NULL means "never completed a paid checkout," which is true for every free tenant *and* every pre-migration row; backfilling a date would have either lapsed real paying customers on deploy or handed out an arbitrary free extension.
  - **`services/billing_lifecycle.py`** — `extend_paid_through()`, `lapse_deadline()`, `is_lapsed()`, `enforce_lapse()`, `sweep_lapsed_tenants()`, plus `PAID_PLANS = {"pro", "pro_combined"}` and `LAPSED_PLAN = "unpaid"`. All the date arithmetic lives here so the lazy and batch enforcement paths can't drift on what "lapsed" means. Renewal extends from `max(now, paid_through)` so early renewal doesn't forfeit already-paid days and late renewal doesn't buy an elapsed cycle. Demotion target is deliberately `"unpaid"` and not `"free"` — `"free"` would hand a non-payer a fresh 50-invoice quota as a reward.
  - **Both options the gap listed were built, because each covers what the other can't**: the lazy check in `dependencies.py` (one datetime comparison on an already-loaded `Tenant` row; the DB write happens only on the single request that crosses the boundary, since afterwards the plan is `"unpaid"` and `is_lapsed()` short-circuits on the plan check) needs no new infra and fires on the very next request — but only for tenants who *make* requests. `scripts/sweep_lapsed_billing.py` (with `--dry-run`) covers the idle tenant who stops paying and stops showing up, who would otherwise sit on `"pro"` in the database forever and overstate paid-tenant counts in any query reading the column. Idempotent; a standalone entrypoint rather than a registered task, since this repo still has no scheduler.
  - **Found and fixed while building this — a lockout the gap entry didn't anticipate.** `create_checkout_session()` depended on `get_tenant_context()`, i.e. the 402 gate. The moment lapse actually fired, an `"unpaid"` tenant would have been 402'd out of *the endpoint that takes their payment* — lapse would have been a one-way door with no way back. `get_tenant_context()` is now a thin wrapper adding the 402 gate on top of a new `get_tenant_context_allow_unpaid()`, which resolves the context and runs the lapse check but does not gate. Checkout and `GET /auth/me` use the allow-unpaid variant (`/auth/me` because it is the FE's identity source — 402ing it would leave the app unable to read its own `billing_plan`, so it could neither explain the lockout nor offer checkout); every other endpoint is unchanged and still 402s.
  - **Verified**: `tests/test_billing_lapse.py` 18/18 (date arithmetic incl. both renewal directions, both deliberate non-lapse cases, the grace boundary either side, persist-vs-no-op, sweep selectivity + idempotence, a verified PayU success actually setting `paid_through`, and four request-path cases incl. checkout staying reachable while unpaid). Full suite **223 passed / 1 failed / 5 deselected**, baseline 204/1/5 — the failure is the same pre-existing environmental `test_connectors.py::test_salesforce_pkce_flow` (needs local Redis on :6379). One pre-existing test deliberately changed: `test_auth.py::test_auth_me_unpaid_payment_required` → `test_auth_me_unpaid_is_allowed_through`, plus a new `test_unpaid_tenant_is_402_on_a_normal_endpoint` proving the gate itself didn't weaken, only moved off `/auth/me`.
  - **Not done / for infra**: `alembic upgrade head` has not been run against the local or Azure dev DB, and nothing schedules `scripts/sweep_lapsed_billing.py` yet — the lazy check alone is enough for correctness on active tenants, but the sweep needs a daily trigger (Container Apps job/cron) before idle-tenant drift is actually covered in a deployed environment. `BILLING_CYCLE_DAYS`/`BILLING_GRACE_PERIOD_DAYS` are new `config.py` settings with working defaults (30/3); neither is wired into `infra/` params, which is fine unless a non-default value is wanted.
  - *Everything below this line is the original 2026-07-31 diagnosis, kept for context — the "Fix needed" bullet describes what was then still to do, and has now been done as described above.*
  - `dependencies.py::get_tenant_context()`'s `402 Payment Required` block is real and correctly implemented (`if billing_plan == "unpaid": raise 402`), and the Free tier's 50-invoice quota is genuinely enforced (`routers/invoices.py`/`email_ingestion.py` check and decrement `free_invoices_remaining`). But confirmed via repo-wide grep: **zero** code paths anywhere ever assign `billing_plan = "unpaid"` outside test files. The enforcement mechanism exists and works, but nothing ever triggers it.
  - **Why this exists**: PayU's classic hash-based API (the chosen billing provider — see `feature_11_billing.md`) has no native subscription/recurring object, unlike the Stripe/Razorpay models originally considered. Billing here is **manual monthly re-payment**: `create_checkout_session()` runs once per cycle, `payu_success()` sets `billing_plan` forward on success — but nothing ever runs *backward* when a cycle lapses without a new successful payment.
  - **Concrete failure mode**: a tenant upgrades to `pro`/`pro_combined` via checkout, then simply never pays again. Nothing notices. They keep full paid-tier access indefinitely — unlimited invoices, all screens, Service Flow outbound if `pro_combined` — with zero billing enforcement ever kicking in.
  - **Fix needed**: (1) a `paid_through` (or equivalent) timestamp on `Tenant`, set/extended by `payu_success()` on every successful checkout (currently `payu_success()` only sets `billing_plan` and `payu_subscription_id` — no date field exists yet, needs a migration); (2) a scheduled job (options: a periodic Azure Container Apps job, a Celery-beat-style scheduled task, or a lazy check inside `get_tenant_context()` itself comparing `now() > paid_through` on every request — the lazy-check option needs no new infra but adds a date comparison to the hot auth path on every request) that flips `billing_plan = "unpaid"` once `paid_through` lapses.
  - **Scope note**: this is enforcement-side only. The checkout/payment-collection side (`routers/billing.py`) already works correctly per the 2026-07-31 build and the earlier manual PayU sandbox connection test — this gap is specifically about the missing "other half" of the billing lifecycle.
  - **Depends on**: Feature 11 (`routers/billing.py`) being built first — done, 2026-07-31.

- `[x]` **Gap 61: Split extraction agent into its own microservice** — flagged 2026-07-29, deliberately deferred. **Cancelled 2026-08-10 — not needed.** Checked where `agents/extraction_agent.py` actually runs today: the main ingestion volume already goes through `queue-worker` (`ca-queue-worker-dev`) — `routers/invoices.py`'s upload endpoint only enqueues (`queue_client.send_message`) and returns immediately, the real extraction happens async in `queue_worker/main_worker.py`'s polling loop (`handlers.py`). That's the isolation this gap was actually after (extraction load not blocking the API), and it already exists via the existing container split — a further, separate microservice would be splitting something that's already split.
  - **Known, accepted exception, not closed by this**: `routers/trainer.py` (rule-testing against a sample invoice) and `routers/audit.py` (an in-request re-extraction path) both import and call `run_extraction_agent` **synchronously**, inside `invoice-be` itself — these block the HTTP request until done, and a slow/heavy extraction there could still affect API responsiveness for other requests on the same container. Accepted as out of scope for this gap (low-volume, interactive paths, not the main ingestion pipeline) rather than silently ignored — worth its own gap if trainer/audit load ever becomes a real problem.

- `[x]` **Gap 70: No rule-attribution path from a failing extraction back to the global/vendor rule that caused it** — **Closed 2026-08-12**. When structured extraction fails to parse a model response, the active `ExtractionTemplate` constraints are appended to the `extraction_failed` alert details so auditors can see which rules were active when extraction failed.

- `[x]` **Gap 68: Doc Intelligence tax-anchor backfill for null `tax_amount` — and the reusable pattern behind it** — found + fixed 2026-07-29 during live E2E validation on a real India invoice (`india_inbound_2`, vendor Ganges Textile Mills) that landed on `AUDIT_REQUIRED` with `tax_amount: null` despite the LLM correctly splitting CGST (36,199.50) + SGST (36,199.49) into `taxes[]` — it just never summed them into the scalar `tax_amount` field, tripping `tax_mismatch`. Root cause: Azure Document Intelligence's `prebuilt-invoice` model already isolates each printed tax line into a structured `TaxDetails[]` array (verified directly against this document — both CGST/SGST rows present, each with its own confidence score), completely independent of the LLM's read of the page. That structured data was being fetched and then thrown away — `_run_ocr()` only ever returned the flat OCR text, never DI's own field-level output.
  - **Fix (`handlers.py::_run_ocr`)**: sum `doc.fields["TaxDetails"]` and return it as `tax_details_sum` alongside the existing `content`/`coordinates`/`field_confidence` dict (the `ocr_result` dict already flows into the extraction graph via `run_extraction_agent(..., ocr_result=...)` — no new plumbing needed, Gap 3's confidence-score wiring already established the pattern).
  - **Fix (`extraction_agent.py::extract_node`)**: after the LLM's structured extraction, if `tax_amount is None`, backfill it from `tax_details_sum`. **Deliberately never overrides a value the LLM did populate** — this is a fill-the-gap fallback, not a source-of-truth override — and every existing faithfulness/arithmetic check (Gap 31/33/36/40/46) still runs unchanged against the final value regardless of which path filled it in. This distinction matters: those checks exist specifically to catch a model *silently correcting* a printed figure instead of flagging it, and blindly trusting Doc Intelligence's fields instead would just move that same risk to a second model rather than removing it — confirmed live on this exact document that DI's own `Items[].Amount` field picked the identical (arguably wrong) tax-inclusive column the LLM did, so DI is a second opinion worth backfilling from, not an infallible ground truth to bypass verification with.
  - **This is a template, not a one-off**: the underlying failure class — an LLM (or DI) populating a derived/summary field incorrectly or leaving it null on an unusual layout — will recur on other fields and other layouts (e.g. `discount_amount` when only per-line discounts are printed, `subtotal` on a layout DI's model mis-segments). The reusable shape is: **(1)** identify a DI-native structured field that can deterministically ground the gap, **(2)** backfill only on null/missing, never override a populated value, **(3)** leave all existing verification checks running unchanged against the final value. Apply this shape first before reaching for a prompt-only fix next time this pattern shows up.
  - **Verified**: full pytest suite still 119/119 passing (no regression to Gap 31/33/36/40/43/44/46's existing checks); a known, separate, un-fixed issue remains on this same invoice — `Items[].amount` was extracted as the tax-inclusive column instead of `quantity × unit_price`, causing `line_items_mismatch` — intentionally left alone in this fix since it needs its own column-disambiguation logic, not a blind DI-copy (see note above re: DI sharing the same column-choice issue).

- `[x]` **Gap 69: `verify_tax_amount_in_source_text` (Gap 46) guaranteed-flagged every real India CGST+SGST invoice, regardless of vendor** — found + fixed 2026-07-29 immediately after Gap 68, live-testing the same vendor (Ganges Textile Mills). Distinct from Gap 68 (which fixes *whether* `tax_amount` gets the right value): even after Gap 68 correctly backfilled `tax_amount` as the CGST+SGST sum, the invoice still landed on `AUDIT_REQUIRED` — because the *combined* figure never appears as one printed number on a source document that only ever prints CGST and SGST as separate lines, so the original check (which only accepted a single verbatim match) would fail on every genuine India GST split, by construction, forever.
  - **Fix (`verification_tools.py::verify_tax_amount_in_source_text`)**: added an optional `tax_components` param (the extracted `taxes[]` list). When the combined figure isn't found verbatim, fall back to checking that **each individual component amount** is independently verbatim in the OCR text **and** sums to `tax_amount` within tolerance. This is not a weaker check — a genuinely fabricated tax figure still gets caught (either a component won't verify individually, or the components won't sum correctly) — it just now recognizes a faithfully-transcribed multi-line split as faithful instead of guaranteed-flagging it.
  - **Fix (`extraction_agent.py::verify_node`)**: passes `data.get("taxes")` through to the call.
  - **New unit test**: `test_gap_69_tax_amount_component_sum_faithful` in `tests/test_extraction.py` — covers the faithful-split case (passes), a fabricated component that happens to sum correctly (still flagged), and real components that don't actually sum to `tax_amount` (still flagged).
  - **Verified live end-to-end**: a 4th real Ganges Textile Mills invoice (`india_inbound_4`, real Azure OpenAI + Doc Intelligence) landed **`COMPLETED` with zero alerts** (`tax_amount: 40855.38` = CGST `20427.69` + SGST `20427.69` exactly) — the first India CGST+SGST invoice in this entire test series to clear the pipeline touchless, without needing manual audit dismissal. Full pytest suite 119/119 passing throughout, no regression to any existing faithfulness/arithmetic check.
  - **What**: Move `agents/extraction_agent.py` + its dependencies (`utils/verification_tools.py`, `services/invoice_classifier.py`, prompt templates) out of `invoice-be` into a new standalone FastAPI service exposing one endpoint, `POST /extract`.
  - **Why**: Currently duplicated as two independent in-process imports — `queue-worker` (real async ingestion: upload/webhook/connectors, all funnel through the same queue) and `invoice-be`'s trainer router (synchronous preview during commit) both call the same extraction logic separately. Centralizing avoids drift between the two and lets the heaviest, most crash-prone piece of the system (long-running OCR+LLM calls) scale and deploy independently of the rest of `invoice-be`.
  - **Code changes, both integration points**:
    1. `queue_worker/handlers.py::handle_process_invoice()` — replace the direct in-process call to `run_extraction_agent()` with an `httpx` POST to the new service's `/extract` (async flow — upload, webhook, and connectors all reach this same call unchanged upstream of it).
    2. `routers/trainer.py` — replace its direct in-process call to the same extraction agent with an `httpx` POST to the new service's `/extract` (synchronous flow, no queue involved — Trainer's commit-preview needs an immediate response for the live chat UX).
    3. Trainer's own conversational chat loop (gathering the correction in natural language, *before* the final re-extraction call) is a **separate** LLM touchpoint that stays in `invoice-be` unchanged — only the final re-extraction call moves.
  - **Infra changes**:
    1. New `Dockerfile.extraction`.
    2. New bicep module (`infra/modules/compute/extraction-service.bicep`) — internal-only ingress (never user-facing, called only by `queue-worker` and `invoice-be`), `min-replicas ≥ 1` (Trainer's synchronous calls can't tolerate a cold start).
    3. New env var `EXTRACTION_SERVICE_URL`, added to both `queue-worker` and `invoice-be`.
    4. **Azure Doc Intelligence** Key Vault secrets move entirely to the new service — nothing else in `invoice-be`/`queue-worker` calls OCR. **Azure OpenAI** does *not* move away — it stays in `invoice-be` (needed for chat/SAGE and Trainer's own conversational loop) *and* gets added to the new service (needed for extraction's own LLM calls) — shared/duplicated across both, not relocated.
  - **Scope note**: no database access needed in the new service — confirmed the DB write already happens in `queue_worker/handlers.py` *after* getting the result back, not inside the agent itself, so this is a clean stateless input→output boundary, low-risk to extract.
  - **Docs to update when this lands**: `feature_2_pipeline_extraction.md`, trainer's BE/FE docs (note the new HTTP call), `Cloud_Architecture_Document.md` (new Container App entry).
- `[x]` **Gap 62: Let inbound Audit teach a standing rule directly, like outbound already does — closed 2026-07-29, superseded by `feature_7_audit.md` Task 7.5.** Closing this Gap entry to avoid double-tracking — actual build status now lives on Task 7.5. **Built 2026-07-29** — see Task 7.5 in `feature_7_audit.md` for what shipped (5 new tests, full suite green).
  - **What**: Add an "apply this correction as a standing rule?" checkbox to `routers/audit.py`'s resolve/correction flow, writing to the existing `ExtractionTemplate` + `ExtractionTemplateVersion` tables (the same real, working mechanism Trainer already uses — confirmed both tables exist and are in active use, see the note below) — mirroring what the (unmerged) outbound Vendor Flow branch already does for outbound corrections.
  - **Why**: today, fixing a recurring vendor mistake means correcting the invoice in Audit, then separately going to Trainer, picking the right vendor scope, chatting through the correction, previewing, and committing. Collapsing this into one checkbox in Audit would be meaningfully faster for the common case.
  - **The risk outbound didn't have**: outbound could commit a rule directly with no preview because "there's no vendor variability to de-risk against — it's always the same one document format for a given tenant." Inbound is the opposite — every vendor can have a different layout, so a rule taught from one invoice correction, applied directly, risks being based on a one-off anomaly, and if scoped wrong (Global vs. that one vendor) could silently start mis-extracting other vendors' invoices.
  - **Proposed safety gate**: before committing, auto-re-run extraction on that same invoice with the candidate rule applied and only commit if it still looks correct — a lightweight single-invoice check, not Trainer's full multi-turn chat sandbox, but enough to catch an obviously bad rule before it goes live for every future invoice from that vendor.
  - **Note for whoever picks this up**: a review of the unmerged outbound branch incorrectly claimed `ExtractionTemplateVersion` "does not exist anywhere in the codebase" and cited Gap 29 as still open to support that — both wrong. Gap 29 closed Jul 23, 2026, and `ExtractionTemplateVersion` is real, defined in `models.py`, and actively read/written by `routers/trainer.py` today. Don't repeat that mistake — the reuse this gap asks for is already fully backed by working code.
  - **FE piece**: `feature_4_auditor.md` Task 4.10 (was `fe_features_tracker.md` Gap 67, closed alongside this one for the same reason — see Task 7.5 above for the actual build task).
