Defects in auth & permission pipeline resolved: added central `RoleMapper` Engine in `models.py` mapping `org:admin`→`Admin`, `org:trainer`→`Trainer` (`can_train=True`), `org:auditor`→`Auditor`, `org:member`→`Viewer`; wired `RoleMapper` into `dependencies.py` and `routers/auth.py`.

- `[x]` **Gap 100: orphaned files found during 2026-08-01 doc audit, not referenced anywhere** — Removed dead code `apps/invoice-be/utils/mock_service.py` and stray empty directory `apps/invoice-be/migrations/` to prevent confusion. Verified they are not referenced or used.

- `[x]` **Gap 117: no self-service way to grant a tenant a paid plan for QA on a deployed (non-mock-auth) environment — closed 2026-08-04.** `dependencies.MOCK_BILLING_PLAN = "active"` only applies when `ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH` is on, i.e. local dev with no real Clerk. On a deployed dev environment with real Clerk auth, `billing_plan` came from exactly one place — the verified PayU callback — so testing anything gated on `'pro'`/`'pro_combined'` meant a full PayU sandbox checkout by hand, per tenant, every time a cycle lapsed. Gap 104's `/admin` console edits a user's *role*, never `billing_plan`.
  - **Shipped**: `apps/invoice-be/scripts/grant_test_plan.py`, following `sweep_lapsed_billing.py`'s standalone-entrypoint pattern (same `sys.path` bootstrap, `argparse` + `--dry-run`, `Session(engine)`, logging not printing). `--tenant` takes an id **or** a domain (a UUID-parsing value is only ever looked up as an id); `--plan` is constrained to `sorted(PAID_PLANS)` imported from `billing_lifecycle` so it can't drift from what `is_lapsed()` recognises; `--cycles` (default 12) calls `extend_paid_through()` once per cycle rather than inventing a separate far-future date, so a granted plan is identical in shape to a paid one. `payu_subscription_id` is deliberately left unset — forging a txnid would make a hand-granted plan indistinguishable from a real payment in support queries.
  - **The safety-critical part**: `assert_non_production()` enforces two independent guards, split out of `main()` specifically so it is testable. (1) `settings.ENVIRONMENT` must be in `NON_PRODUCTION_ENVIRONMENTS` (`dev`/`development`/`local`/`test`/`testing`/`qa`/`staging`). No such setting existed, so `config.py::ENVIRONMENT` is new and **defaults to `"production"`** — the same fail-closed choice as `ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH`, so an environment that never declared itself refuses and a typo (`prod-eu`) refuses rather than being assumed safe. (2) `settings.PAYU_MODE` must not be `"live"` — defence in depth against the accident guard 1 can't catch alone (exporting `ENVIRONMENT=dev` in a shell pointed at the production DB). Neither is a security boundary — whoever runs this already holds the DB credentials — which is exactly why this stays a script and never becomes an endpoint. `ENVIRONMENT` is read **only** by this script; no request-handling code branches on it.
  - **Follow-up for infra-devops (not done here)**: nothing in `infra/` sets `ENVIRONMENT` on any Container App, so on deployed dev the operator supplies it at invocation (`ENVIRONMENT=dev uv run python scripts/grant_test_plan.py --tenant … --plan pro`). Adding it to the dev container app's env block is a one-line infra change.
  - **Tests**: 10 of the 31 in `tests/test_billing_free_quota.py` (guard refuses production / an unrecognised name / live PayU-with-dev-ENVIRONMENT, accepts the allowlist case-insensitively; `resolve_tenant()` by id, by domain, and None for unknown values of both shapes; the grant landing `DEFAULT_CYCLES` whole cycles out). **Not verified**: `main()` has never been run against the deployed dev database — the guards and helpers are unit-tested, the end-to-end invocation is not. Docs: `feature_11_billing.md` Task 11.9 + "Granting a paid plan for QA on a deployed environment".

- `[x]` **Gap 118: Free tier's 50-invoice quota was a one-time lifetime cap, not a monthly allowance — closed 2026-08-04.** `routers/invoices.py` decrements `tenant.free_invoices_remaining` on every free-tier upload at two call sites (`upload_invoices()`, `start_directory_watcher()`) and nothing anywhere ever reset it. `models.py` seeds it once via `default_factory` from `DEFAULT_FREE_INVOICES_LIMIT` (50), so the tier advertised as "50 invoices a month" was in practice 50 invoices for the **lifetime of the account**, after which the tenant was permanently 402'd "Limit reached" with paying as the only exit.
  - **Built as the mirror image of Gap 71's lapse**, in the same module, rather than as a second mechanism — same problem shape (a state change inferrable only from a date, with nothing evaluating that date), so the date arithmetic stays in one place.
  - **Schema**: `Tenant.free_quota_reset_at`, migration **`c6d7e8f9a0b1_add_tenant_free_quota_reset_at.py`** on head `b5c6d7e8f9a0`. Nullable, no `server_default`, **no backfill** — the same shape `paid_through`'s migration chose, for the same reason: NULL means "the cycle clock hasn't started for this row" (every pre-migration tenant). Backfilling to `now()` would have made the deploy itself a mass grant of 50 invoices to every free tenant.
  - **Logic** (`services/billing_lifecycle.py`): `FREE_PLAN`, `free_quota_deadline()`, `is_free_quota_due()`, `advance_free_quota_reset_at()`, `refresh_free_quota()`. `refresh_free_quota()` has three outcomes and only one is a refill: non-free plan → nothing; free plan with no clock → seed the date one cycle out and commit but **leave the counter alone** (returns False); past the deadline → counter back to `DEFAULT_FREE_INVOICES_LIMIT`, deadline advanced, commit (returns True). Only `'free'` refills — excluding `'unpaid'` is load-bearing, since that's what Gap 71 demotes a lapsed payer to and refilling it would hand them a free tier as a reward. `advance_free_quota_reset_at()` advances by *whole cycles from the existing date*, so a tenant idle three months returns to their original anniversary in one pass instead of the refill date drifting to whenever they came back.
  - **Cadence knob**: `config.py::FREE_QUOTA_CYCLE_DAYS` (30), deliberately **not** a reuse of `BILLING_CYCLE_DAYS` despite the same default. That value means "how much time one PayU payment buys"; if a future annual plan pushed it to 365, reusing it would silently turn the free tier into 50 invoices per *year*.
  - **Wiring**: lazily in `dependencies.get_tenant_context_allow_unpaid()`, immediately **after** `enforce_lapse()` — same request path and same rationale as Gap 71 (no scheduler exists; one datetime comparison on a row the request already loaded; the write happens only on the request that crosses the boundary). At the dependency rather than the two upload call sites, so the balance is correct whichever door the tenant comes in through. Ordering after the lapse check is deliberate and test-pinned: a tenant demoted to `'unpaid'` on that very request must not then be handed a fresh free allowance. `routers/invoices.py` itself is **unchanged**.
  - **Tests**: 21 of the 31 in `tests/test_billing_free_quota.py`, modelled directly on `test_billing_lapse.py`. Covers both non-refill cases (NULL date; non-free plans parameterised over `pro`/`pro_combined`/`unpaid`/`active`), the deadline boundary both ways, advancing from unset / one day stale / three whole missed cycles, seed-without-refill, no stacking within a cycle, and four request-path cases including an exhausted tenant actually uploading again afterwards (ends at 49) and an in-cycle exhausted tenant still getting 402.
  - **Full backend suite**: 274 passed, 2 failed, 5 deselected. Both failures environmental, neither a regression — the long-standing `test_salesforce_pkce_flow` (needs local Redis on :6379) and `test_handle_import_connector_file_refreshes_expired_token`, which fails only because that run happened inside a git worktree whose deeper path made the connector's local-fallback file path exceed Windows' 260-char `MAX_PATH`. Migration checked structurally: `alembic heads` → single head `c6d7e8f9a0b1`; `--sql` render clean. **Not applied to any real Postgres.**
  - **Later closed as Gap 121 (2026-08-11):** idle free-tier refill is no longer out of scope — `sweep_free_quotas()` + ACA Job `caj-billing-lifecycle-{env}` (with Gap 119). Lazy path above still covers first-sighting / NULL clocks.
  - Docs: `feature_11_billing.md` Task 11.8 + "Free-tier quota refill".

- `[x]` **Gap 119: Gap 71's paid-plan monthly lapse check has no scheduled trigger in infra — only fires lazily per active request** — **closed 2026-08-11** together with Gap 121. Lazy path unchanged. Scheduled trigger: Azure Container Apps Job `caj-billing-lifecycle-{env}` (`infra/modules/compute/billing-lifecycle-job.bicep`, wired from `08-apps.bicep`) runs `scripts/sweep_billing_lifecycle.py` daily (06:00 UTC) on the invoice-be image with `DATABASE_URL` only — lapse then free-quota refill. Standalone `scripts/sweep_lapsed_billing.py` remains for manual lapse-only runs.

- `[x]` **Gap 122: AI Score Metrics & Rejection Reason Tracking (Backend)** — closed 2026-08-05. Implement backend logic to calculate a unified 3-parameter "AI Score" for the dashboard:
  1. **AI Field Extraction:** Rate of human corrections on the core 7 fields ($\text{Corrections} / (\text{Invoices} \times 7)$).
  2. **AI Alert Response:** Rate of valid alerts vs. false alarms. An alert is considered valid if the invoice is finalized (PAID/REJECTED) with the alert still active (not dismissed), or if the alert was confirmed via a correction.
  3. **AI Alerts Missed:** Rate of invoices rejected specifically due to "AI Extraction Error".
  Requires updating the `resolve_audit_invoice()` endpoint schema to accept and log a structured `reject_reason` in `AuditLog.details`, and exposing these three calculated values in `get_dashboard_metrics` and `get_outbound_dashboard_metrics`.

- `[x]` **Gap 123: Alert Classification and Severity-based Error Accuracy** — closed 2026-08-05. Added severity categories (`information`, `warning`, `error`) to verification tools and updated dashboard metrics algorithms to only count `error` type alerts in accuracy calculations.

- `[~]` **Gap 124: SendGrid / GoDaddy live mail settings + hardening (public URL proxy done; hardening done)** — **KEEP OPEN for dashboard/DNS + live E2E only.** Code-hardening items 5–7 closed 2026-08-12 (details below); items 1–4 are a separate user-owned checkpoint and remain untouched. App model + **website public relay** shipped 2026-08-10:
  - **Shipped (public reachability):** `invoice-website` `POST /api/v1/email/mailintegration` → internal BE (PayU topology). SendGrid Destination URL must be the **website** FQDN, e.g. `https://ca-invoice-website-dev.<caeDomain>/api/v1/email/mailintegration` — not the internal BE host. Path does **not** collide with Multi-Zone FE rewrite `/api/email/*`.
  - **Still open — SendGrid + GoDaddy (manual / ops):**
    1. **MX + domain** on GoDaddy (or DNS host) for `EMAIL_APP_DOMAIN` / `invoiceeq.app` so mail to `invoices@invoiceeq.app` reaches SendGrid.
    2. **SendGrid Inbound Parse** host/settings: Destination URL = website relay above; optional spam check / multipart.
    3. **SendGrid domain auth / Single Sender** for Gap 125 *send* deliverability (API key already in KV/bicep; live send not verified).
    4. One real E2E: registered From → mailbox → Parse → website relay → BE → invoice row.
  - **Hardening — items 5–7 DONE 2026-08-12 (code-only half of this gap):**
    5. `[x]` **SendGrid authenticity check** — `INBOUND_PARSE_SHARED_SECRET` (the env var `invoice-be.bicep` already mapped from KV `SENDGRID-INBOUND-SECRET`, which nothing in the app ever read) is now verified with `hmac.compare_digest` on every POST. Inbound Parse does no request signing — a Destination URL is all its dashboard exposes — so the secret is accepted from an `X-Inbound-Secret`/`X-Sendgrid-Inbound-Secret` header, a `?key=`/`?secret=` query param, or the password half of Basic credentials in the URL. **Fail-closed:** an empty setting rejects everything (reason `secret_unconfigured`), it does not mean "enforcement off".
    6. `[x]` **Dropped/skipped mail is recorded and visible to an Admin** — new `dropped_inbound_emails` table (`models.py::DroppedInboundEmail`, migration `a2b3c4d5e6f7`) written by all ten rejection paths, read by `GET /api/v1/admin/dropped-emails` and rendered as a panel in the FE Admin console. Visibility is tenant-scoped: rows attributed to the caller's tenant, plus *unattributed* rows (the mailbox is platform-wide, so a drop before the From address matches `tenant_email_senders` belongs to no tenant) whose sender domain is one this workspace owns — narrow on purpose, so other tenants' sender addresses are not exposed. Bounce-back still uses Gap 125 send and is **not** built.
    7. `[x]` **25 MiB cap** — `INBOUND_EMAIL_MAX_BYTES`, checked against the declared `Content-Length` before the body is touched (Starlette spools multipart to a temp file while parsing, so a post-parse check is not a cap), then re-checked against measured attachment bytes for a chunked client. The website relay enforces the same ceiling at the public edge.
    - **Two things this turned up that were not in the original item list:** (a) the handler declared `files: list[UploadFile]`, so it only matched a part named `files` — real Inbound Parse sends `attachment1..N`, meaning the webhook could not have ingested a live SendGrid POST as written; attachments are now collected by type. (b) The website relay forwarded only `Content-Type`, dropping the query string and all headers — enforcing the secret backend-side without fixing the relay would have rejected 100% of real mail.
    - Files: `services/inbound_mail_security.py` (new), `routers/email_ingestion.py`, `routers/admin.py`, `config.py`, `models.py`, `alembic/versions/a2b3c4d5e6f7_add_dropped_inbound_emails.py`, `invoice-website/app/api/v1/email/mailintegration/route.ts`, `invoice-fe/app/api/admin/dropped-emails/route.ts`, `invoice-fe/app/admin/page.tsx`. Tests: 12 new in `tests/test_email_ingestion.py` (secret reject/accept incl. all five transports, both size-cap paths, drop rows written + queryable, Admin visibility rule, non-Admin refused).
  - **GAP STAYS OPEN.** Only the code-hardening half (5–7) is done. Items 1–4 — GoDaddy MX, SendGrid Inbound Parse Destination URL, domain auth, and one live E2E — are external/user-owned dashboard+DNS work and are a **separate pending checkpoint**; none of it was attempted and no live send/receive has been verified. When that checkpoint runs, the KV secret `SENDGRID-INBOUND-SECRET` must be seeded with a real value at the same time, or the now fail-closed check will (correctly) reject the first real mail — which will at least show up in the Admin dropped-mail list rather than vanishing.
  - Blocker order: DNS + Inbound Parse (1–2) first; then E2E (4). Authenticity (5) is now in place ahead of any production traffic.
  - Staff notify = **Gap 125** (separate). Never customer email from app.

Overdue is **still never persisted as a status** — no `OVERDUE` value is written anywhere, so no existing filter/metric/FE tab changes.

Flip to `[x]` after: deploy Stage 8, `az containerapp job start -n caj-overdue-sweep-dev`, confirm one `outbound_invoice.overdue` POST lands at a subscribed endpoint and that a second run sends nothing. Note the migration branches off head `f9a0b1c2d3e4` in parallel with other in-flight workstreams — `alembic heads` will need linearizing/merging before deploy.
  - (2) ~~No live E2E delivery test to a real external URL~~ — **done 2026-08-10**: live-tested against a real `webhook.site` endpoint end-to-end (real `invoice.completed` event from an actual completed invoice, plus a manual direct call to `dispatch_webhook_event`) — both delivered successfully (confirmed via webhook.site's request inbox showing the POSTs from the container's outbound IP). No delivery defect found; a live investigation initially suspected one, but the true cause was checking webhook.site's `/edit/` response-config page instead of `/view/` inbox — not a real bug.

- `[x]` **Gap 179: Google Drive "Import request queued!" but nothing appears in Ingestion** — **fixed 2026-08-10.** Two bugs:
  1. **`trigger_file_import` lied** — returned `success: true` even when Azure queue send was skipped/failed (missing connection string or exception). FE Gap 166 only checked HTTP status, so the green banner still showed. Now returns **503** unless `send_message` actually succeeds.
  2. **`handle_import_connector_file` (inbound) never created an `Invoice` row** — only enqueued `process_invoice`. `handle_process_invoice` updates only when `Invoice.file_path` matches, so extraction had nowhere to land and the UI never showed the file. Now creates a `PROCESSING` invoice (tags `connector` + provider) before enqueue, stamps `last_enqueued_at` when queue succeeds.
  - Tests: `test_trigger_import_returns_503_when_queue_unavailable`, inbound import asserts `invoice_id` + DB row. Files: `routers/connectors.py`, `queue_worker/handlers.py`, `tests/test_connectors.py`.
  - **UX note:** Ingestion **Dispatch / Status Ledger** only tracks DropZone → Submit batches (`jobIds`/`batchId` in page state). Drive imports never populate that panel — look at **Invoices** (PROCESSING / FAILED / AUDIT_REQUIRED). Follow-on: Gap 180.

- `[x]` **Gap 180: Queue-worker missing Google/Salesforce OAuth env — Drive import uploaded stub PDF → Doc Intelligence `InvalidContent`** — **fixed 2026-08-10.** Root cause: `invoice-be` had `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`/`SECRET` (and Salesforce) for OAuth connect + file listing, but `ca-queue-worker-dev` did not. `has_real_credentials()` was false on the worker, so `handle_import_connector_file` skipped the real Drive download and wrote the local stub `%PDF-1.4 stub content…`, which OCR correctly rejected. Live env patched on worker; bicep updated (`queue-worker.bicep` + `08-apps.bicep`). Handler now **raises** if a tenant connection is active but platform credentials/download fail (stub only when no active connection). Files: `infra/modules/compute/queue-worker.bicep`, `infra/08-apps.bicep`, `queue_worker/handlers.py`, `tests/test_connectors.py`.

Fix: `infra/08-apps.bicep` + live `ca-invoice-be-dev` use **website** FQDN for `GOOGLE_REDIRECT_URI` / `SALESFORCE_REDIRECT_URI` / `FRONTEND_URL`; Connected App / Google Console Callback URLs updated to match (manual). - `[x]` **Gap 133: A signed-up Clerk Organization can silently fail to provision into a real tenant, landing the user in an unrelated auto-created fallback tenant instead — with the FE displaying the org name they actually signed up with, masking the mismatch** — **closed 2026-08-19.** Core fix + hardening pass landed 2026-08-11/12 (3b, then 3c, see the second "---" block below — 3c fixed five further holes a security review found in 3b, all reproduced live and closed). Original defect (silent fallback tenant + mismatched FE org name) is closed: live local signup on Clerk instance `great-baboon-11` + Docker Postgres created a real tenant bound to the Clerk org, not a domain-fallback workspace. Sub-items: (1) adoption TOCTOU advisory-lock **verified 2026-08-19** against real Postgres (`tests/test_auth.py::test_provision_concurrent_same_org_id_creates_one_tenant_on_postgres` — two threaded calls, same `clerk_org_id`, exactly one tenant row, `{True, False}` is_new split); early-lock-release on the IntegrityError fallback path remains a **known limitation**, not the original gap; (2) Clerk `invoice-app` JWT Template `org_id` + `email` **verified 2026-08-19** in Dashboard claims and live `[jwt-diag]` (`org_id` present, `email_present=True`); (3) independent second-pair-of-eyes re-review of 3c was never done — **non-blocking residual**, not the original defect. Found 2026-08-05 debugging why a Pro Combined grant "wasn't working": it had been granted to a tenant the user wasn't actually using. Confirmed live via direct DB queries (see Gap 131's investigation thread in chat for the full trace).

  **Orphan-tenant pre-check landed 2026-08-18 (founder-directed requirement):** `routers/auth.py::provision_tenant()` now pre-checks `existing_user` with an already-set `tenant_id` and pre-checks email conflict *before* creating `new_tenant`. If the user already belongs to another workspace, it raises `HTTPException(409 Conflict)` ("This account is already provisioned to another workspace...") without creating any orphan `Tenant` row. If the admin email is already in use by another account, it also raises `409 Conflict` before creating a tenant. Covered by automated tests `test_provision_rejects_user_already_in_another_workspace`, `test_provision_rejects_email_conflict_before_creating_tenant`, and `test_provision_allows_user_with_null_tenant_id` in `tests/test_auth.py`. Files: `routers/auth.py`, `tests/test_auth.py`.
  **Live local signup verified 2026-08-19:** website `POST /api/auth/provision` → backend `POST /auth/provision` **200**; `[jwt-diag]` `org_id='org_3I7hLriaDY50eCP1qZwQBI48Mfs'` `org_role='org:admin'` `email_present=True`; `users` row `madusudhanmishra@olipii.com` / `user_3I7hLnpIurlaxOJh4286cR6T8Kw` bound to tenant **test 413 co.** (`bcbeb718-751d-4ecd-a61e-50fe4d7cfce4`) with matching `clerk_org_id` — not a `domain.com` fallback tenant. FE dashboard loaded at `/dashboard` for that session. **Not this gap:** subsequent `/auth/me` 401s from PyJWT `The token is not yet valid (iat)` (no leeway; separate clock-skew issue). **Not verified:** Azure `ca-invoice-be-dev` deploy of the same flow.
  1. **The user's real Clerk Organization was never provisioned.** Their profile displays org name "test1" (Clerk org `org_3HH1VFXsiEo4hAdScaHFLGi2AxX`, written to `unsafeMetadata.orgName` at sign-up per `invoice-website/app/signup/page.tsx`) — but no `Tenant` row anywhere in the database has that `clerk_org_id`. Either `POST /auth/provision` was never called for this org at sign-up, or it failed silently with nothing surfaced to the user.
  2. **The JWT for this user also carries no real `email` claim.** `dependencies.py`'s fallback (`payload.get("email") or payload.get("email_address") or f"{user_id}@domain.com"`) kicked in, producing a synthetic placeholder email literally shaped `user_<clerkid>@domain.com` — confirmed stored verbatim in the `users` table.
  3. **`get_tenant_context()`'s auto-provisioning fallback chain (Priority 1: `clerk_org_id` → Priority 2: `tenant_id` claim → Priority 3: email domain) silently absorbed the user into an unrelated, pre-existing tenant** rather than surfacing the Priority-1 lookup failure. Because the synthetic email's domain is always literally `"domain.com"` (an artifact of item 2's fallback, not a real domain), Priority 3 matched (or on a first occurrence, created) a generic tenant literally named "Domain Workspace" — a tenant with no relationship to the user's real organization at all. Confirmed live: 2 of the 3 `User` rows in the dev database share this exact synthetic-email pattern and both landed in the same generic `domain.com` tenant, despite presumably representing different real sign-ups.
  4. **The FE never shows this mismatch.** `Header.tsx`'s displayed org name (`orgName`, from Clerk's `unsafeMetadata`) and the tenant the backend actually resolved and stores data under are two entirely independent data sources — nothing cross-checks or displays that they've diverged. A user has no way to discover from the UI that "test1" isn't actually where their data lives.
  - **Fix, several independent pieces**:
    - Root-cause why `/auth/provision` isn't completing for some sign-ups (or isn't being called at all) — needs investigation into the website sign-up flow's call to that endpoint and whether it's failing silently.
    - The JWT Template issue producing a missing/malformed `email` claim needs the same Clerk Dashboard investigation as Gap 108/109's role-claim issues — likely related, possibly the same root misconfiguration.
    - `get_tenant_context()`'s Priority-3 email-domain fallback should not be able to merge unrelated users into a shared generic tenant this easily when the "email" it's matching on is itself a synthetic placeholder, not real user data — worth gating the domain-fallback match on the email actually being real (not the synthetic pattern) before trusting it as an identity signal.
    - FE should surface a visible mismatch warning (or simply display the *actual* tenant name resolved by the backend, from `/auth/me`, instead of Clerk's disconnected `unsafeMetadata.orgName`) so this class of problem is now obviously visible in the UI instead of silently invisible.
  - ---
  - **FIXED 2026-08-11** (defect-remediation Checkpoint 3b; scope refined by four user decisions taken at Checkpoint 3a — strictest gating with no auto-provision fallback, blocking sign-up UX with Retry, no adoption of a populated domain tenant, and auth hardening bundled into the same change).
  - **Two further defects found in the same code while investigating, neither in the original write-up above, both reproduced over real HTTP before fixing:**
    1. **`POST /auth/provision` had no authentication whatsoever.** An anonymous caller could POST any `clerk_org_id`/`org_name`/`admin_email` and, via the domain-adoption branch, *rename an existing populated tenant and point their own Clerk org at it*. Reproduced end-to-end: an anonymous POST for `anon@victim2.com` returned **HTTP 200** and renamed the seeded `Victim2 Real Workspace` tenant to `Anonymous Takeover` with the attacker's org id attached — with `ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH=false`, i.e. exactly the deployed configuration.
    2. **The second organisation to sign up from any shared email domain got a bare 500.** `Tenant.domain` is `unique=True`; the handler inserted a tenant with the raw email domain and never handled `IntegrityError`. Reproduced: 1st sign-up on `shared.com` → 200, 2nd → **HTTP 500**, leaving a Clerk user with no tenant anywhere — one of the concrete ways an org "was never provisioned" in the original report.
  - **Code changed**
    - **`dependencies.py`** — Priority 3 (email-domain lookup) and the request-time `Tenant(...)` creation that followed it are **removed for real tokens**. Priority 1 (`clerk_org_id`) and Priority 2 (`tenant_id` claim) both missing is now **`409`** with a detail naming the org id, in both places that could invent a tenant: the new-user branch and the existing-user-with-no-`tenant_id` branch (the latter used to fall through to `MOCK_TENANT_ID`). Mock/test identities (`ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH`, default off) keep the old behaviour via a local `is_mock_identity` flag — that is what the existing pytest suite runs on. `email_is_placeholder` is computed at the claim source (`payload.get("email") or payload.get("email_address")`) and, with `email_present=`, added to the `[jwt-diag]` stdout line; diagnostic only, not a gating input. The JWT decode/verify body was extracted verbatim into **`verify_clerk_jwt()`** so the provision endpoint can authenticate with identical checks, and **`get_authenticated_clerk_user_id()`** was added. `TenantContext` gained **`tenant_name`**, populated from the already-loaded Tenant row (no extra query); `routers/email_ingestion.py`'s separate `TenantContext(...)` construction site updated to match.
    - **`routers/auth.py`** — `provision_tenant()` now depends on `get_authenticated_clerk_user_id()` and **403**s unless the verified `sub` equals `body.clerk_user_id`. Domain adoption additionally requires the domain tenant to have **no users at all**, not merely no `clerk_org_id`; a domain tenant with real users is never renamed or claimed, a fresh isolated tenant is created instead. New `_create_tenant_with_unique_domain()` catches `IntegrityError`, rolls back, retries once with `org-<clerk_org_id>.invalid` (RFC 2606 reserved TLD, deliberately non-resolving), and on a second failure re-reads by `clerk_org_id` — returning that row if a concurrent request won the race, otherwise an explicit **409** with the constraint error. No path returns a bare 500 any more.
    - **`invoice-website/app/signup/page.tsx`** — provisioning failure is **blocking**; the old `console.warn` + `router.push("/login")` is gone. Clerk-org creation failure (and an org created with no id) stop the flow with a support-facing message. User id is defensive: `result.createdUserId || window.Clerk?.user?.id`. New `provisionTenant()` mints `getToken({ template: "invoice-app" })` client-side immediately after `setActive` (freshest session state — the server-side cookie can lag `setActive`, per Gap 157) and throws on any non-2xx; `handleRetryProvision()` + a **Retry** button re-POST only the backend call for the org that already exists client-side. Copy: *"Your account was created, but we couldn't finish setting up your organisation ({detail}). Click Retry — if it keeps failing, contact support and quote org id {orgId}."*
    - **`invoice-website/app/api/auth/provision/route.ts`** — forwards the caller's `Authorization` header, falling back to a server-side `auth()` + `getToken({ template: "invoice-app" })`; refuses with 401 if neither yields a token; `console.error`s backend status/body server-side. The "Not authenticated … deliberately out of scope" and "best-effort" comments are gone.
    - **FE display** — `hooks/useAuth.ts` gained `tenantName` on `AuthMeResponse`/`AuthContextType`/`normalise()`/`ANONYMOUS`; `components/layout/Header.tsx` and `app/admin/page.tsx` render `useAuth().tenantName`, with `unsafeMetadata.orgName` only as the placeholder while `authLoading`. Four e2e `**/api/auth/me` stubs gained `tenant_name`.
    - **Tests** — `tests/test_auth.py` had **zero** `/auth/provision` coverage; it now has 12 new tests (26 in the file): happy path, idempotency on `clerk_org_id`, second sign-up on a claimed domain gets a distinct tenant with the `org-….invalid` domain, populated domain tenant is not adopted, empty org-less domain tenant still is, unauthenticated provision is 401, mismatched-`sub` token is 403, matching `sub` succeeds, unmatched org + placeholder email is 409 with nothing created, two unprovisioned users are not merged, and `/auth/me` returns `tenant_name`.
  - **Verified 2026-08-11**
    - `pytest tests/test_auth.py` → **26 passed**. Full suite `pytest tests/` → **316 passed, 1 failed**; the failure is `test_connectors.py::test_salesforce_pkce_flow`, a local-Redis connection error, confirmed to fail identically on a stashed (pre-change) tree.
    - `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on **both** `invoice-fe` and `invoice-website`.
    - **Live before/after repro over real HTTP** (uvicorn running the actual app on a throwaway SQLite DB, driven by `POST /auth/provision`; the dev backend itself has `ingress.external: false` and reproducing the takeover against it would have mutated real tenant rows). **Before (HEAD):** 2nd sign-up on a shared domain → `500`; attacker sign-up on `victim.com` → `200`, victim tenant renamed `Victim Real Workspace` → `Attacker Org` with the attacker's `clerk_org_id`; anonymous POST with `ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH=false` → `200` and victim2 likewise renamed. **After:** both sign-ups → `200` with **distinct** tenant ids; attacker sign-up → `200` into its own new tenant with the victim row byte-identical; anonymous POST → **`401`**. Both originally-reported bugs and both newly-found ones confirmed closed.
    - **Not verified**: nothing was deployed, so no live check against `ca-invoice-be-dev`/the real Clerk sign-up flow; the Retry button and the new error copy have no automated (Playwright) coverage — `invoice-website` still has no e2e suite.
  - **Who this locks out (dev environment, audited 2026-08-11 against the live `psql-invoicellm-dev` database + the Clerk Backend API; no data changed)**: the current dev DB has **7 tenants, 11 user rows, every tenant carrying a `clerk_org_id`, every user row carrying a `tenant_id`, and zero synthetic `…@domain.com` emails** — the "Domain Workspace" tenant and the two synthetic-email users from the original 2026-08-05 finding no longer exist (this is the rebuilt `rg-invoice-llm-dev` environment on a new Clerk instance). **No existing `users` row loses access**: the gate only fires for a caller with no `users` row (or one with a NULL `tenant_id`, of which there are none). Clerk, however, holds **16 users and 14 organisations — 7 of those orgs have no tenant row at all**, so **5 Clerk accounts will now get a `409` on first sign-in instead of being silently absorbed**: `0dja3qz5sv@lnovic.com` (`user_3HilIGH82e7hDlyGHfdThwdxcMz`, org `org_3HilIOqvkIP6WeRPqLCjuaEfi2o`), `sbanerji@admsofttech.com` (`user_3HagLPrpsUoo0gI49IrJm6QaPwX`, org `org_3HagLmzygUhb7q7knYMw5CwUW5x`), `sanjibzxc@asd.com` (`user_3HagLEhMdrUjIO2YGHZO96vIHoR`, org `org_3HagLg2v82VGCkeDuhl7oFjMb5C` "test_2ndorg"), `asd@asd.com` (`user_3HagLDALjsViGpAigSv3GaSwYo0`, org `org_3HagLejlZZU5tdkVgwN3bQJFUmN` "abdc"), `22ek57yvnm@olipii.com` (`user_3HXJDfxLS38yOxjwOzpnKeCbkFv`, org `org_3HXJDpgMWIXux9FOHl5Usw3W0Om`). Two of those five are the strongest possible argument for the change: `sanjibzxc@asd.com` and `asd@asd.com` carry the domain `asd.com`, which **matches the existing `New_org_clerk` tenant** (`e8f7b354-…`, plan `pro_combined`, 2 users, 6 invoices) — under the old Priority-3 fallback their first sign-in would have dropped them straight into somebody else's paid workspace. Three org-less Clerk users (`devashish.india.007`, `devashish.ind`) also own unprovisioned orgs but already have `users` rows, so they are unaffected. **Deliberately not fixed here**: no backfill, no migration, no re-provisioning of these accounts — that needs its own approved data plan. The clean remedy for each is to sign up again (the flow now blocks and offers Retry) or to have `POST /auth/provision` called once for the org.
  - **JWT Template (sub-item 2) closed 2026-08-19**: Clerk `invoice-app` template on instance `great-baboon-11` emits `org_id` and `email`; live `[jwt-diag]` showed `email_present=True` and a real `org_id` on the provision + dashboard calls. The body's `admin_email` is still ignored for real tokens (3c); the stored address `madusudhanmishra@olipii.com` therefore came from the token claim, not a placeholder.
  - **Cross-references**: `invoice-website` Gap 176 (sign-up UX half), `invoice-fe` Gap 217 (tenant-name display half). Spec docs updated: `feature_1_auth.md` (BE), `feature_4_auth_gateway.md` (website), `feature_1_layout_theme.md` (FE).
  - ---
  - **HARDENED 2026-08-12 (Checkpoint 3c)** — a security review of the 3b implementation found five residual holes and confirmed all of them live. 3b's own fixes are unchanged and still hold; the problem was that 3b authenticated **who** was calling `POST /auth/provision` but never checked **what org or email they were entitled to claim**, and that the role a request runs as was still read straight off the token. Every finding below was reproduced over real HTTP before fixing and re-checked after — see "Verified" at the end of this section.
    1. **HIGH — `body.clerk_org_id` was never checked against the token's own `org_id` claim.** Any authenticated user could POST an arbitrary org id (e.g. an org that existed in Clerk but had not been provisioned yet) and claim it as their own tenant. Reproduced: a token for `org_attacker_own` provisioning `org_someone_elses_unprovisioned` → **200**, tenant created and claimed.
    2. **HIGH — the same hole made the idempotent early return a cross-tenant read.** POSTing an org id that *did* already exist echoed back that tenant's UUID, display name, `billing_plan` and `free_invoices_remaining`. Reproduced: an unrelated caller received `Victim Holdings` / `pro_combined` / quota `13` / the tenant's real UUID in a **200**.
    3. **HIGH (pre-existing, not introduced by 3b) — `TenantContext.role` was never reconciled with `org_matches`.** Gap 173 computed that check but used it only to gate *persisting* `user.role`, not to gate the role *used for the request*. So the exact attack Gap 173 was written to stop still worked: a user stored as **Viewer** creates a throwaway Clerk Organization (Clerk makes its creator `org:admin`), switches active org to it, and their token's `org_role=org:admin` produced an Admin `TenantContext` on their **real** tenant. Reproduced: `/auth/me` returned `role: Admin`, `can_train/can_audit/can_load: true`, `tenant_id` = their real tenant — while the stored role correctly stayed `Viewer`, which is exactly why nothing looked wrong.
    4. **MEDIUM — `body.admin_email` was fully caller-controlled.** `User.email` is globally unique, so an attacker could provision using a stranger's real address: it squats the address *and* makes the real owner's later provision attempt hit an unhandled `IntegrityError` → bare **500**, contradicting the handler docstring's own "never a bare 500" claim from 3b. Reproduced: body `admin_email: ceo@bigcorp.com` from a token belonging to `squatter@evil.test` → **200**, `users` row stored with the victim's address.
    5. **MEDIUM — the adoption check treated "no users" as "unclaimed".** A user-less tenant can still hold billing/plan/OAuth/invoice data (a legacy pre-Clerk-org tenant). Reproduced: a `pro_combined` tenant with a PayU customer id and an invoice, zero users, was renamed `Adopter Inc`, given the attacker's `clerk_org_id`, and its `billing_plan: pro_combined` returned to them in the response — a full workspace takeover, the same class 3b was supposed to have closed.
    6. **LOW — the collision `409` leaked raw DB constraint text.** `e.orig` (the raw SQLAlchemy/driver exception) was interpolated into the response body; on Postgres that discloses the table, the constraint name and the colliding domain value.
  - **Code changed (3c)**
    - **`dependencies.py`** — `get_authenticated_clerk_user_id()` (returned a bare `sub`) is replaced by **`get_authenticated_clerk_identity()`** returning the new **`AuthenticatedClerkIdentity`** model (`clerk_user_id`, `org_id`, `email`, `is_mock`) — a name promising only a user id is a fair part of why the org/email binding was missed in 3b. `email` uses the same `email or email_address` precedence as the real-token branch below it, one pattern rather than two. New **`reconcile_role_with_org()`** decides the role a request runs as: mock identities are exempt; a token role claim is used only when the resolved tenant's `clerk_org_id` equals the token's `org_id`; otherwise the persisted `User.role` governs, falling back to `Viewer` when absent. It is applied in two places — immediately before `resolve_permissions()` (so `can_train`/`can_audit`/`can_load` follow the reconciled role, not the token's) and on the first-login `User(...)` write path (so a distrusted role is never persisted and then read back as "our data"). Deliberate side effect: a request with no org claim at all (the Gap 157 stale-cookie window) now keeps the user's persisted role instead of clamping to `Viewer`. The `tenant_id`-claim branch gained a comment-only note (review finding 8, inert today): if that claim is ever added to the Clerk JWT Template it must come from `public_metadata` or an org shortcode, never `unsafe_metadata` — the `role` claim precedent proves that is client-writable.
    - **`routers/auth.py`** — `provision_tenant()` now **403**s unless `caller.org_id == body.clerk_org_id` (in addition to 3b's `sub` check), which closes findings 1 and 2 together: there is no longer a request that reaches the idempotent early return for somebody else's org. `admin_email` is taken from the caller's verified `email` claim and `body.admin_email` is ignored for real tokens (the field stays on the schema — the website still sends it, and the `ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH` path, default off, still uses it). A missing `email` claim falls back to the same `user_<id>@domain.com` placeholder `dependencies.py` uses, and a placeholder address now **skips the domain-adoption lookup entirely** rather than matching every such caller on the literal domain `domain.com`. New **`_tenant_adoption_blockers()`** + **`_TENANT_SCOPED_TABLES`** replace the "no users" test: adoption additionally requires a default `billing_plan`, no `payu_customer_id`/`payu_subscription_id`/`paid_through`, and zero rows in `Invoice`, `TenantConnection`, `AuditLog`, `ExtractionTemplate`, `ExtractionTemplateVersion`, `ChatSession`, `ChatFeedback`, `TenantEmailSender`, `WebhookSubscription` (`ChatMessage` is covered transitively via `ChatSession`). The admin `User` INSERT is wrapped in the same `IntegrityError` pattern 3b added for the tenant INSERT (rollback → re-read for a concurrent winner → explicit **409**, never a bare 500). The `409` in `_create_tenant_with_unique_domain()` no longer contains `e.orig`; the raw exception goes to stdout as `[provision-diag]`, matching `dependencies.py`'s `[jwt-diag]` convention (this container's log stream only captures stdout).
    - **`tests/test_auth.py`** — **16 new tests (26 → 42)**: mismatched `clerk_org_id` → 403; a caller with no active org → 403; no victim tenant details in the 403 body; attacker-supplied `admin_email` ignored in favour of the token's claim (and the victim address left unregistered); a missing `email` claim uses the synthetic placeholder and does not adopt a `domain.com` tenant; four parametrized adoption-refusal cases (non-default plan, PayU customer id, `TenantConnection`, `Invoice`); a genuinely empty domain tenant is still adopted; the collision 409 contains no DB-error markers; the admin-user insert conflict is a handled 409; and four role-reconciliation cases (throwaway-org `org_role` does not elevate, `unsafe_metadata` `role` does not elevate, an unmatched org falls back to the *persisted* role rather than a blanket clamp, and a matching org's `org_role` still applies and still syncs).
  - **Verified 2026-08-12**
    - `pytest tests/test_auth.py` → **42 passed**. Full suite `pytest tests/` → **332 passed, 1 failed**; the failure is `test_connectors.py::test_salesforce_pkce_flow`, a local-Redis connection error, re-confirmed this pass to fail identically on a stashed (pre-change) tree. No FE/website file was touched, so no `tsc` run was needed.
    - **Live before/after repro over real HTTP, with real token verification** — an RSA keypair generated per run, its public JWK served from a local JWKS endpoint, `CLERK_JWKS_URL`/`CLERK_JWT_ISSUER` pointed at it and `ALLOW_MOCK_AUTH=false`, so tokens are genuinely RS256-signed and genuinely verified by `verify_clerk_jwt()` — no monkeypatching of the auth path. Uvicorn ran the real app against a throwaway SQLite file. **Before (findings 1-6 above, all `200`):** arbitrary org claimed; victim tenant UUID/name/`pro_combined`/quota returned; `ceo@bigcorp.com` squatted; `role: Admin` + all three permissions from a throwaway org; the `pro_combined` legacy tenant renamed and claimed. **After:** arbitrary-org claim → **403**; cross-tenant read → **403** with none of the four victim tokens present in the body; email squat → **200** but the `users` row holds the *token's* address and `ceo@bigcorp.com` is still unregistered; throwaway-org escalation → **200** with `role: Viewer` and all three permissions `false` on the correct tenant; adoption attempt → **200** into a *new* tenant with the legacy row byte-identical (`Legacy Holdings`, `clerk_org_id=None`); collision → **409** with no `UNIQUE`/`constraint`/`sqlite`/`tenant.domain` markers in the body.
    - **Not verified**: nothing deployed, so no check against `ca-invoice-be-dev` or the real Clerk sign-up flow. The "before" run for finding 6 could not be produced against the pre-3b tree (the `409` branch it leaks from only exists as of 3b, and pre-3b that request was absorbed by the looser adoption check) — the leak is evidenced by the diff and the "after" run confirms the branch is reached and clean.
  - **Deployment dependency (JWT Template) — verified locally 2026-08-19**: provisioning requires the `invoice-app` Clerk JWT Template to emit **`org_id`** (mismatch/absent → `403`) and **`email`** (else synthetic `user_<id>@domain.com` because `body.admin_email` is untrusted). Confirmed on Clerk instance `great-baboon-11` via Dashboard claims plus live `[jwt-diag] org_id=… email_present=True`. Still required on any *new* Clerk instance / production cutover. Azure `ca-invoice-be-dev` has not run this same signup.
  - **Finding 7 (adoption-branch TOCTOU race) — sub-item (1) closed 2026-08-19.** Two concurrent provisions for the same `clerk_org_id` against real Postgres now have executed evidence: `tests/test_auth.py::test_provision_concurrent_same_org_id_creates_one_tenant_on_postgres` (two threads, separate sessions, `threading.Barrier` start gate) → exactly one `tenant` row, one `user` row, both calls return the same `tenant_id` with `{is_new: True, is_new: False}`. Advisory-lock mitigation in `routers/auth.py` is no longer unverified. **Still a known limitation**: the lock is released early when committing on the adoption path, so the subsequent IntegrityError fallback branch is not fully protected under concurrent execution — not covered by this test (fresh-tenant path only).

- `[x]` **Gap 137: Trainer chat fails on "Existing Vendor" (production invoice) sessions — an OCR re-run failure at load time is silently swallowed, then breaks chat later with a confusing 500** — user-reported 2026-08-05 ("trainer chat is not working when loading production invoice"), root-caused precisely in `routers/trainer.py`, confirmed **not** to affect the other two Trainer scopes, and **fixed 2026-08-06**.
  1. **`start_from_production_session()` (line ~456-462) re-runs OCR on the production invoice's file to get fresh text for later chat corrections**, but the call is wrapped so a failure is only logged: `ocr_text = ""` / `try: ocr_text, _, _ = _run_ocr_split(invoice.file_path) / except Exception as e: logger.warning(...)`. If OCR fails for any reason, the session is still created and returned successfully — the user sees the invoice load fine, with no indication anything went wrong.
  2. **The failure only actually surfaces later, confusingly, inside chat.** `trainer_chat()` (line ~563-575) passes that same `session.get("ocr_text", "")` straight into `run_trainer_agent(...)`; with an empty string, this fails, and *that* exception is what gets caught and turned into the real `500 "Failed to process chat feedback"` the user sees — on the chat call, not the load that actually caused it.
  3. **Checked "for others" — the other two Trainer scopes do not have this bug.** Both **New Vendor** (file upload, line ~344-357) and **Global scope with an uploaded file** (line ~402-410) call the same `_run_ocr_split` + `run_extraction_agent` pattern, but on failure they `raise HTTPException(500, ...)` **immediately at session-creation time** — the session is never created, and the user gets a clear, immediate error instead of a working-looking screen that breaks later on an unrelated action. Existing Vendor is the only one of the three that swallows this failure and lets a broken session through.
  - **Fix, shipped**: `start_from_production_session()` now matches the New Vendor/Global pattern exactly — a failed `_run_ocr_split(invoice.file_path)` call is logged at `error` level (was `warning`) and `raise HTTPException(500, detail=f"Could not prepare '{vendor_name}' for training: {...}")` immediately, instead of silently proceeding with `ocr_text = ""`. The failure now surfaces at the moment it's actually diagnosable (invoice load) instead of a later, unrelated-looking chat error.
  - **Note, resolved 2026-08-06**: an unrelated IDE session working on this same repo concurrently proposed a different root cause for the same user report (a `null` session from a swallowed blob-storage 500 in `get_invoice_pdf`, chained through Gap 90 and Gap 171) — checked directly against the live code and that chain doesn't hold: Gap 90 only affects the separate PDF-*viewer* endpoint, not session creation, and `start_from_production_session()`'s blanket `except Exception` means `session` is essentially never `null` on this path. The actual, verified mechanism is exactly items 1-3 above.
  - **Not root-caused further, deliberately**: *why* the OCR re-run itself fails for a given production invoice (storage access, Doc Intelligence error, a specific document's format) still hasn't been determined — this fix makes the failure fail loudly and diagnosably regardless of that underlying cause, which was the actual user-facing bug; the underlying *why* is a separate, lower-priority investigation.
  - **Verified**: new regression test `tests/test_trainer.py::test_from_production_ocr_failure_returns_500_not_a_broken_session` (mocks `_run_ocr` to raise, asserts a 500 with the vendor name in the detail, and asserts no session was persisted to `trainer_sessions._memory_store`). Full `tests/test_trainer.py` suite: **22/22 passed**. `py_compile routers/trainer.py` clean.

- `[x]` **Gap 157: Admin Console rejects "add user" for an actual admin with "must be an admin to perform this action"** — user-reported (AUT-03, Devashish's gap report, 2026-08-05), not yet root-caused at the time. **Root-caused and fixed 2026-08-09**, live, on a real admin's own account (`New_org_clerk`), same underlying cause behind a parallel billing 403 ("only Admin can upgrade") on the same account.
  1. **Confirmed via direct browser-console evidence**, not guesswork: `window.Clerk.session.lastActiveOrganizationId` and `organizationMemberships` correctly showed the real org (`org:admin`) client-side, at the same moment the server-side check failed. Backend JWT diagnostics (`dependencies.py`'s `[jwt-diag]` line) showed `org_id=None org_role=None` for the identical user/session, intermittently — present on most requests, briefly absent for a ~10s window right after navigating into Settings/Subscriptions or Admin Console.
  2. **Cause**: `apps/invoice-fe/app/api/admin/create-user/route.js` destructured `orgId` directly from `auth()` — Clerk's Next.js server SDK reading the org claim out of the session cookie. That cookie is a short-lived, periodically-refreshed JWT; it does not update the instant `Clerk.setActive({organization})` resolves client-side, so a server-side request landing in that gap sees a stale, org-less snapshot even though the browser's own live state is already correct. Same class of issue independently explains the billing-upgrade 403: `dependencies.py`'s role resolution (Gap 173) has no `org_role` to work with in that window.
  3. **Fix 1 — stop trusting the stale cookie for org lookup**: `create-user/route.js` now resolves the caller's organization by calling Clerk's Backend API directly (`GET /v1/users/{userId}/organization_memberships`, using `CLERK_SECRET_KEY` server-side) — a live, authoritative read of Clerk's actual membership data, immune to session-cookie staleness. Also switched the route's own `/auth/me` precheck token from a bare `getToken()` to `getToken({ template: "invoice-app" })`, matching `backendProxy.ts`'s existing, already-fixed pattern (a bare token carries none of this app's custom `org_id`/`org_role`/`role` claims — the same class of bug Gap 4/108 fixed once before for the main proxy path, just never applied here).
  4. **Fix 2 — stop the demotion, `dependencies.py`**: the existing-user role-sync gate used `clerk_org_id is None` as a pass condition (needed so Settings-added, never-org-member users can still get an initial role) — but that same branch let a real admin's momentarily stale, org-less token overwrite their correct stored `"Admin"` with the clamp's `"Viewer"`. This is what silently demoted the account between logins with no visible error — exactly the "Admin was there, click Add User, then it's gone" pattern reported. Fixed: role only syncs from a request that actually carried a real `org_role` claim (`if raw_org_role and org_matches and role and user.role != role`); an org-less request now leaves the stored role untouched instead of overwriting it.
  5. **Fix 3 — login hardening**: `login/page.tsx`'s `processSignIn` replaced a blind, fixed `setTimeout(..., 200)` — a guess at how long Clerk takes to populate `session.user`/`organizationMemberships` after `setActive({session})` — with an actual poll (up to 2s, every 50ms) for `metadata.orgId` or a populated `organizationMemberships` array before calling `setActive({organization})`. Doesn't fix the cookie-staleness bug directly but removes a separate, real race on the same code path.
  - **Considered and rejected**: `getToken({ skipCache: true })` to force a fresh token mint — `skipCache` only exists on Clerk's *client-side* `GetTokenOptions`; the server-side `ServerGetTokenOptions` type (used by `auth()` in Route Handlers) only accepts `template`, confirmed directly against `node_modules/@clerk/types`. No cache-bypass exists server-side in this SDK version, which is exactly why resolving org membership via a direct, independent Backend API call (Fix 1) is the right shape of fix rather than trying to force a fresher cookie read.
  - **Billing 403**: not separately patched with its own direct-API resolution — Fix 2 (stop the demotion) is expected to be the meaningful fix for it too, since the same stale-cookie window was demoting the stored Admin role that the billing check reads. If the billing 403 recurs after this ships, it needs its own investigation, not assumed to be covered.
  - **Verified 2026-08-09**: deployed via CI (`deploy-dev.yml` run `31296800452`, success), live-tested by the user against the original account this was diagnosed against — "Add User" confirmed working.

- `[x]` **Gap 172: On a genuinely new Azure environment (`rg-invoice-llm-dev`), every authenticated FE→backend call 401'd with "Missing or malformed Authorization header" even after a real, valid Clerk token was confirmed being minted — root cause: internal `http://` calls against Container Apps ingress get 301-redirected to `https://`, and Node's `fetch()` strips the `Authorization` header on that scheme-change redirect** — found and fixed 2026-08-07, after an extended live investigation (real logs, live diagnostic builds, direct network tests — not guesswork) that ruled out several real-looking but incorrect theories along the way.
  1. **Symptom**: logging in as Admin on the new environment showed only 3 sidebar tabs (Dashboard/Chat/Help) — `Sidebar.tsx` gates the rest on role/permissions from `useAuth()`, which falls back to fully anonymous the moment `GET /api/auth/me` returns non-2xx. Backend access logs showed **every** authenticated endpoint 401ing, continuously, for hours.
  2. **Ruled out, each confirmed directly, not assumed**: Clerk domain/origin allowlisting (dev instances are lenient about this per Clerk's own docs); the `invoice-app` JWT Template's claims (queried directly via Clerk's Backend API — `org_id`/`org_role`/`email` all correctly shortcoded); Key Vault secret values and references; `middleware.ts`'s route matcher; a stray `DISABLE_CLERK_AUTH` flag; Postgres health (proven via a real test `INSERT`/read-back/`DELETE`, not just `SELECT`); the JWT issuer (matches, confirmed via a real captured session token's `iss` claim).
  3. **Real, separate bug found and fixed along the way**: `NEXT_PUBLIC_CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` was never set as a **runtime** container env var on `ca-invoice-fe-dev` — only baked into the client bundle at build time (`docker/Dockerfile.fe`'s `builder` stage). `invoice-fe.bicep`'s own comment (Gap 6) deliberately omits it, reasoning that a runtime env var "has zero effect on the browser" — true for the client bundle, but incomplete: Clerk's **server-side** SDK (`auth()`/`getToken()`, used inside Route Handlers) also reads that same variable name from `process.env` at runtime, completely independently of the browser bundle. Without it, `auth()` returned no `userId` at all — confirmed by adding temporary diagnostic logging to `clerkSessionToken()` (`lib/backendProxy.ts`, kept intentionally, see below) and observing the old environment (`invoice-llm-dev`, subscription `Azure subscription 1`) **already had this set as a live, undocumented manual patch** that was never fed back into bicep source — which is exactly why the old environment worked and a fresh deployment of the new one didn't, despite both using the identical pipeline. Fixed live by setting `NEXT_PUBLIC_CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`/`CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` as runtime env vars on `ca-invoice-fe-dev`. **Bicep still needs updating** to set these two vars on `invoice-fe.bicep`'s container `env` block (in addition to, not instead of, the existing build-arg path) so future environments don't need this same manual patch — not yet done, tracked here since it surfaced in the same investigation, separate from item 4 below.
  4. **The actual final root cause**: fixing item 3 made FE reliably mint a real, valid ~800-character token (confirmed via live logs: `auth() resolved, userId: ...` / `getToken result, length: 821`) — but the backend **still** 401'd on every call. Backend-side diagnostic logging (added to `dependencies.py`, kept intentionally — see below) proved the header arrived as literally `None`, timestamped within ~60ms of FE's successful token-mint log for the same request. Traced with a raw Node `http.request()` test executed live from inside the FE container (`az containerapp exec`) against the backend's internal FQDN over plain HTTP: **`301` redirect to `https://` on the identical host**, because the backend's ingress has `allowInsecure: false` — which enforces HTTPS even for internal, same-VNet traffic, not just external. Node's built-in `fetch()` (undici) follows redirects automatically, but strips the `Authorization` header whenever a redirect crosses an *origin* — and per the Fetch spec, a scheme change (`http`→`https`) counts as an origin change even on the exact same hostname. So: FE builds the header correctly, sends it on the initial `http://` request, the request gets silently upgraded to `https://` by the ingress, and `fetch()` drops the header before the upgraded request ever reaches the app.
  - **This directly contradicts `invoice-fe.bicep`'s own existing comment** ("must use `http://` for internal Container Apps traffic") — that assumption was wrong, proven by the live `301` response, not just theorized.
  - **Fix, confirmed working live 2026-08-07** (via direct `az containerapp update --set-env-vars`, before the bicep fix): change all three internal service-to-service URLs from `http://` to `https://`:
    1. `infra/modules/compute/invoice-fe.bicep:78` — FE's `BACKEND_API_URL`
    2. `infra/modules/compute/invoice-website.bicep:83` — website's `BACKEND_API_URL`
    3. `infra/modules/compute/invoice-website.bicep:105` — website's `FE_INTERNAL_URL` (bicep param `frontendApiUrl`)
  - **Long-term architecture note** (per user decision, see Gap 131's own decision update): this fix is independent of the planned VNet/subnet consolidation and gateway (APIM/App Gateway+WAF) work — the `http→https` redirect is enforced by Container Apps' ingress layer itself for *any* caller, internal or external, and will resurface identically inside a shared subnet unless every internal service-to-service call consistently uses `https://` against each app's ingress FQDN. Adopted as a standing rule for all future internal calls, not just these three.
  - **Diagnostic tooling added during this investigation, kept intentionally (not temporary)**: `dependencies.py`'s `print()`-based JWT decode diagnostics (issuer/exp/org claims/header-presence — chosen over `logger.warning()` after confirming live that this container's log stream only captures stdout, not Python `logging` module output) and `backendProxy.ts`'s `clerkSessionToken()` outcome logging. Both already committed (`06c3b92`).
  - **Verified**: live, end-to-end, on the real new environment — confirmed by the user after the env var fix was applied directly to the running containers, before this bicep fix. Bicep fix (items above) applied in this same change.

- `[x]` **Gap 173: privilege escalation — any signed-in user could grant themselves Admin (or overwrite their role on their real tenant) via Clerk's own client SDK, no backend involved** — found and fixed 2026-08-07, while investigating why a brand-new signup ("New_org_clerk", `Clerk_payu@asd.com`) showed only 3 tabs despite being that org's own creator.
  1. **How the 3-tabs symptom on this specific account led here**: `apps/invoice-website/app/signup/page.tsx` hardcoded `unsafeMetadata: { ..., role: "admin,user" }` (two places) on signup — a comma-joined string, not a real role token. `RoleMapper.normalize_role()` couldn't map it to `"Admin"` (no match in `ROLE_ALIAS_MAP`, falls through to `.title()`), so this user's permissions all resolved `False`. Fixed the two literal strings to `"admin"` (commit `0753762`). Confirmed via the Clerk export (`clerk_export_20260807_191255.json`) that this exact bug was present on **all 7** users on the old Clerk account too — it just never mattered there, because the old account's `invoice-app` JWT Template only requested `email`/`org_id`/`org_role` claims, never `role`. The new account's template (pre-existing, not something this session configured) additionally requests `"role": "{{user.unsafe_metadata.role}}"` — newly surfacing a bug that had been dormant and harmless for as long as the app existed.
  2. **The real finding, once that was traced through**: `dependencies.py::get_tenant_context_allow_unpaid()` resolved role as `payload.get("role") or payload.get("org_role", "Viewer")` — the `role` claim (sourced from `unsafe_metadata`, which Clerk's client SDK lets *any signed-in user freely rewrite on themselves*, e.g. `Clerk.user.update({ unsafeMetadata: { role: "admin" } })` from a browser console, no backend call involved) was checked **before** `org_role` (sourced from real Clerk Organization membership, changeable only via Clerk's own permission-checked Organizations API or the Dashboard). This meant any authenticated user — including the majority added via Settings' "add user" flow, who the code deliberately never gives Admin (see `admin.py`'s `create_user`/`set_permissions`: hardcodes `role="Viewer"`, comment: *"Admin is never granted by this endpoint, only by the Clerk-side role the JWT carries"*) — could self-grant Admin purely client-side.
  3. **First fix, insufficient alone**: reordering to prefer `org_role` only closes the hole for users who are real Clerk org members. Settings-added users (confirmed via `apps/invoice-fe/app/api/admin/create-user/route.js`: calls Clerk's bare `POST /v1/users`, never adds them to the Organization) have **no `org_role` at all, ever** — for them `role` is the only signal, so a plain reorder still leaves them able to self-escalate. Fixed by clamping: when `org_role` is absent, a `role` claim that resolves to `"Admin"` is explicitly downgraded to `"Viewer"` rather than trusted.
  4. **Second, deeper hole, found when the user pushed back on whether the first fix was actually complete**: `org_role` is only meaningful for whichever Clerk Organization is currently *active* on the session — and Clerk lets any signed-in user self-serve `Clerk.createOrganization({name: "..."})`, which makes its creator that new org's `org:admin` automatically, no spoofing needed, entirely real Clerk-issued data. The existing-user branch applied `role` (freshly computed from whatever org happened to be active in that request's token) straight onto `user.role` with no check that the token's `org_id` matched the org the user's *actual* tenant was tied to — so any existing Viewer could self-escalate on their real tenant just by creating-and-activating a throwaway org of their own. Fixed by gating the role update on `clerk_org_id == tenant.clerk_org_id` (or `clerk_org_id is None`, the already-clamped non-org-member case) — a role claim for an unrelated org no longer touches this user's stored role at all.
  - **Net result**: `role` (`unsafe_metadata`, client-writable) can now only ever produce non-Admin labels, and only ever updates a user's stored role when it comes from the org their tenant is actually tied to. The only path to `"Admin"` anywhere in this function is a real `org_role == "org:admin"` claim for that user's own tenant's org — which only Clerk's own permission-checked Organizations API (or the Dashboard) can grant.
  - **Live remediation for accounts already affected**: patched `unsafe_metadata.role` back to `"admin"` for the 4 real Clerk users already touched during this migration (`Clerk_payu@asd.com`, `sanjib@abcd.com`, `larzopil@fosil.pro`, `mbirla@infinevocloud.com`) and corrected their already-synced Postgres `role` values to match their real `org:admin` membership.
  - **Known residual edge case, not fixed here**: the tenant `clerk_org_id` backfill (`elif clerk_org_id and not tenant.clerk_org_id`) still fires even when `clerk_org_id` is for an unrelated org, if that tenant's `clerk_org_id` was never set to begin with. Narrow: only matters for a tenant with no `clerk_org_id` on record, and doesn't grant anything in the request that triggers it — worth tightening if it ever proves reachable in practice.
  - **Follow-up, same change**: `apps/invoice-fe/app/api/admin/create-user/route.js` used to never add the new user to the caller's Clerk Organization at all, so Settings-added users had no `org_role` at all — they were exactly the population the clamp in point 3 above exists to protect, permanently relying on it rather than ever getting the real `org_role` signal. Fixed by adding the new user to the caller's own org (`orgId` from the caller's own `auth()` session) as `"org:member"` immediately after creation, so `org_role` is now the uniform source of truth for every user, not just org creators. Also cleaned up the now-inert `unsafe_metadata: { role: 'user' }` → `'member'` (still harmless either way post-fix, `'user'` just wasn't a recognized `ROLE_ALIAS_MAP` key).
  - **FE half of the same fix — `apps/invoice-website/app/login/page.tsx`**: with `role` no longer trusted for authorization, the login page's Admin/User role picker and its Access Denied gate (which checked the selected role against the same `unsafeMetadata.role` string, comma-split) had no correctness purpose left — `Sidebar.tsx`/`useAuth()` already resolve real screens from `GET /auth/me` regardless of what was picked here. Removed entirely, along with the 2026-08-05 two-tab "Sign In / Create Organisation" mode switcher built around it (Gap 164) — with no role to pick, "Sign In" was the only tab left, so the tab row itself no longer earned its place. "+ Create Organisation" used to switch to an intermediate static pane with its own second button linking to `/signup`; now it's a direct link to `/signup`, no extra click. Final shape: header → credentials form (or OTP form) directly → a small "New here? Create an organisation →" link below the submit button. Dead styles/helpers removed: `S.roleRow`/`roleIcon`/`accessDenied`/`createPane`/`createIcon`/`createTitle`/`createCopy`/`createBtn`/`tabRow`, `roleBtnStyle()`/`tabBtnStyle()`. `tsc --noEmit` clean on the file both times.
  - **Docs updated for this entire gap**: `apps/invoice-website/website_features/feature_4_auth_gateway.md` (Functionality items 1, 2, 7), `apps/invoice-website/website_features/website_features_tracker.md` (Gap 164, superseded-by note added), `docs/test_cases/website/feature_4_auth_gateway_tests.md` (TC-WEB4-01/04/07/08/14/15). `apps/invoice-fe/docs/fe_features_tracker.md` and `feature_1_layout_theme.md` already documented `Header.tsx` avoiding `unsafeMetadata.role` for display (a separate, earlier, already-correct decision) — left as-is, still accurate.

- `[x]` **Gap 178: Persist Doc Intelligence structured invoice JSON for extraction completeness checks** — opened 2026-08-09. DI already analyzes the full PDF via `prebuilt-invoice`, but `_run_ocr()` only kept OCR text + coordinates + field_confidence and discarded the structured field tree (VendorName, Items[], TaxDetails, totals, …). Without that snapshot there is no PDF-side reference to compare against `Invoice.items` / header fields when asking “did extraction miss a line?”.
  - **Fix applied (code)**: new nullable JSONB column `invoice.source_document_json`; alembic `d7e8f9a0b1c2`; `_serialize_di_document_fields` / `_serialize_di_field` in `queue_worker/handlers.py` (values only — no polygons); filled on inbound `handle_process_invoice` and outbound `outbound_handlers`. Local `pypdf` path leaves the column `NULL`. Completeness *compare* (DI Items vs extracted `items` → alert) is a follow-up — this gap ships the store.
  - **Closed 2026-08-10**: code on `master`/`main` (`6938596`); Azure `ca-invoice-be-dev` image includes that commit; `alembic current` on the live DB reports **`d7e8f9a0b1c2 (head)`** (migration that adds `invoice.source_document_json`). Completeness-compare alert remains a separate follow-up, not required to close this store gap.

- `[x]` **Gap 183: Tenant Autopilot Backend Ingestion & Scheduling** — **Closed 2026-08-12**. Integrated database schemas (`tenant_autopilot_configs` & `tenant_autopilot_logs`), core sync engine service (`services/autopilot_sync.py`), REST API routing endpoints (`routers/autopilot.py`), ACA Job script runner (`scripts/autopilot_job.py`), Alembic DB migration, and 18/18 pytest suite (`tests/test_autopilot.py`).

- `[x]` **Gap 185: Dashboard/chat aggregates blended money across currencies; duplicate-detection dropped `currency`** — opened and fixed 2026-08-11. Backend half of `fe_features_tracker.md` **Gap 183** (which holds the user-facing history and the FE display work); recorded here because this is where BE changes are tracked.
  - **The bug**: `Invoice.currency` (`models.py:68`, nullable) existed and was populated by extraction, but every money aggregate ignored it. `routers/dashboard.py::get_dashboard_metrics` ran four bare `func.sum(Invoice.grand_total)` case-expressions plus ungrouped top-vendors and spend-over-time sums; `routers/outbound_dashboard.py::get_outbound_dashboard_metrics` did the same on the AR side. A tenant with a $500 and a ₹40,000 invoice got one blended `40500`, which the FE then stamped a `$` on. `agents/query_agent.py::_get_tenant_stats_summary` fed the same blended number into the chat model's system prompt formatted with a hardcoded `$` — i.e. the "grounding fact" given to the LLM was arithmetic across incomparable units.
  - **Fix — aggregates**: all money aggregates now `GROUP BY` a normalized currency expression, `UPPER(COALESCE(NULLIF(TRIM(currency), ''), 'USD'))`. Read-time only: **no migration, no backfill, nothing written back** to the historical NULL rows. `NULLIF`/`TRIM` collapses an empty-string extraction into the same bucket as NULL; `UPPER` stops `"usd"` and `"USD"` becoming two currencies. Both endpoints return `totals_by_currency: [{currency, ...}]` and the old flat scalars were **removed outright**, not kept alongside — a blended number has no correct label, so leaving it on the wire only invites re-rendering it. `top_vendors`/`top_customers` and `spend_over_time`/`revenue_over_time` each gained a `currency` field (arrays of objects, matching this API's existing convention for grouped data; maps stay reserved for count-style data like `invoices_by_status`).
  - **Zero-touch respected**: `outbound_dashboard.py` was written independently rather than importing a shared helper from `dashboard.py`, per the rule in `docs/architecture/System_Journey_Developer_Guide.md` Part 3 already cited in that module's own docstring. The duplicated `_currency_expr()` is deliberate and commented as such.
  - **Fix — LLM-facing data**: `get_dashboard_insights()` builds its prompt context per currency (`totals_by_currency`, and `top_vendors_by_spend` entries carry a `currency`), and the prompt now explicitly forbids adding/comparing amounts across currencies and requires naming the currency beside any amount — there is no FX rate anywhere in this system to make such a comparison valid. `_get_tenant_stats_summary` reports `total spend per currency: USD 500.00; INR 40,000.00` with the same instruction and no hardcoded symbol.
  - **Fix — real data-loss bug**: `routers/invoices.py::_ingest_single_file()`'s duplicate-detection branch copied `vendor_name`/`grand_total`/`tax_amount`/`po_number`/dates/`items` onto the new `DUPLICATE` row but **not `currency`**. A duplicate of an INR invoice landed with `currency=NULL` and was read as USD forever after — unrecoverable, since a duplicate row never re-enters extraction. Now copied (and added to the SSE duplicate payload).
  - **Endpoints that hand-build response dicts** (rather than returning the ORM row) had to list `currency` explicitly or their FE consumers kept rendering `$`: `GET /invoices/status/{job_id}` and `GET /outbound-dashboard/invoices`. `GET /invoices` and `GET /invoices/{id}` already returned it via the full model.
  - **Tests**: `tests/test_dashboard.py` 11/11, `tests/test_outbound_dashboard.py` 18/18, `tests/test_rag.py` 10/10, `tests/test_ingestion.py` 9/9 — 48 passing. Existing assertions were rewritten to the new shape (via a `_totals(data, currency)` helper) rather than the change being reverted to suit them. New coverage: multi-currency totals never blended (incl. `"inr"`/`"  "` folding), old flat keys asserted absent in both suites, insights prompt carries both currencies and never their sum, tenant-stats snapshot splits by currency and contains no `$`, duplicate upload copies `currency`, and both hand-built endpoints expose it.
  - **Not addressed here**: webhook payloads (`services/webhooks.py` dispatch sites in `audit.py`/`outbound_invoices.py`/`handlers.py`) still send `grand_total` with no `currency`. Same class of bug, but it's an external consumer contract rather than FE display, so it was left out of this pass deliberately rather than changed as a drive-by — now tracked as **Gap 215** below.

- `[x]` **Gap 184: Programmatic API Authentication & Documentation Portal** — flagged 2026-08-10, backend built 2026-08-12 (Checkpoint 14). Static API tokens (`inv_live_...`) shown on the frontend security settings page were fully mocked — no backend concept of an API key existed at all. FE half in `fe_features_tracker.md` Gap 184.
  - **Storage** (`models.py:39-56`, `Tenant`): `api_key_hash`, `api_key_salt`, `api_key_prefix` (indexed), `api_key_rotated_at`, `api_key_last_used_at`. The raw key is never persisted — only a PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 digest over it with a fresh per-issuance 16-byte salt, so a database dump is not a set of usable credentials. `api_key_prefix` is the deliberately non-secret leading slice (`inv_live_` + 6 chars) the UI displays to identify which key is live; ~37 characters of secret remain unknown from it. Migration `alembic/versions/c9d0e1f2a3b4_add_tenant_api_key_columns.py`, all columns nullable, **no backfill** — NULL means "never issued a key", and generating keys at migration time would create credentials nobody asked for and nobody can ever read.
  - **Primitives** (`services/api_keys.py`, new): `generate_api_key()` (`inv_live_` + `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`), `generate_salt()`, `hash_api_key()`, `verify_api_key()` (compares with `hmac.compare_digest`, not `==`), `key_prefix()`, `masked_display()`, `looks_like_api_key()`. Stdlib only — no new dependency. PBKDF2 iterations are 100k, deliberately below password-KDF cost and commented as such: the input is 256 bits of machine entropy, and this runs on every key-authenticated request.
  - **Endpoints** (`routers/settings.py:180-314`): `GET /api/v1/settings/security/api-key` (metadata only — `has_key`/`key_prefix`/`masked_key`/`rotated_at`/`last_used_at`/`can_rotate`; readable by any authenticated role), `POST /api/v1/settings/security/api-key/rotate` (**Admin only**, 403 otherwise; issues first key and rotates a later one through the same write — overwriting hash+salt+prefix in one commit is what revokes the old key, since nothing remains to verify the old digest against), `GET /api/v1/settings/security/api-key/verify` (authenticated **by the key itself**, returns identity only, never tenant data — the one route that exercises the programmatic path end to end so an integrator can test a key). The raw key appears in exactly one response in the whole API, the rotate one; the rotation log line records the prefix only.
  - **Auth dependency** (`dependencies.py:739-858`): `resolve_api_key_context()` + `get_api_key_context()`, a **parallel** path to the Clerk dependency, not a replacement — nothing existing was rewired. Accepts `X-API-Key: <key>` or `Authorization: Bearer <key>` (X-API-Key wins if both; the `inv_live_` prefix is what distinguishes a key from a Clerk JWT in the shared header). Looks up by indexed prefix, then constant-time digest compare; unknown prefix, wrong key and "tenant never issued a key" are all the same 401 with the same message, so the response can't be used to enumerate which tenants have keys. Runs the same `enforce_lapse`/`refresh_free_quota`/402 billing gate as the session path. Key-auth resolves as **role `Viewer` with no can_train/can_audit/can_load** — a key proves "from the tenant's own system", not "an Admin approved this action", so `require_admin`/`require_can_*` routes do not become reachable by holding a key. There is no mock fallback on this path at all.
  - **Docs** (`routers/webhook_docs.py` new, wired at `main.py:58-64` via `app.webhooks.routes.extend(...)`): the seven outbound webhook payloads now render in Swagger UI's OpenAPI 3.1 `webhooks` section beside the inbound REST routes; documentation-only, nothing becomes callable. Schemas taken verbatim from `services/webhooks.dispatch_webhook_event()`'s `{"event", "data"}` envelope, and each operation documents the `X-Webhook-Signature` HMAC/retry/auto-disable contract. **Deviation from this entry's original text**: the events documented are the real `ALLOWED_EVENT_TYPES` seven (`invoice.completed`, `invoice.audit_required`, `invoice.paid`, `invoice.rejected`, `outbound_invoice.sent`, `outbound_invoice.overdue`, `outbound_invoice.paid`), not `invoice.failed`/`invoice.overdue` — those two do not exist as events anywhere in this codebase. FastAPI's `/docs` was already exposed (never disabled via `docs_url=None`), so no docs server was rebuilt; `main.py`'s new `description=` is what both it and the in-app Docs Hub read their authentication summary from.
  - **Tests**: `tests/test_api_keys.py` (new) — **22 passed**, `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests/test_api_keys.py -q`. Covers: digest ≠ raw key and is salt-dependent; verify accepts only the matching key; "no key issued" verifies False rather than raising; rotate stores only the hash (raw key asserted absent from every stored column); **raw key returned once** (asserted absent from the entire status-response text, not merely from a named field); **rotation actually revokes** (old key 200 → rotate → old key 401, new key 200); `last_used_at` cleared on rotation and set on use; both accepted headers; 5 parametrized invalid/missing-credential cases → 401; key resolves only its own tenant (two-tenant test); unpaid tenant → 402; non-Admin rotate → 403 and `can_rotate: false`; OpenAPI document exposes all seven webhook events plus the key endpoints. Regression: `tests/test_settings.py` + `test_auth.py` + `test_webhooks.py` + `test_rbac.py` + `test_api_keys.py` re-run together against the changed `dependencies.py`/`settings.py`/`main.py` — **122 passed**, no failures.
  - **Migration branch — resolved 2026-08-12**: this revision was one of five that had branched off `f9a0b1c2d3e4` from concurrent work (this one, `c1d2e3f4a5b6` overdue_notified_at, `a2b3c4d5e6f7` add_dropped_inbound_emails → `b8c1d4e7f209` add_webhook_delivery_logs chained on top, and `a0b1c2d3e4f5` add_invoice_deleted_at from the merged `Global-common-bugs-Fix` branch).
