Deliberately narrow trigger (an explicit numeric back-reference, not just "count went down") — a genuinely narrower follow-up SHOULD often return fewer rows and a blind "count decreased" check would false-positive on that constantly; this targets the actual reported failure shape (self-contradiction against an explicit number), not "any narrowing." **This is a symptom mitigation, not the root-cause fix** — steps 1-2 (confirm the WHERE-clause-drop mechanism against a seeded tenant, then have the SQL prompt reuse/narrow the prior turn's exact clause) are unchanged and still need that live repro before they can be built. Marked partial, not closed.

**Step 1 (live repro) done 2026-08-17 by functional-tester — hypothesis disconfirmed, real mechanism is different, and two more findings surfaced.** Seeded a dedicated test tenant with 5 invoices deliberately built so a broad "cloud" question matches via three separate SQL branches (vendor-name-only, tags-only, items-only), then ran the same real two-turn conversation against the live backend **7 times** (LLM non-determinism meant one run wasn't enough to trust). Full evidence: `docs/test_evidence/gap237_sql_repro_2026-08-17/README.md` + `raw_turns_run0.json`..`run6.json`; repro script `tests/gap237_sql_repro.py`.

1. **The defect is real and reproduces live** — 2 of 7 runs.
2. **The tracker's own hypothesis was wrong**: in both real reproductions, the follow-up's regenerated SQL dropped the **`vendor_name`** branch, not `items` — the `items` branch stayed intact in both. Step 2's fix (below) needs to target the actual dropped branch, not the one originally guessed.
3. **New failure mode, not previously documented**: in 4 of 7 runs, the follow-up generated **no SQL at all** (`sql: null`) and answered purely from the prior turn's aggregate text — a different, arguably worse shape of the same "confidently answers without a backing query" pattern this session's category-browsing test (Gap 240) also found elsewhere.
4. **The shipped step-3 hedge never fired in either real reproduction** — traced to a real bug in its own trigger condition: it compares the current turn's count against the prior turn's *total* row count, but the reported failure phrasing references a *sub-count* ("the 3 USD ones" is a subset of a larger original total) — so the mismatch the hedge is designed to catch doesn't match what it actually checks. Needs fixing alongside step 2, not left as-is.

Step 2 (the SQL-generation prompt fix) and the step-3 hedge's trigger-condition bug are still open, now scoped correctly against confirmed live evidence instead of the original guess.

**Steps 2 + 3 fixed 2026-08-17/18; gap closed.** All of it in `agents/query_agent.py`, verified live rather than by mocked test alone (see the note under Gap 241 on why that distinction is load-bearing here). Four changes:

1. **Step 2, the cause** — new `get_prior_turn_sql()` reads the most recent assistant `ChatMessage` in the session that actually ran a query (skipping RAG/CHAT turns, whose `generated_sql` is NULL), and a `PREVIOUS TURN'S SQL` block puts that exact query in front of the model. New prompt **rule 9** requires the follow-up to start from *its* WHERE clause verbatim, add the new restriction with `AND`, and never drop/merge/simplify a branch of an existing OR group — the SELECT list stays free to change. Targeted at the branch the live repro actually showed being dropped (`vendor_name`), but structural, not column-specific.
2. **The `sql: null` failure mode** (5 of 8 in this pass's own baseline, 4 of 7 in functional-tester's) — **rule 8a** states plainly that conversation history is a record of what was said, not a data source; a null `sql` on a follow-up where a prior SQL turn exists gets exactly **one** forced regeneration (`_NULL_SQL_FOLLOWUP_RETRY_DIRECTIVE`), and if the model still declines, the reply ships with `_NO_FRESH_QUERY_NOTE` appended rather than passing a history-restated answer off as query-backed. Paired with a deterministic routing override: a back-reference phrasing (`_is_narrowing_followup()`) in a session that has a prior SQL-answered turn is forced onto the SQL route, since the measured mechanism was `classify_query()` sending "explain the 3 USD ones" to RAG, which has no notion of the previous result set.
3. **Step-3 hedge trigger bug** (item 4 above) — the hedge now compares the current turn's harvested id count against the number *the user referenced*, and grounds that number by requiring it to appear verbatim in the prior reply's text (so "show me the 3 largest invoices" is not hedged), instead of against the prior turn's total row count. It also stays silent when the current turn harvested 0 ids, which is indistinguishable from an unharvestable aggregate.
4. **A prompt bug found on 2026-08-18 while verifying** — see the jsonb-cast note under Gaps 241/242 below; it applies to this gap's queries too.

**Verified live, same harness and same run count as the baseline** (`tests/gap237_sql_repro.py`, 8 runs, private backend on :8100 so the shared :8000 stack was untouched): **before — correct 3/8, no_sql 5/8, branch_drop 0/8; after — correct 8/8, no_sql 0/8, branch_drop 0/8.** "Correct" is scored mechanically (turn 2's reply must name all three seeded USD invoices, one of which matches only via `vendor_name`, one only via `tags`, one only via `items`) by `score_runs.py` in the evidence dir, not by eye. The hedge fired 0/8 — correctly, since all 8 runs surfaced everything the user referenced; its own fix is covered by unit tests, not by this run set. Evidence: `docs/test_evidence/gap237_jsonb_cast_2026-08-18/README.md` (+ the mid-pass 2026-08-17 set in `gap237_step2_fix_2026-08-17/`). Unit tests: `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py` (32). Full suite green: **616 passed, 0 failed.**

  **Impact**: Correction #4 ("flag a missed alert") — the one genuinely LLM-interpreted path in the whole Feature 18 redesign, and the mechanism Gap 225 named as the way a user reports a semantically-wrong-but-correctly-transcribed field — is non-functional against the real configured model. It also breaks the terminus of the chat wrong-data triage's `pdf_agrees=false` redirect (Gap 232/Feature 18 §8): a user who follows that whole flow correctly lands on this same broken endpoint. The fail-closed behavior itself is correct (no fabricated rule gets staged, matching the Gap 212 contract) — this is a real usability outage, not a data-integrity risk. Other trainer/extraction LLM calls in this codebase already use much larger budgets for exactly this reason (`agents/extraction_agent.py` uses 16384 and 2048, `agents/outbound_extraction_agent.py` uses 8192) — only these two Gap 217/228 calls in `routers/trainer.py` were left at 512.

  **Fix**: raise `max_tokens` on both `get_llm(max_tokens=512)` call sites in `routers/trainer.py` (lines 227 and 1230) to a budget proven safe against the deployed reasoning model (4096 was sufficient in live testing; confirm a margin above that), or switch reasoning-capable Azure deployments to `reasoning_effort`/`max_completion_tokens` handling that reserves room for visible output. Add a regression test asserting the real deployed model's `finish_reason` never comes back `length` for these two prompts specifically (the existing unit suite mocks the LLM, so it cannot catch a live token-budget shortfall).

- `[x]` **Gap 238 (BE): Chat's SQL route uses exact match instead of partial match on entity names in follow-up questions, false-negatives real vendors/customers with a suffix the user didn't type** — opened 2026-08-17, found during a live 12-turn continuous conversation test (tenant-us, real mixed inbound/outbound data). Asked *"And what's the PO number for Cascade Manufacturing?"* as an elliptical follow-up (real vendor, invoice `CMC-330217`, $2,600, `PO-88342`, confirmed to exist by direct DB query) — reply: *"No records were found for Cascade Manufacturing."* The actual generated SQL: `WHERE ... AND LOWER(vendor_name) = LOWER('Cascade Manufacturing')` — an **exact match**, but the real stored value is `"Cascade Manufacturing **Co**"`. **Distinct from Gap 210, checked directly**: Gap 210's `_normalize_string_equality()` fix only tolerates case/whitespace drift (`TRIM(LOWER(...))` on both sides of an already-equal string) — it cannot make `'cascade manufacturing'` equal `'cascade manufacturing co'`, because those are genuinely different strings, not a case/whitespace variant of the same one. This is a SQL-*generation* choice (exact vs. partial match), not a post-hoc normalization gap. **Correction found while fixing**: the "only on retry" framing above was wrong — there was no retry-triggered partial-match fallback of any kind for vendor/customer names; a syntactically valid 0-row query just returned "No records found," full stop (the only 0-row fallback, `lookup_invoice_by_number_fallback()`, is gated on an invoice-number-shaped regex and never fires for names). **Fixed 2026-08-17, two levers**: (1) added a rule (`agents/query_agent.py` SQL prompt, rule 6a) instructing `LOWER(vendor_name)/LOWER(customer_name) LIKE LOWER('%value%')` for name filters; (2) split `_FUZZY_STRING_COLUMNS` into `_EXACT_FUZZY_COLUMNS` (`invoice_number`, `po_number` — case/whitespace only, unchanged) and `_SUBSTRING_FUZZY_COLUMNS` (`vendor_name`, added `customer_name`), whose `=` rewrite in `_normalize_string_equality()` now emits `LIKE '%value%'` instead of `= 'value'` — defense-in-depth regardless of what the LLM emits, same pattern as the existing case-normalization. Verified: `tests/test_rag.py::test_normalize_string_equality_rewrites_name_equality_to_substring_like` (SQL-text assertion) and `test_generated_sql_vendor_suffix_matches_despite_exact_equality` (live execution against a seeded "Cascade Manufacturing Co" row) both added; full suite green (557 passed, 0 failed) against a real local Postgres/Redis/Chroma stack.

- `[x]` **Gap 239 (BE): RAG chat citations can reference an invoice id that has no corresponding `Invoice` row at all** — opened 2026-08-17, found during the same live conversation test. Asked *"Which vendors have freight or logistics related charges?"*, routed to RAG — reply cited 3 invoice ids for "Blue Ridge Logistics"; direct DB lookup found only **1 of the 3** actually exists (correctly, as `BRL-200981`, tenant-us) — **the other 2 return zero rows**. **Checked against Gap 192 before filing, and this is not what that gap covers**: Gap 192's soft-delete design deliberately retains Chroma/blob data for a deleted invoice so it can be restored — a soft-deleted invoice being cited would be expected, not a bug. These 2 ids are not soft-deleted rows (which would still return a row with `deleted_at` set) — they return **no row whatsoever**, meaning Chroma holds embedded chunks with no corresponding Postgres record at all. **Origin still not established** — could be a real gap in whatever deletion/reset path produced this state, or a test-environment artifact from repeated re-processing/DB resets against a Chroma volume that outlived them; the defensive fix below closes the symptom regardless of which, but the origin question itself is still open — worth a follow-up grep for any code path that deletes an `Invoice` row without a matching Chroma chunk-deletion call. **Symptom fixed 2026-08-17**: `agents/query_agent.py`'s RAG branch now collects the distinct cited `invoice_id`s and runs one batched `Invoice.id.in_(...)` existence check (tenant-scoped, same "existence not visibility" pattern as `routers/chat.py::_snapshot_invoices()` — deliberately **not** `invoice_not_deleted()`, since a soft-deleted invoice per Gap 192 is still a legitimate citation) before citations are returned or embedded in the reply text; dropped citations are logged. **Also found in the same turn, same root cause class, not addressed here**: the RAG route missed that Cascade Manufacturing Co also has a "freight" tag — a retrieval-recall gap, separate code path from citation validation. Verified: `tests/test_rag.py::test_rag_citations_drop_ids_with_no_matching_invoice_row` added; caught and fixed a real bug this test itself surfaced (`Invoice.tenant_id`/`.id` are UUID-typed columns — the raw `str` params this function receives threw `AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'hex'` until explicitly cast to `uuid.UUID` before the query); full suite green (557 passed, 0 failed). **Marked partial, not closed**: the desync's origin is still unestablished.

**Origin confirmed 2026-08-17, closing the open question**: during Gap 244's RAG rework, orphan Chroma collections (chunks with no matching Postgres invoice) grew from 38 to 84 across three consecutive `pytest` runs — 84 of 89 hold 1-2 chunks, the signature of a test indexing one document under an ephemeral `uuid4()` tenant and then dropping its Postgres schema at teardown, never deleting the Chroma side. No product code executed during that growth. **Confirmed origin: the test suite itself, not a real deletion-path bug** — the "test-environment artifact" branch of this entry's original two hypotheses is the correct one; there was never a missing-Chroma-cleanup path in application code to find. **Founder decision 2026-08-17: code fix only, no data cleanup** — fix the test fixtures (Chroma teardown, or point tests at an ephemeral Chroma path) so future runs stop accumulating orphans, but do not prune the already-accumulated orphan collections. Filed as Gap 245 below for the code fix itself.

- `[x]` **Gap 245 (BE): test suite creates orphan ChromaDB collections on every run, with no teardown** — opened 2026-08-17, split out of Gap 239's now-confirmed origin finding above. Tests that call `index_invoice_document()` under an ephemeral test tenant never call the matching `delete_invoice_chunks()` (or equivalent collection drop) when their Postgres fixture tears down, so the Chroma side outlives the test. Measured growth: 38→84 orphan collections across 3 consecutive suite runs during Gap 244's work. **Closed 2026-08-18.** Fix: `tests/conftest.py` gained a session-scoped, autouse fixture (`use_ephemeral_chroma`) that patches the default Chroma client factory to `chromadb.EphemeralClient()` for the whole test session — an in-memory client that never touches the shared local Chroma instance and is discarded automatically on process exit, so there is no collection left behind to orphan. **Verified independently** (not taken on the implementing tool's word): full backend suite re-run, 638 passed; live Chroma collection count measured before and after the run — 108 → 108, zero growth. Confirmed every Chroma access in `apps/invoice-be` routes through `get_chroma_client()` (no bypass), and embeddings are passed explicitly so the ephemeral client triggers no model download. Does not conflict with `chroma_client.py`/`scripts/reembed_chroma_collections.py` (Gap 239/244 work) — those run outside pytest, untouched by this fixture. **Per founder direction, this gap was code-fix-only — the already-accumulated orphan collections in the shared local Chroma instance from before this fix were deliberately not pruned as part of it.** Minor fidelity note: tests no longer exercise the real `HttpClient` code path at all during the suite; acceptable tradeoff for eliminating the orphan-accumulation bug.

  **Origin established and closed 2026-08-17 — the answer is that no such product code path exists.** Exhaustive grep of `routers/`, `services/`, `queue_worker/`, `scripts/`, `agents/`, `utils/`, `models.py`: **zero** hard-deletes of an `Invoice` row anywhere in product code (the only `session.delete()` calls are `admin.py` User, `chat.py` ChatSession/Message/Rule, `connectors.py`, `email_ingestion.py`, `webhooks.py` — none touch `Invoice`). Both invoice delete endpoints (`routers/invoices.py::delete_invoice`, `::rollback_batch`) are **soft** deletes by design (Gap 192), and `chroma_client.delete_invoice_chunks()` had **zero call sites in product code** — nothing ever removed chunks at all. So the "real gap in a deletion path" option can be ruled out; the other option the gap listed is correct. **Real origin measured directly**: local Chroma holds **42** `invoice_chunks_*` collections against **7** Postgres tenants, and **38 belong to tenant ids that no longer exist at all** — produced by Postgres schema teardown (every `tests/test_*.py` fixture calls `SQLModel.metadata.drop_all(engine)`) and DB resets against a Chroma volume that outlives them. A test-environment artifact, not a product defect. **The reported symptom was then reproduced exactly**: a new chunk-level orphan scan found tenant-us holding **2 chunks whose `invoice_id` matches no `Invoice` row — both `vendor_name: Blue Ridge Logistics`** (`12ffad51-933f-48c9-92d1-591a31857186`, `645e0d90-5827-42eb-abb5-6647da4739db`), matching this gap's "cited 3 ids for Blue Ridge Logistics, 2 return zero rows" precisely. **Delivered**: orphan detection/pruning in `scripts/reembed_chroma_collections.py` at both granularities — whole orphan collections, and orphan chunks inside a live tenant's collection (`_orphan_chunk_invoice_ids()`, built from a set of live invoice ids deliberately **without** an `invoice_not_deleted()` filter, since a soft-deleted invoice is a legitimate citation and not an orphan) — plus a `--prune-only` audit mode that never loads the embedding model. `delete_invoice_chunks()` now documents in place why it must stay **out** of the soft-delete paths: Gap 192 retains chunks on purpose so a restore stays possible; its callers are the pruning path and any future hard-delete/purge, and if a hard delete is ever added it must call this. After migrating tenant-us, a re-scan reports **0** orphan chunks. Evidence: `docs/test_evidence/gap244_rag_retrieval_2026-08-17/README.md` §5.

  **The mechanism was then caught happening live, which settles the origin question beyond doubt.** Over the course of this pass the orphan-collection count rose from **38 → 84** (total `invoice_chunks_*` collections 42 → 89), purely from running the BE test suite three times: 84 of the 89 collections hold 1–2 chunks each, the signature of a test that indexes a single one-page document under an ephemeral `uuid4()` tenant and then drops its Postgres schema in the fixture teardown. **No product code ran at all during that growth.** So the desync is not merely explainable as a test artifact — it is *actively manufactured by every suite run*, against a Chroma volume that has no corresponding teardown. **Left unpruned deliberately**: the `--prune-orphans` tooling is ready and the operation is safe by construction (it only touches collections whose tenant has no Postgres row, so no live tenant can be affected), but a functional-tester was working the same shared local stack during this pass and the standing instruction was not to wipe shared databases. **Recommended follow-up, not filed as a new gap without founder direction**: either have the test fixtures drop the Chroma collection alongside `SQLModel.metadata.drop_all()`, or point tests at an ephemeral Chroma path — otherwise the local Chroma instance grows a fresh batch of orphan collections on every single test run. This is local-environment hygiene, not a production defect: production tenants aren't deleted from Postgres, and no product path hard-deletes an `Invoice`.

**8-turn category-browsing chat test, 2026-08-17** — founder-directed, live against tenant-us (real mixed inbound data), mixing real categories against plausible-but-absent ones, explicitly checking whether the agent honestly says "no match" versus hallucinating one. 4 of 8 turns wrong. Three new gaps below; none overlap 238/239 (checked directly — 238 is a SQL-route exact-vs-partial-match issue on entity *names* in follow-ups, 239 is stale Chroma entries with no Postgres row, i.e. the opposite direction of Gap 240 below).

- `[x]` **Gap 240 (BE): an invoice that lands `AUDIT_REQUIRED` is never indexed into ChromaDB — permanently, not just until review** — opened 2026-08-17. Asked *"What about printing costs?"* and *"Do we have any steel or materials related invoices?"* against tenant-us — both wrongly answered "not found," despite Apex Print Solutions (`APS-410093`, $453.60) and Titan Steel Distributors (`TSD-620458`, $10,557.60) genuinely existing. **Root cause confirmed by direct comparison, not inferred**: `classify_query()` correctly routes both questions to RAG (verified by calling it directly against the real LLM, not guessed), and a real Chroma vector query does execute — but a direct diff of tenant-us's actual Chroma collection against its real Postgres invoices shows every `COMPLETED`-status invoice present and both `AUDIT_REQUIRED`-status invoices (Apex Print Solutions, Titan Steel Distributors) entirely absent. `queue_worker/handlers.py:754-770` gates the only call to `chroma_client.index_invoice_document()` on `if status == "COMPLETED":` — an invoice that comes back `AUDIT_REQUIRED` (i.e., trips any verification alert) skips indexing outright. **Confirmed permanent, not a "before review" window**: checked `routers/audit.py`'s resolve path, which can set `invoice.status` to `COMPLETED` later — it never calls `index_invoice_document()`, so an invoice that was ever `AUDIT_REQUIRED` stays unindexed for the life of the row, even after a human resolves it, unless the file is re-uploaded from scratch. This means any RAG-routed chat question can never surface a flagged invoice's content — arguably the invoices most likely to be discussed. **Secondary, related but separate finding, not fixed here**: even for indexed invoices, `chroma_client.py::query_invoice_chunks()`'s real vector distances measured 5.8–7.3 against its 0.4 relevance threshold (Gap 21) for every query tried — true semantic similarity is not functionally contributing at all right now; turns 6/7 in this same test only succeeded via the function's literal-keyword-overlap fallback. Needs its own investigation into the embedding model/normalization before concluding whether the 0.4 threshold itself is wrong or the embeddings are the problem — not scoped into this gap's fix. **Suggested fix**: call `index_invoice_document()` for `AUDIT_REQUIRED` invoices too at ingestion time (RAG content is independent of arithmetic-verification outcome — there's no reason a flagged invoice's text shouldn't be searchable), and add a call in the audit-resolve path as a backstop for any row that reaches `COMPLETED` after the fact without having been indexed.

  **Fixed 2026-08-17.** `queue_worker/handlers.py`'s gate is now the shared `chroma_client.should_index_status()` instead of `status == "COMPLETED"` — an allow-everything-except predicate backed by `NON_INDEXABLE_STATUSES` (UPLOADED, PROCESSING, PROCESSING_OCR, EXTRACTING_DATA, INDEXING, FAILED, DUPLICATE, SKIPPED_DUPLICATE). Shared deliberately: the same predicate is used by both ingestion handlers, both resolve backstops and the migration script, so the four cannot drift apart the way the `COMPLETED`/`VERIFIED` literals did. **Correction to the suggested fix, found while implementing**: the proposed backstop "for any row that reaches `COMPLETED` after the fact" **would never have fired**. `routers/audit.py::resolve_audit_invoice` validates `target_status` against exactly PAID/REJECTED/AUDIT_REQUIRED, and repo-wide `COMPLETED` is only ever set by the queue worker — so no resolve can ever produce a COMPLETED transition to key on. `target_status` is also `None` for a plain alert-dismiss/correction, which is still a resolution worth backstopping. The backstop is therefore keyed on **the resolution itself**, probing `has_invoice_chunks()` first so the normal already-indexed case costs one cheap Chroma `get` rather than a PDF download plus re-embed, and wrapped so a Chroma failure logs and never fails the human's resolve action. **Confirmed against real data, not inferred**: tenant-us has 4 COMPLETED + 3 VERIFIED + 2 AUDIT_REQUIRED + 1 NEEDS_REVIEW, and exactly **7 = |COMPLETED| + |VERIFIED|** invoices were in its pre-fix Chroma collection — the 3 missing being precisely the 2 AUDIT_REQUIRED (this gap) and the 1 NEEDS_REVIEW (Gap 243). After the re-embed migration the collection holds all 10. **The most direct proof this mattered**: post-fix, the live question *"What about printing costs?"* retrieves Apex Print Solutions (`APS-410093`, `AUDIT_REQUIRED`) at cosine distance 0.3924 — an invoice that could not previously be retrieved at all because it was never indexed. Verified: 26 tests added in `tests/test_rag.py`; full suite green (**609 passed, 0 failed**, run three times) against a real local Postgres/Redis/Chroma stack. Evidence: `docs/test_evidence/gap244_rag_retrieval_2026-08-17/`. Backfill for existing rows ships in `scripts/reembed_chroma_collections.py` (procedure documented in `docs/feature_6_rag.md`).

  **The "secondary, related but separate finding" in this entry (distances 5.8–7.3 vs the 0.4 threshold) was escalated to Gap 244 and is now known to have been measured on the mock embedding path, not the real model — see Gap 244's root-cause correction below.**

> ⚠️ **Gap-number collision warning, recorded 2026-08-18.** The unmerged branch `feature/contact-us-and-support-tickets` (commit `fc48ef0`, "Contact Us / Support Ticket Engine", Features 5/15/19) adds its **own** `Gap 240 (BE)`, `Gap 241 (BE)` and `Gap 242 (BE)` entries to this same file — `SupportTicket` model/migration, the `Application@infinevocloud.com` email dispatch service, and the support router. Those are entirely different defects that happen to reuse these three numbers. `fc48ef0` is **not on `master`** as of 2026-08-18 (verified: `git merge-base --is-ancestor` says no; it exists only on that branch and its remote), so nothing is duplicated in this file *yet* — but the two sets of entries are in different regions of the document, so a merge will most likely auto-resolve into **two Gap 240s, two Gap 241s and two Gap 242s** rather than raising a conflict anyone would notice. The three entries below (241, 242) and Gap 240 above are the **chat SQL-route / RAG-indexing** ones. Whoever merges that branch should renumber one of the two sets (the Support-Ticket set is the newer and, being unmerged, the cheaper one to move). The same collision exists in `invoice-fe/docs/fe_features_tracker.md`.

- `[x]` **Gap 241 (BE): category SQL generation expands a multi-word category phrase into bare single-word `LIKE` branches, causing false-positive aggregation across unrelated categories** — opened 2026-08-17. Asked *"How much did we spend on office supplies?"* against tenant-us — answered **$2,040.00**; the real figure is $450 (Summit Office Supplies only). **Root cause confirmed from the actual generated SQL**, not inferred: alongside the correct `LIKE '%office supplies%'` phrase match, the query independently OR's in bare `LIKE '%office%'` and `LIKE '%supplies%'` terms against both `tags` and `items`. Redwood Facilities Group's tags (`janitorial, supplies, services` — a real but unrelated janitorial-category invoice) match the bare `%supplies%` branch, pulling its $1,590 into the total: 450 + 1590 = 2040.00, an exact match to the wrong figure reported. **Suggested fix**: tighten the SQL-generation prompt's category-matching rule to require the full phrase (or a small, deliberately curated set of true synonyms decided per-category) rather than decomposing a multi-word category into independent single-word OR branches — the current behavior effectively treats every word in the user's phrase as its own unscoped filter.

- `[x]` **Gap 242 (BE): category SQL generation inconsistently limits itself to item-description text only, missing vendors identifiable by name or tag** — opened 2026-08-17. Asked *"And logistics or freight costs?"* — answered $200.00 (one Cascade Manufacturing Co freight line item); missed Blue Ridge Logistics entirely ($2,386.31, a vendor literally named "Logistics" with tags `freight, logistics`). **Root cause confirmed from the actual generated SQL**: this query only checks `LOWER(item->>'description') LIKE '%freight%'/'%logist%'/'%shipping%'` via `jsonb_array_elements` — it never checks `vendor_name` or `tags` at all, unlike this same test's turn-1 query (Gap 241, above), which did check `tags`. **Confound disclosed, not hidden**: Blue Ridge Logistics' `items` column was overwritten to a placeholder (`"TEST LINE ITEM EDIT"`, no freight-related text) during this session's own earlier Gap 234 end-to-end verification test — so this specific vendor's miss is partly my own test-data side effect, not purely the app. The query-structure gap is independently real regardless: it's inconsistent with turn 1's query about which columns get checked for a category-style question, and Blue Ridge Logistics' tags (`freight`, `logistics`) are untouched by the data corruption and would have matched had the query checked them. **Suggested fix**: same prompt rule as Gap 241's fix should also standardize which columns a category/keyword question checks (`tags`, `items` description, and `vendor_name`/`customer_name`) — right now different questions produce differently-scoped queries with no visible pattern to which columns get included.

**Gaps 241 + 242 fixed together 2026-08-17/18 — one prompt rule, because they are the same rule seen from two directions** (which text a category phrase is matched against, and how the phrase may be broken up). Both live in `agents/query_agent.py`'s SQL `system_prompt`:

- **Rule 6b (Gap 242)** — one standard shape for every category/subject-matter question: the phrase is matched against the **same four columns** in one parenthesised OR group (`tags`, `items`, `vendor_name`, `customer_name`), never a subset. The rule states outright that checking only item descriptions (or only tags) is a bug, and names the reason — which column a category is identifiable through varies per invoice ("Blue Ridge Logistics" is identifiable by vendor name alone).
- **Rule 6c (Gap 241)** — a multi-word category phrase is **never** decomposed into independent single-word branches: "office supplies" is `'%office supplies%'`, not `('%office%' OR '%supplies%')`, with the janitorial-invoice false positive from this gap's own report given as the worked example. Two categories joined by "or" are two whole phrases, each applied to all four columns. Generic spend words the user tacks on (`costs`, `spend`, `charges`, `invoices`, `bills`, ...) are stripped before the LIKE literal is built, so "freight costs" searches `'%freight%'` — no tag or line item is literally named "freight costs".

**A third defect was found on 2026-08-18 while verifying these two, and is fixed in the same place.** A live run reported `psycopg2.errors.UndefinedFunction: function lower(jsonb) does not exist` on tag/keyword questions. Root cause was the prompt's own worked example: `LOWER(tags) LIKE LOWER('%"hardware"%')`, annotated "(works in both SQLite and Postgres)". It does not — `tags`/`items`/`sa_alerts` are `Column(JSON_VARIANT)` (models.py 82-84) and `JSON_VARIANT` is `sa.JSON().with_variant(JSONB, "postgresql")`, i.e. **JSONB on Postgres, which has no `lower(jsonb)`**. SQLite is untyped and accepts the bare form, which is exactly why the entire mocked unit suite ran green over it. Rule 6 is now split into 6(a) "cast every JSONB column with `CAST(... AS TEXT)` before LOWER/LIKE, never cast the VARCHAR ones" — with the error string quoted in the prompt so the model has the reason — and 6(b) the pre-existing LOWER-both-sides rule; rule 6b's four-column group shows the cast form too. **`CAST(... AS TEXT)` rather than `::text` deliberately**, verified by executing both against both engines: `::text` fixes Postgres and breaks SQLite (`unrecognized token: ":"`), while `CAST` works on both. A repo-wide grep confirmed no other application-code site emitted an uncast `LOWER(tags|items|sa_alerts)`.

**Verified live, before vs after** (two private uvicorn instances — fixed on :8100, deliberately-reverted prompt on :8200 — so the shared :8000 stack stayed free), by `tests/gap6d_jsonb_cast_probe.py` over category-phrase and named-tag question sets: **pre-fix, 13 SQL executions aborted with `function lower(jsonb) does not exist` across 16 chat messages; post-fix, 0 across 32.** Worth recording *why the chat UI never showed this*: all 13 aborts were on attempt 1 and the SQL route's error-feedback retry loop repaired every one of them on attempt 2, so the user-visible answer looked fine while every JSON-column question silently cost an extra LLM round-trip, a failed statement and a session rollback. The probe's own SQL-shape counters read 0 pre-fix for the same reason (they see the post-repair query) — the server log is the ground truth, and both are kept in the evidence dir. Unit tests: `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py` (32 tests; 4 for the cast, two of which *execute* the predicate against the real SQLite and Postgres engines rather than asserting on prompt text). Full suite green: **616 passed, 0 failed.** Evidence: `docs/test_evidence/gap237_jsonb_cast_2026-08-18/README.md`.

**Scope note, deliberately left open**: rule 6c's phrase-preserving behaviour and rule 6b's four-column group were re-checked live only on the Gap 237 seed tenant and the probe's question set, not re-run against tenant-us's original "$2,040.00 vs $450" and "$200.00" numbers — that reseeded tenant-us data is shared with other concurrent work. The prompt rules are in place and unit-asserted; a tenant-us re-ask is the cleaner end-to-end confirmation if one is wanted.

**Confound cleared 2026-08-17 by functional-tester, ahead of the fix**: reseeded Blue Ridge Logistics' `items`. Confirmed the corruption went further than known — `subtotal` had also been silently overwritten to 200.0, no longer reconciling with the untouched `tax_amount`/`grand_total`. Reverse-solved the real pre-corruption subtotal ($2,225.00, an exact match against the row's stored 7.25% tax rate) and restored 4 realistic freight/logistics line items summing to it; `grand_total`/`tax_amount`/`tags` were untouched throughout and left as-is. Evidence: `docs/test_evidence/gap242_blue_ridge_reseed_2026-08-17/README.md` + `before_after.json`; script `tests/gap242_reseed_blue_ridge.py`. The fix can now be verified against clean data.

- `[x]` **Gap 243 (BE): outbound invoices have the same permanent RAG-indexing gap as Gap 240, on the `NEEDS_REVIEW` side** — opened 2026-08-17, filed separately from Gap 240 per founder direction (same root shape, but a distinct code path worth its own tracking and its own close). `queue_worker/outbound_handlers.py:127` gates the only call to `chroma_client.index_invoice_document()` on `if status == "VERIFIED":` — an outbound invoice that lands `NEEDS_REVIEW` (the outbound equivalent of `AUDIT_REQUIRED`) is never indexed, permanently: confirmed `routers/outbound_audit.py` (the outbound resolve path) has **no call to `index_invoice_document()` anywhere in the file** — grepped directly, zero matches. So a customer-facing invoice a human has to review never becomes searchable via RAG chat even after it's resolved, same shape as Gap 240's inbound finding. **Suggested fix, same shape as Gap 240's corrected fix (see that gap's note on why the naive "backstop on resolve" doesn't work for the inbound side — the outbound resolve path needs the same "index on resolve, regardless of final status" treatment, not a status-gated one)**: call `index_invoice_document()` for `NEEDS_REVIEW` outbound invoices at ingestion time too, and add an indexing call to `routers/outbound_audit.py`'s resolve path keyed on the resolution happening at all, not on which status it resolves to.

  **Fixed 2026-08-17, exactly as suggested.** `queue_worker/outbound_handlers.py` now gates on the same shared `chroma_client.should_index_status()` as inbound, and `routers/outbound_audit.py::resolve_outbound_alert()` got the twin backstop (probe `has_invoice_chunks()` first, index with `customer_name` to match how outbound ingestion passes it through the `vendor_name` parameter, never fail the resolve on a Chroma error). **Worth recording why the "keyed on the resolution happening at all" framing is not just a preference here**: this endpoint never mutates `invoice.status` at all — an outbound resolve is corrections plus alert dismissal only — so unlike inbound there is no status transition available to key on even in principle. Confirmed by direct read of the endpoint, not assumed. **Found and corrected while fixing**: `tests/test_direction_aware_chat.py::test_needs_review_outbound_invoice_skips_rag_indexing` was an existing test that *asserted this bug* (it locked in Feature 6.1's original `VERIFIED`-only gate). Inverted to `..._also_triggers_rag_indexing`, and paired with a new `test_unextracted_outbound_invoice_is_not_rag_indexed` so widening the gate stays bounded — a FAILED extraction has no usable content and still stays out. **Also corrected**: `feature_2.1_vendor_flow_ingestion.md` Task 2.1.3 still claimed "No RAG indexing — out of scope for ingestion", stale since Feature 6.1 Task 6.1.3 added it; the spec body now reflects what is actually built. Verified against real data: tenant-us's 1 `NEEDS_REVIEW` outbound invoice was among the 3 absent from its pre-fix collection (see Gap 240's `7 = |COMPLETED| + |VERIFIED|` arithmetic) and is present after the re-embed migration. `tests/test_rag.py::test_outbound_resolve_backfills_rag_index_for_an_unindexed_invoice` added; full suite green (609 passed, 0 failed).

- `[x]` **Gap 244 (BE): RAG semantic vector search is not functionally contributing to retrieval — real embedding distances are far outside the enforced relevance threshold for every query tested, so matches only succeed via a literal-keyword fallback** — opened 2026-08-17, escalated from a secondary finding in Gap 240 into its own gap per founder direction, since it's a distinct root cause needing its own investigation and applies identically to **both inbound and outbound** (both flows share the same `chroma_client.py` indexing/query functions — this is not two separate bugs).

  > ⚠️ **ROOT-CAUSE CORRECTION, 2026-08-17 — read this before the original analysis below.** Items **2** and **4** of the root cause as originally filed (the "measured L2 norm ≈ 1.82" and the "L2 distances of 5.8–7.3") are **factually wrong as statements about the real model**. They are measurements of the **mock** embedding path. See the "Corrected root cause" block after item 5. The gap's *conclusion* — that semantic similarity was contributing nothing and only the keyword fallback was working — is nonetheless correct, and the fix shipped in full. Items 1, 3 and 5 stand.

  **Root cause, confirmed by direct measurement, not inferred**:
  1. `chroma_client.py::get_embedding_model()` loads `BAAI/bge-m3` via `sentence-transformers`, a model whose intended similarity metric is **cosine** (i.e., it expects comparisons on unit-normalized vectors).
  2. `get_embeddings()` (`chroma_client.py:66`) calls `model.encode(texts, convert_to_numpy=True)` **without `normalize_embeddings=True`** — measured directly against the real model: returned vectors have L2 norm ≈ **1.82**, not 1.0.
  3. None of the three `client.get_or_create_collection(...)` call sites in `chroma_client.py` pass `metadata={"hnsw:space": "cosine"}` — Chroma defaults to raw (squared) L2 distance, which is unbounded and scales with vector magnitude, not a 0–2 cosine-equivalent range.
  4. Net effect, measured directly against tenant-us's real collection: genuine matches (a vendor's own indexed chunk against a query naming that vendor's category) return **L2 distances of 5.8–7.3**, evaluated against the **0.4** relevance threshold set in `query_invoice_chunks()` (Gap 21). The threshold was almost certainly calibrated assuming a bounded cosine/normalized-L2 distance space (where 0.4 is a sensible, fairly tight cutoff) — against raw unnormalized L2 it is unreachable by construction, for **every** query, not just the ones this session happened to test.
  5. The only reason RAG retrieval works at all today is `query_invoice_chunks()`'s literal-keyword-overlap override (`chunk["keyword_score"] >= min_k_score` lets a candidate through regardless of vector distance) — confirmed by this session's category-browsing test, where every correct RAG answer (manufacturing/tooling, janitorial) matched via literal keyword overlap with the vendor name or line-item text, never via genuine semantic similarity. A question phrased without enough literal word-overlap with the source document text has no retrieval path at all right now.

  **Corrected root cause (measured 2026-08-17 while implementing the fix)**:

  - **Item 2 is wrong.** Real `BAAI/bge-m3`, loaded live (2.2 GB into the HF cache), returns vectors of L2 norm **1.0**, not 1.82 — with or without `normalize_embeddings=True` — because its own `modules.json` already ends in a `sentence_transformers.models.Normalize` module (index 2, after Transformer and Pooling). The model was never the problem.
  - **`1.82` is the signature of the mock path.** `MOCK_EMBEDDINGS=true` is set in this repo's local `.env`, and `get_embeddings()`'s mock branch returned `random.uniform(-0.1, 0.1)` over 1024 dims: measured mean norm **1.847** (range 1.814–1.942), and mock-vs-mock squared-L2 distance **6.55** — squarely inside the reported 5.8–7.3 band. **Item 4's entire measured range was therefore a mock-path artifact**; no real-model distance was ever in it.
  - **Items 1, 3 and 5 are real and were independently re-confirmed.** Chroma really did default every collection to `l2` (verified: all three live local collections read back `space=l2`), the 0.4 threshold really was unreachable in that space, and retrieval really was running entirely on the keyword fallback.
  - **The fix is still worth shipping in full, on the strength of item 3 rather than item 2.** Verified live against **chromadb 1.5.9**: passing `metadata={"hnsw:space": "cosine"}` to `get_or_create_collection()` for a collection that **already exists** silently returns it still on `l2` — no error, no warning. Re-embedding is therefore mandatory, not optional, and a migration script had to be written.
  - **Blast radius, measured, and worth knowing for other gaps' evidence**: the L2 norm of every vector stored in every live local collection, before anything was migrated, was IEQ-India **1.8604**, IEQ-US **1.8570**, IEQ-Europe **1.8557** — all the mock signature, all on `space=l2`. **Not one real-model embedding existed in the local Chroma stack, so real-model RAG retrieval had never been exercised locally by any test before this pass.** Consequences: Gap 240's Chroma-vs-Postgres diff **survives intact** (it is about which invoice ids are present, which is independent of vector content, and is independently corroborated by the code gate and by the `7 = |COMPLETED| + |VERIFIED|` arithmetic); the category-browsing test's "every correct answer came via keyword overlap" **survives and is now fully explained** (with random vectors the vector channel carries no signal by construction, so the keyword fallback was mathematically the only thing that could have worked); Gaps 241/242 are SQL-route findings and never involved embeddings.
  - **Process lesson**: the mock branch now normalizes its vectors too, specifically so unnormalized mock output can never again be mistaken for a real-model measurement. `tests/test_rag.py`'s new coverage is explicitly labelled structural-only for the same reason, with retrieval-quality claims kept in `docs/test_evidence/` where the embedding mode of each measurement is stated.

  **Immediate, model-independent fix (do this regardless of any model decision below)**: add `normalize_embeddings=True` to the `model.encode()` call, set `hnsw:space: "cosine"` on collection creation (a new setting on new collections — existing collections would need re-embedding to switch space, since Chroma's HNSW index is built for one space at creation time), and re-derive the relevance threshold empirically against real bounded cosine distances instead of carrying the old 0.4 value forward unchanged. This alone is expected to make genuine semantic matches reachable without changing the underlying model, and should be tried and measured before spending on a model upgrade.

  **Separate question, needs a market decision — self-hosted `BAAI/bge-m3` vs. a hosted embedding API.** Researched current options (Aug 2026):
  - **Recommended: Azure OpenAI `text-embedding-3-large`.** This codebase already holds a live Azure OpenAI resource/credentials for the chat LLM (`AZURE_OPENAI_ENDPOINT`/`AZURE_OPENAI_API_KEY` in `config.py`) — adding an embedding deployment on the same resource needs no new vendor relationship, no new procurement/security review, and reuses the existing auth/billing path. Strong general retrieval quality, well-documented, low integration risk given what's already wired up.
  - **Highest measured retrieval quality, if a new vendor is acceptable: Voyage AI (`voyage-3-large` / `voyage-3.5`).** Independently benchmarked as leading MTEB retrieval scores in 2026 comparisons, reported ~14% ahead of OpenAI `text-embedding-3-large` and ~8% ahead of Cohere `embed-v4` on NDCG@10, with a 32K context window (useful for longer multi-page invoices). Worth it only if retrieval precision remains a business priority after the immediate normalization/cosine fix above is measured — a new vendor relationship is real integration + security-review cost this repo hasn't taken on before for RAG specifically.
  - **Keep self-hosted `BAAI/bge-m3` (fix the normalization/space bug only, no model change)** if avoiding both a new vendor and added per-token API cost is the priority — 2026 comparisons still list it as "a practical default" for self-hosting, and it may perform adequately once the actual bug (not the model) is fixed.
  - **Not recommended for this use case**: Google Gemini Embedding (multilingual/multimodal-leading, but invoices here are financial documents, not the video/audio-multimodal case Gemini is differentiated for) and Cohere `embed-v4` (multilingual leader, but Voyage AI outperforms it on retrieval accuracy specifically, which is the more relevant axis for invoice line-item/category search).

  **Suggested path**: ship the normalization/cosine-space fix first (cheap, no vendor change, likely closes most of the gap on its own), re-measure real distances and retrieval quality against a re-run of this session's category-browsing test, and only then decide whether a hosted-model upgrade (Azure OpenAI `text-embedding-3-large` first choice) is still worth it. **Applies to both inbound and outbound** — `chroma_client.py` is shared, no separate outbound work needed for this specific fix.

  **Decision 2026-08-17 (founder-directed, per architect's independent second opinion)**: fix the config bug first, keep `BAAI/bge-m3`, do **not** add Voyage AI now. Architect's reasoning, given directly to the founder and accepted: BGE-M3 is a mainstream, competitive model — the measured 1.82 norm is evidence it worked correctly, the vectors were just never normalized before comparison. Swapping to a hosted model (Voyage or Azure OpenAI) right now would very likely *appear* to fix retrieval, because hosted APIs return pre-normalized vectors by default — but that would misattribute a free one-line config fix to a new recurring per-token vendor cost. Scope for this pass is therefore strictly: (1) `normalize_embeddings=True` on `get_embeddings()`'s `model.encode()` call, (2) `metadata={"hnsw:space": "cosine"}` on all three `get_or_create_collection()` call sites (existing collections need re-embedding, since Chroma fixes the HNSW distance space at creation), (3) re-derive the 0.4 relevance threshold empirically against real bounded cosine distances rather than carrying the old value forward, (4) re-run this session's category-browsing test live to confirm real semantic matches now pass without the keyword-overlap fallback carrying them. The Voyage/Azure-OpenAI model-upgrade question is deferred, not closed — revisit only if retrieval quality is still short after this fix, and only against a clean baseline this fix establishes.

  **Fixed 2026-08-17, all four scoped items shipped.** `BAAI/bge-m3` kept, no new vendor, per the decision above.

  1. **`normalize_embeddings=True`** on `get_embeddings()`'s `model.encode()`. Against the real model this is a **no-op** (see the root-cause correction) and is stated as an explicit contract so a future model swap can't silently reintroduce unnormalized vectors. The **mock branch was also normalized**, which is the change that carries real weight here.
  2. **`metadata={"hnsw:space": "cosine"}`** on every `get_or_create_collection()` call site, via `_collection_metadata()`. Added `_collection_space()` and `_to_cosine_distance()` so the threshold means the same thing on a not-yet-migrated collection — for unit vectors squared-L2 = 2 × cosine distance *exactly*, and the measurements confirm it (every `l2` figure is precisely double its cosine twin: 0.9709/0.4854, 0.7646/0.3823, 0.8263/0.4131, …), which is itself an independent confirmation that the real model's output is unit norm.
  3. **Threshold re-derived empirically → `RELEVANCE_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD = 0.49` cosine** (was 0.4). Not chosen by intuition: the 8 category-browsing questions were embedded with the real model against real invoice PDFs, twice — once over tenant-us's 10 invoices, once over all 30 invoices of the three InvoiceEQ tenants so the false-positive floor was estimated off ~3× the negatives. Both runs produced the same separation band: **hardest genuine match that is separable at all = 0.4749** ("janitorial or cleaning services" → Redwood Facilities Group); **best false positive on a deliberately-absent category = 0.5062** ("legal or attorney fees" → Fieldstone Analytics). **0.49 is the midpoint**, the maximally drift-tolerant point between catching real matches and still honestly answering "no match". The one genuine match 0.49 misses is CMC-330217 at **0.5331** (a single $200 freight line on an otherwise-manufacturing invoice) — it sits *above* the false-positive floor, so no threshold can admit it without also admitting fabricated matches; it is left to the keyword pass, which is now a genuine second channel rather than the only working one.
  4. **Category-browsing test re-run live** through the shipped `query_invoice_chunks()`, with a real before/after on the real stack.

  **Measured before/after** (full data: `docs/test_evidence/gap244_rag_retrieval_2026-08-17/`):

  | | before (unmigrated tenant IEQ-Europe: `space=l2`, mock vectors) | after (migrated tenant-us: `space=cosine`, real vectors) |
  |---|---|---|
  | stored-vector L2 norm | ~1.857 | **1.000000** exactly |
  | distance on a genuine match | **2.3610** — 4.8× the threshold | **0.3823 – 0.4749** |
  | what carried the match | `matched_by=keyword`, always | `matched_by=vector` on 2 of 6 turns, `vector+keyword` on 4 |
  | absent categories | — | **no match returned**, correctly, on both |

  Post-fix, 6/6 real categories retrieve their correct vendor and 2/2 deliberately-absent categories honestly return nothing. **The decisive result: *"What about printing costs?"* matched Apex Print Solutions at 0.3924 with `keyword_score = 0`** — the document says "Print", the question says "printing", so there is zero literal overlap and the keyword fallback could not have carried it at any threshold. That match is pure semantic similarity, which is exactly what this gap said was missing. (Apex is also `AUDIT_REQUIRED`, so pre-fix it wasn't in the index at all — one turn proving Gaps 240 and 244 together.) Turn 6, Titan Steel at 0.3823 with kw=1 below the min_k_score of 2, is the same proof a second time. **Honest caveat**: at 0.49 several turns also admit non-expected chunks under the threshold (turn 6 admits 5) — recall-oriented by design, since the LLM filters the passed chunks afterwards, and the reason 0.49 sits at the band's midpoint rather than its top. Not a regression: pre-fix those questions returned only keyword hits or nothing.

  **To make the claim assertable at all**, each chunk returned by `query_invoice_chunks()` now also carries `keyword_score` and `matched_by` (`vector` / `keyword` / `vector+keyword`) — purely additive; `agents/query_agent.py` reads only `document` and `metadata` (verified by reading it, not assumed) and was not touched.

  **Migration**: `scripts/reembed_chroma_collections.py` — drops and rebuilds each tenant's collection as cosine and re-indexes everything passing `should_index_status()`, making it the backfill for Gaps 240 and 243 as well; also does Gap 239 orphan detection/pruning at collection and chunk granularity, has a `--prune-only` audit mode that never loads the model, refuses `--apply` while `MOCK_EMBEDDINGS` is on, and reads each rebuilt collection's space back to confirm it really landed on `cosine` rather than assuming the metadata took. Procedure (pre-flight, ordering, rollback, verification) documented in `docs/feature_6_rag.md` §"Gap 244 re-embed migration procedure". **Run so far against tenant-us only**, deliberately — the other tenants were left unmigrated to preserve the before-state control above and to avoid disturbing a concurrently-used local stack. **The remaining tenants still need the migration run before their RAG chat benefits**; until then `_to_cosine_distance()` keeps their threshold semantics correct, but their stored vectors are still mock.

  **Verified**: 26 tests added in `tests/test_rag.py` (unit-norm embeddings, `_to_cosine_distance` rescaling, cosine-space collection creation, `matched_by` channel reporting, threshold pinned inside its derived band, `should_index_status()` in both directions, and the resolve backstops); an existing test asserting the Gap 243 bug inverted in `tests/test_direction_aware_chat.py`; full BE suite green — **609 passed, 0 failed, 5 deselected, run three times** against a real local Postgres/Redis/Chroma stack. Also fixed a **pre-existing, order-dependent** suite failure surfaced by this work: `test_rag_citations_drop_ids_with_no_matching_invoice_row` asks a fixed question as the fixed `MOCK_TENANT_ID`, and the Task 6.11 answer cache lives in the *real shared* local Redis with a 1hr TTL and no per-test namespace, so a `chat_answer_cache:00000000-…:which vendors have freight charges?` entry left by an earlier run was served instead of the mocked RAG path and the test asserted against a stranger's citations (confirmed by reading the live key out of Redis). Fixed hermetically by forcing a cache miss in the test, not by clearing shared state.

  Sources: [Best Embedding Model for RAG 2026 — Milvus](https://milvus.io/blog/choose-embedding-model-rag-2026.md), [Voyage 3.5 vs OpenAI vs Cohere Embedding Models 2026](https://www.buildmvpfast.com/blog/best-embedding-model-comparison-voyage-openai-cohere-2026), [Best Embedding Models for RAG in 2026 — StackAI](https://www.stackai.com/insights/best-embedding-models-for-rag-in-2026-a-comparison-guide)

## Feature 19 — Support Ticket Engine, AI Support Agent & Notification Email Dispatch (2026-08-17)

Full BE design + verification record: `docs/feature_19_support_tickets_and_notifications.md`.

> **Renumbered 2026-08-18, before merge.** These three entries were originally filed on this branch as Gaps **240/241/242 (BE)**. Master independently opened its own Gaps 240–245 (BE) for the chat SQL-generation / RAG-indexing defects while this branch was unmerged, and a `git merge-tree` dry run confirmed this file **auto-merges without raising a conflict** — which would have silently produced two Gap 240s, two Gap 241s and two Gap 242s. This branch's set is the newer and still-unmerged one, so it was moved to **246/247/248** rather than master's. No other gap number in this file was touched.

- `[x]` **Gap 246 (BE, was 240 on this branch): No `SupportTicket` data model or migration to persist contact inquiries & escalation tickets** — **closed 2026-08-17.**
  - **`models.py`** — new `SupportTicket` SQLModel table (`__tablename__ = "supportticket"`) at end of file:
    - `ticket_number` — unique, indexed human-visible ref (`INQ-YYYY-XXXX` for website contacts, `TICK-YYYY-XXXX` for app tickets).
    - `tenant_id` — nullable UUID (anonymous for website contact form submissions, populated for authenticated app tickets).
    - `user_email`, `user_name` — submitter identity.
    - `source` — `WEBSITE_CONTACT | HELP_CHATBOT | DIRECT_TICKET`.
    - `category` — `SALES | TECHNICAL_SUPPORT | BILLING | PARTNERSHIP | GENERAL`.
    - `priority` — `LOW | NORMAL | URGENT`.
    - `subject`, `description` — content.
    - `chat_transcript` — JSONB array (full AI conversation for chatbot-escalated tickets).
    - `status` — `OPEN | IN_PROGRESS | RESOLVED | CLOSED`.
    - `admin_notes` — nullable text.
    - `created_at`, `updated_at`.
  - **Deployment**: run Alembic migration before deploying — `alembic revision --autogenerate -m "add support ticket table" && alembic upgrade head`.

- `[x]` **Gap 247 (BE, was 241 on this branch): No automated email dispatch service routing alerts to `Application@infinevocloud.com`** — **closed 2026-08-17.**
  - **`services/support_email.py`** (new) — templated HTML email dispatch via existing SendGrid helper (`services/outbound_email.py`):
    - **Staff alert** → rich HTML to `SUPPORT_NOTIFY_EMAIL` (default `Application@infinevocloud.com`): priority badge, submitter metadata table, description block, optional chat transcript table, `Reply-To: submitter_email`.
    - **User receipt** → clean acknowledgement to submitter: reference number, SLA commitment (< 2h Urgent / < 24h Standard).
    - Graceful degradation: when `SENDGRID_API_KEY` absent, returns `{"status": "skipped"}` instead of raising — ticket is already persisted, email failures never roll back the record.
  - **`config.py`** — new `SUPPORT_NOTIFY_EMAIL: str = "Application@infinevocloud.com"` field. Override via Key Vault env var for alternate environments.

- `[x]` **Gap 248 (BE, was 242 on this branch): No support router or API endpoints to intake contact inquiries, app support tickets, and AI support troubleshooting chat** — **closed 2026-08-17.**
  - **`agents/support_agent.py`** (new) — platform knowledge agent providing domain-guided troubleshooting (Trainer rules, Auditor console, Email ingestion, Webhooks, Billing), automated error condition detection (504 gateway timeouts, PayU errors, OCR stalls), and structured escalation metadata.
  - **`routers/support.py`** (new) — four endpoints:

    | Endpoint | Auth | Source field | Ticket prefix |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `POST /api/v1/support/contact` | ❌ Public | `WEBSITE_CONTACT` | `INQ-` |
    | `POST /api/v1/support/chat` | ✅ `get_tenant_context_allow_unpaid` | — | — |
    | `POST /api/v1/support/ticket` | ✅ `get_tenant_context_allow_unpaid` | `HELP_CHATBOT` or `DIRECT_TICKET` | `TICK-` |
    | `GET  /api/v1/support/tickets` | ✅ `get_tenant_context_allow_unpaid` | — | — |

  - Ticket numbers are random (4-digit suffix), non-sequential — total volume is not publicly enumerable.
  - Email dispatch is wrapped in `try/except` in all endpoints — an email outage never prevents the 201 response.
  - **`main.py`** — `support.router` registered at `/api/v1`.
  - **`tests/test_support.py`** (new) — **22/22 tests passing** (in-memory SQLite, mock email dispatch):
    - Contact form: valid payload → 201, ticket number format, DB persistence, field validation (name/email/message required, email format, message length), unknown category/urgency coercion.
    - AI Support Chat: documentation query returns guidance, error keywords trigger smart escalation card, human help triggers escalation, empty input validation.
    - App ticket: 201 response, `TICK-` format, chat transcript persisted, `HELP_CHATBOT` source stored.
    - Ticket list: empty list, tenant's own tickets returned.
    - Email: dispatch mock called once, `RuntimeError` from dispatch → still 201 with `email_dispatched: false`.
