## Feature 19 — security findings on the public contact endpoint (2026-08-18)

Opened 2026-08-18 from a **read-only** security review of the new unauthenticated contact-form path, filed at `reports/security/2026-08-18-support-contact-endpoint.md` (scoped exception to this repo's normal coding → functional → env-split → load → security order, taken because a merge-readiness review flagged the endpoint). All three were **open and deliberately not fixed in the merge-prep pass that filed them** — that pass was docs-only, and the review's recommendation that they were merge blockers rather than follow-ups was recorded here for the founder to call rather than acted on. **All three have since been fixed on branch `fix/support-contact-security-and-tenant-provisioning` (2026-08-18); see each entry below.** Findings 5–8 of the report (sync-handler threadpool exhaustion — plausible, not load-tested; tenant isolation and SendGrid key handling — both checked, no issue) are still not filed as gaps: 5 and 6 are deliberately deferred and 7–8 need no action. The report now carries a remediation-status block at the top recording exactly that.

- `[x]` **Gap 249 (BE): `POST /api/v1/support/contact` is public, unauthenticated and has no rate limiting or CAPTCHA anywhere in its path, while two other places in the codebase claim it does** — opened 2026-08-18. **Fixed 2026-08-18 in two passes; closed after the second.**

  **Pass 1 (initial fix).** Dual-layer rate limiting and anti-spam: (1) the Next.js proxy (`apps/invoice-website/app/api/contact/route.ts`) enforced an in-memory sliding window of 5 requests / 10 minutes per IP, dropped bot traffic with a silent 201 when the hidden `hp_field` honeypot was populated, and forwarded a client IP header to the backend; (2) the backend (`routers/support.py`) added `_ContactRateLimiter`, an in-memory sliding window on origin IP and email (5 / 5 minutes) returning `429` with `Retry-After: 300`. The contact page (`app/contact/page.tsx`) renders the honeypot and surfaces a retry message on 429.

  **Pass 2 (hardening — this is what actually closes the gap).** An architect review found pass 1 implemented but bypassable, and found a 500 of the same bug class the branch claimed to have fixed. All addressed:
  - **The rate-limit key was fully attacker-controlled.** Both layers took the *leftmost* `X-Forwarded-For` entry — the value the client supplies — so sending a fresh one per request reset both windows and defeated the limiter completely. Trust order is now: `X-Azure-ClientIP` **only** when `X-Azure-FDID` matches a configured `FRONT_DOOR_ID` (unset today, so ignored — Front Door is gated on `customDomainName` in `infra/08-apps.bicep` and is not deployed, making those headers forgeable); then `X-Client-IP`, which the website proxy sets from its own resolution and never forwards from the browser; then the **rightmost** valid `X-Forwarded-For` entry (the hop Container Apps' Envoy ingress actually observed); then the socket peer. `X-Real-IP` is no longer consulted. Every candidate must parse as a real IP via `_parse_ip`, so junk cannot become a rate-limit key.
  - **Limiter state was unbounded and per-replica.** The backend window now lives in Redis (sorted set per key, `EXPIRE` on every write), so it is genuinely shared across the app's 5 replicas and self-bounding. Redis being unreachable degrades to a bounded in-process window (`services/trainer_sessions.py`'s `_get_redis` fallback pattern) that prunes out-of-window keys on every check and caps tracked keys at `_MEMORY_MAX_TRACKED_KEYS` — it does not fail open and does not 500. The `defaultdict` was replaced with an `OrderedDict`, which also stops a rejected lookup from permanently creating a key.
  - **Subject-length 500.** `submit_contact_inquiry` built `f"[{category}] Contact inquiry from {name}"` with `name` capped at 255 but the prefix adding 31–41 more, overflowing `SupportTicket.subject`'s `max_length=255` → Postgres `StringDataRightTruncation` → uncaught 500. Now truncated to `_SUBJECT_MAX_LENGTH` at construction (chosen over lowering `name`'s cap so a future prefix-format change cannot reintroduce it).
  - **Honeypot false positives were invisible.** A password manager filling `hp_field` silently discarded a genuine inquiry while telling the user it was received. The proxy now logs a `console.warn` with the trigger value, claimed name/email and user agent so a false-positive pattern is visible in logs; the client-facing response is deliberately unchanged so bots learn nothing.

  **Remaining caveat, stated rather than glossed:** the *website proxy* window is still per-instance and that app scales 0–3 with scale-to-zero, so a cold start wipes it and its effective ceiling is up to `instances x 5`. This is accepted, not overlooked — that layer is best-effort edge shedding, and the authoritative limit is the backend's Redis-backed one. The backend falls back to a per-replica window only while Redis is down.

  **Verification:** full backend suite green — 674 passed, 4 skipped. 28 new tests in `tests/test_support.py` (`TestSubjectLengthOverflow`, `TestClientIpResolution`, `TestRateLimitBypassResistance`, `TestRateLimiterStateBounds`) alongside the original `test_rate_limit_returns_429_after_threshold`. The two regression tests were confirmed to genuinely fail against the pre-fix code, not just pass against the new: reverting the truncation fails the subject tests (256 and 296 chars), and reverting `_get_client_ip` fails `test_rotating_leftmost_forwarded_for_cannot_reset_window` with 201 instead of 429. Subject assertions are on the constructed/stored string's length, because the suite's in-memory SQLite does not enforce `max_length` and a status-only assertion would pass with the bug present. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `invoice-website`. Not deployed to any Azure environment.

  Files: `routers/support.py`, `config.py` (`FRONT_DOOR_ID`), `models.py` (ticket-number format comment), `tests/test_support.py`, `apps/invoice-website/app/api/contact/route.ts`, `apps/invoice-website/app/contact/page.tsx`, `docs/feature_19_support_tickets_and_notifications.md`, `reports/security/2026-08-18-support-contact-endpoint.md`.

- `[x]` **Gap 250 (BE): `services/support_email.py` interpolates attacker-controlled request fields into both email templates with no HTML escaping — an open relay for phishing from the platform's own authenticated sending domain. Highest-severity finding of the review.** — opened 2026-08-18. **Fixed 2026-08-18.** All user-supplied request fields in both email templates (`_ticket_html` and `_receipt_html` in `services/support_email.py`) are now strictly HTML-escaped using Python's `html.escape(..., quote=True)` — covering `user_name`, `company_name`, `user_email` (both display and `mailto:` link), `subject`, and `description`. Subjects are sanitized against newline injection (`\r`/`\n` stripped). Input length validation added to `ContactInquiryRequest` and `AppTicketRequest` for `name` and `company` (max 255 chars), preventing unhandled DB `StringDataRightTruncation` 500s. Covered by automated tests `test_html_in_name_is_escaped_in_receipt_email`, `test_html_in_name_is_escaped_in_staff_alert`, `test_html_in_description_is_escaped_in_staff_alert`, `test_html_in_company_is_escaped_in_staff_alert`, `test_name_over_255_chars_is_rejected`, `test_company_over_255_chars_is_rejected` in `tests/test_support.py`. Files: `services/support_email.py`, `routers/support.py`, `tests/test_support.py`.

- `[x]` **Gap 251 (BE): the ticket-number keyspace is 9,000 values per year, and exhausting it takes the contact form down with an uncaught 500** — opened 2026-08-18. **Fixed 2026-08-18.** `_generate_ticket_number()` updated to use cryptographic randomness `secrets.token_hex(4).upper()` yielding 8 uppercase hex characters (4.29 billion distinct values per year per prefix), expanding keyspace by ~477,000x and eliminating enumeration risk. Ticket format: `INQ-YYYY-XXXXXXXX` / `TICK-YYYY-XXXXXXXX`. If exhaustion retries fail, `_unique_ticket_number()` now raises `HTTPException(status_code=503, detail="Ticket generation service temporarily busy — please retry.")` rather than an unhandled `RuntimeError` crashing the endpoint with a 500. Covered by automated tests `test_ticket_number_uses_hex_format` and `test_ticket_generation_exhaustion_returns_503_not_500` in `tests/test_support.py`. Files: `routers/support.py`, `tests/test_support.py`.

- `[x]` **Gap 252 (BE): Chat (and every other request) fails against the deployed dev backend because the readiness probe checks a path that doesn't exist, so the current revision never becomes ready and stays pinned to 100% traffic** — found 2026-08-18, user-reported ("chat is giving failed to response"). Root cause, confirmed directly against the live `ca-invoice-be-dev` Container App and its replicas (not guessed): the app's Readiness probe is configured to `GET /health/readiness` on port 8000, but this FastAPI app only implements `GET /health` — `/health/readiness` 404s every 10s, confirmed continuously in the live container logs. Azure Container Apps will not mark a replica ready until its readiness probe passes, so revision `ca-invoice-be-dev--0000048` (deployed 2026-08-18 09:06 UTC) sits at `runningState: ActivationFailed`, `healthState: Unhealthy`, replica `ready: false`, while `az containerapp ingress traffic show` confirms it still holds **100% of traffic** as the latest revision — so requests routed to it, including chat, fail. **Infra-drift finding, not a bicep bug at the time this was written**: grepped this repo's entire `infra/` and `.github/` trees for `health/readiness` and found zero references — this probe was configured directly on the live Azure resource (portal or manual `az containerapp update`), not through this repo's IaC at the time. **Stale as of 2026-08-18, corrected**: `infra/modules/compute/invoice-be.bicep` now DOES declare `path: '/health/readiness'` explicitly as the Readiness probe — it landed via the Feature 19→20 observability branch merge the same day, after this line was originally written. The durable fix (a real `/health/readiness` endpoint in `main.py`, checking Postgres + Redis) has also landed via that same merge. **Also corrected**: the tracker's Feature 19/20 entry above claimed a "CI/CD post-deploy health verification gate" for this environment — false at the time for dev. Only `deploy-prod.yml` had a post-deploy health check; `deploy-dev.yml`/`_deploy-service.yml` had none, which is the reason this outage held 100% of traffic for hours with no automated signal — closed separately as Gap 258.

  **Closed 2026-08-19/20**: live-confirmed `Running`/`Healthy` on `ca-invoice-be-dev` across roughly a dozen separate deploys through the rest of this session (`--0000055`, `--0000056`, `--0000059`, and others), independently re-checked via `az containerapp revision list` after essentially every push, not just once. No recurrence of the original readiness-probe failure at any point. Gap 258 (dev post-deploy health gate) now exists and has already fired for real once — see that gap's entry for the false-positive it caught and the fix that followed, itself real evidence the gate (and by extension this fix) works as intended.

- `[x]` **Gap 253 (BE): Chat SQL route has no line-item-level extraction — a question about one specific line item returns the whole invoice's grand total instead** — found 2026-08-18, user-reported live example. Asked *"what is the current training amount"* / *"i want the amount only for training and onboarding from the total invoice"* against an invoice with two line items (Cloud Storage $765.36, Training & Onboarding $29,302.94) plus 18% tax, grand total $35,480.59. Chat answered **$35,480.59** both times — the invoice's full `grand_total`, including the unrelated Cloud Storage line and tax — not the correct **$29,302.94** (the Training & Onboarding line's own `amount`). **Root cause, confirmed from the actual generated SQL**: `agents/query_agent.py`'s only category/keyword pattern (rule 6b) is invoice-level — find invoices where `items`/`tags`/`vendor_name`/`customer_name` mention the keyword, then `SUM(grand_total)` across those invoices. There is no rule at all for reaching into the `items` JSONB array and extracting/summing just the matching line item's own `amount`, so any question asking for a specific line item's figure (not "which invoices relate to X") falls back to this invoice-level pattern and silently includes unrelated lines and tax. **Suggested fix**: add a second SQL pattern using `jsonb_array_elements(items)` to unnest line items into rows, filter on each item's own `description`, and select/sum only the matching items' own `description`/`quantity`/`unit_price`/`amount` fields (not the invoice's `grand_total`) — **correction 2026-08-18: no such precedent actually exists** — Gap 241/242's fix text above mentions `jsonb_array_elements` as an example SQL shape in this tracker's prose, but a repo-wide grep of `agents/query_agent.py` and every other `.py` file in `invoice-be` for `jsonb_array_elements`/`json_each` returns zero real occurrences. This would be the **first** JSONB-unnest query written anywhere in this codebase, not an adaptation of an existing one — treat it as new, unproven ground (including for SQLite, which has no `jsonb_array_elements` equivalent and would need its own `json_each`-based path), not a copy-paste of a working pattern. The prompt needs a rule distinguishing "give me this line's own number" (line-item extraction) from "which invoices are about this category" (existing invoice-level rule, unchanged) — that disambiguation, not just the SQL syntax, is the real design work. **Response formatting requirement, per founder direction**: a line-item answer should render as `{description}: {qty} units × ${unit_price} = ${amount}`, one line per matched item; if more than one line item matches (same invoice or across invoices), list each one that way and add a total underneath only when there's more than one. **Fixed 2026-08-19** — see `docs/feature_6_rag.md` "Rule 6d" for the full design. What shipped, in `agents/query_agent.py`: (1) **new rule 6d, dialect-conditioned at prompt-build time.** `_sql_dialect_name(db_session)` reads the live bind's dialect and `_line_item_rule()` renders exactly one of `_LINE_ITEM_RULE_POSTGRES` / `_LINE_ITEM_RULE_SQLITE` into the SQL system prompt, so the model is never shown syntax the bound engine cannot parse. This is the same mechanism rule 6(a) already settled for `CAST(... AS TEXT)` — teach one correct form per engine, never emit dialect-specific SQL and repair it afterwards. **An intermediate implementation did the opposite** (a regex rewriter inside `execute_generated_sql()` translating `jsonb_array_elements`/`->>`/`::numeric` into SQLite at execution time); it was **deleted rather than patched** — it can only ever cover the exact syntactic shapes it was written against, while the model is free to emit any equivalent one. (2) **NULL / non-array `items` guard, taught in the rule for both engines.** `items` is nullable and machine-populated, and un-nesting it unguarded aborts the query for the *whole tenant* off one bad row (verified by hand against SQLite 3.50.4: `json_each('not json')` → `OperationalError: malformed JSON`; Postgres `jsonb_array_elements` raises on a scalar/object the same way) — and each abort burns an attempt of the 3-try repair loop. Both variants therefore wrap the argument: `jsonb_array_elements(CASE WHEN jsonb_typeof(items) = 'array' THEN items ELSE '[]'::jsonb END)` / `json_each(CASE WHEN json_valid(items) AND json_type(items) = 'array' THEN items ELSE '[]' END)`. Guarding via the join's `ON` clause was tested and **rejected** — with `LEFT JOIN` SQLite still evaluates `json_each` before applying `ON` and aborts anyway. (3) **`->>` is native SQLite (3.38+)**, so the SQLite variant uses `item.value ->> 'field'` with no cast; the `.value` matters and is called out explicitly in the prompt, because `json_each(items) AS item` aliases the *table* json_each returns, not the element. (4) **Rule 7 compliance**: 6d selects `currency` in both the detail and the aggregate shape, the aggregate groups by it, and the summary-prompt format is now `{line_description}: {line_qty} units × {currency} {line_unit_price} = {currency} {line_amount}` using the row's own currency — the founder-requested format above hardcoded `$`, which is exactly what FE Gap 183 is on file for. (5) **Rule 9 gained an explicit FROM-clause exception**: this gap's own reported phrasing ("the amount only for training and onboarding **from the total invoice**") is a narrowing follow-up, so 6d and 9 fire together; rule 9 previously fixed only the WHERE clause and said nothing about FROM, which reads as "keep the previous invoice-level FROM" — i.e. return the grand total again. It now authorises adding the line-item join (and only that) while every tenant/invoice-identifying predicate still carries over verbatim. (6) **`_harvest_invoice_ids_via_companion_query()` un-broken for 6d.** It bailed on any `\bjoin\b` in the reconstructed tail, and every 6d query has one — so `result_invoice_ids` came back empty for *every* line-item answer, silently disabling the FE's "which invoice was wrong?" triage picker (Gap 231) and the Gap 237 step-3 hedge (which needs `current_count > 0` to fire). The un-nest join is now whitelisted **by shape** (`_UNNEST_JOIN_RHS`) and the companion query rebuilt as `SELECT DISTINCT invoice.id` — it multiplies rows per invoice but never changes which invoices match; a join to any real table still bails exactly as before. Rule 6d correspondingly tells the model **not** to select `invoice.id` (raw UUIDs in the user-facing results table), since identity is recovered here instead. **Verification**: 12 tests in `tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py` (38 passed / 6 skipped in that file), of which the load-bearing ones *execute* the taught SQL — extracted verbatim out of the prompt constant, so a divergence between what is taught and what is tested is impossible — against the real SQLite engine over the gap's own reported data (Cloud Storage 765.36 + Training & Onboarding 29,302.94 inside a 35,480.59 grand total, plus a NULL-`items` row and a not-JSON-at-all row): returns **29,302.94** only, with the Cloud Storage line absent and the grand total never surfaced. Multi-currency aggregate verified separately (`{"USD": 29502.94, "INR": 50.0}`, grouped, never summed across). Full BE suite: **691 passed, 2 failed, 6 skipped** — both failures are `tests/test_connectors.py` Salesforce OAuth tests needing a local Redis on :6379, confirmed identical on a stashed clean tree, unrelated to this change. **Postgres half closed 2026-08-19 (Gap 255):** `test_taught_line_item_sql_runs_on_postgres` (both parametrized cases) and `test_recommended_cast_form_runs_on_postgres` **passed** against Docker Postgres on `localhost:5433` (`invoice-postgres-local` via `docker-compose.yml`) — rule 6d's Postgres form is now executed evidence on both engines, not asserted-only. Live functional-tester repro (the three scenarios in `.claude/tasklists/senior-dev-gap253-line-item-sql-extraction.md`'s handoff section) is still outstanding; flush the 1hr answer cache before re-testing live — there is no `flush_answer_cache()` helper in `query_agent.py` (only `get_cached_answer`/`set_cached_answer`/`_cache_key`); the existing flush paths are `routers/chat.py::_invalidate_chat_answer_cache()` and `routers/trainer.py::_invalidate_chat_answer_cache()`, or delete the Redis key `chat_answer_cache:{tenant_id}:{normalized question}` directly.

- `[x]` **Gap 254 (BE): Help Center AI Support Assistant answers only a narrow slice of real questions, and presents every miss as a diagnosed incident requiring escalation** — found 2026-08-18, user-reported ("help agent was telling everything not supported"). Two distinct causes confirmed, not one: (1) **BE Gap 252's outage was live at the time** and would independently produce this symptom — every backend call including `/api/v1/support/chat` was failing, though the frontend's outage message ("temporary connection issue communicating with the troubleshooting service") is textually distinguishable from this gap's fallback message, so which one a given user actually saw can be confirmed by the exact wording. (2) **A real, separate defect exists in the assistant's own logic, independently reproducible against a fully healthy backend**: `agents/support_agent.py`'s `KNOWLEDGE_TOPICS` matcher does raw substring matching (`kw in lower_query`) with keywords as short as 2-3 characters, not word-boundary matching. Tested 24 realistic product questions against the real matcher — **11 of 24 fell through to the generic "raise a ticket" fallback** (upload, delete, file formats/limits, bulk upload, user invites, usage/dashboard, data retention, OCR correction, "what is SAGE") and **4 more matched the wrong article via a substring collision**: "What does the **conf-id-ence** score mean?" and "Can I **prov-id-e** feedback?" both hit the `"id"` keyword and return the password-reset article (first in the topic list, so it's the de facto wrong-answer default on any tie); "stuck in **pro**cessing" hits `"pro"` and returns the pricing guide; "**data** encrypted at rest" hits `"data"` and returns the CSV-export guide. Only 7 topics exist total, matching the four hardcoded prompt chips in `SupportChatWindow.tsx` — the demo path works, most of the real question space does not. Compounding this: the fallback path unconditionally sets `suggest_escalation=True`, so an unanswered question renders the same red "Issue Diagnosis & Recommended Escalation" card as a genuine detected error, rather than an honest "I don't have an article for that." `evaluate_support_query()` also accepts a `history` parameter and never reads it, so a natural follow-up ("how do I do that?") always misses regardless. **Suggested fix**: word-boundary matching with a sensible minimum keyword length (kills the substring-collision class outright); a materially expanded topic set covering ingestion/upload, invoice statuses, dashboard/analytics, user management, retention/security, and file limits; a distinct low-confidence "no article for this, try the Knowledge Base search or here's where to look" response path that does not trigger the incident-card styling; actually reading `history` for follow-ups. **Fixed 2026-08-19, with one sub-feature deliberately descoped — hence `[~]`, not `[x]`.** See `docs/feature_19_support_tickets_and_notifications.md` for the full resolution order. What shipped in `agents/support_agent.py`: (1) **word-boundary matching with a 3-character minimum** (`_score_topic()`, `MIN_KEYWORD_LENGTH`), which kills the `"id"`-in-"confidence" / `"pro"`-in-"processing" substring-collision class outright; the `"id"` keyword itself is gone from `account_auth`. (2) **Five new topics** — `ingestion_upload`, `invoice_statuses`, `dashboard_analytics`, `user_management`, `security_retention` — taking the set from 7 to 12. (3) **The `"data"` example from this entry needed more than word boundaries**, and was re-confirmed broken after them: `"data"` is a genuine whole word, so "Is my data encrypted at rest?" tied 1-1 on hit count between `export_reports` and `security_retention`, and Python's stable sort resolved the tie by list position — returning the CSV-export guide for a security question. Fixed two ways: `"data"` dropped from `export_reports` (its other seven keywords cover the topic fully), **and** the sort key is now `(matched keyword count, total matched keyword length)`, so a longer/more specific phrase match (`"at rest"`, `"data security"`) beats a short generic one instead of list order deciding. Verified against that exact question. (4) **The low-confidence path no longer sets `suggest_escalation=True`** — an unanswered question was rendering the same red "Issue Diagnosis & Recommended Escalation" card as a genuine detected incident. (5) **Bonus, pre-existing bug found and fixed in the same matcher**: KB topics are matched *before* `ERROR_TRIGGERS` and return early, so `billing`'s `"payu"` and `"checkout"` keywords made `ERR_PAYU_BILLING_FAILURE` **unreachable** — "payu error on checkout", "my payment failed at checkout" and "checkout crash" all returned the pricing article instead of the payment-failure troubleshooting card. Both keywords removed. This was not caused by this pass; it was live already. `tests/test_support.py::test_error_triggers_are_not_shadowed_by_kb_keywords` is now a standing screen over every trigger phrasing, so any future keyword addition that re-creates the collision fails there rather than in production, and the pre-existing `test_error_keyword_triggers_escalation` ("504 gateway timeout during batch sync") still passes — none of the five new topics use a keyword that shadows it. **Follow-up resolution (item 4) shipped separately as Gap 256 (2026-08-19):** `SupportChatResponse.topic_id` + `SupportChatRequest.last_topic_id` + FE carry-forward in `SupportChatWindow.tsx` — anaphoric follow-ups like "how do I do that?" now resolve against the prior matched topic; `history` remains accepted but unread.** **Scope expansion, called out rather than done silently**: fixing the follow-up backend-only would have removed the only *contextual* in-chat path to raise a ticket on a miss (a header-level "Raise Ticket Directly" button does still exist), so a third, neutral UI state was added — `SupportChatResponse.low_confidence` (new, defaulted `False`, so an older FE build is unaffected) drives a plain "Didn't find an answer? / Raise a Ticket" card in `apps/invoice-fe/components/help/SupportChatWindow.tsx` with no red framing and no implied incident. **Verification**: `tests/test_support.py` 70 passed (15 in `TestSupportChatEndpoint`, including 4 Gap 256 cases); full BE suite 691 passed / 2 failed (both `test_connectors.py` Salesforce OAuth, needing a local Redis on :6379 — confirmed identical on a stashed clean tree) / 6 skipped. FE `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **Live Help Center click-through verified 2026-08-19** (FE Gap 243): miss `how do I wash my car?` → neutral low-confidence card + prefilled Normal ticket; 504 chip → red incident card + Urgent 504 subject. Two stale claims corrected in this same pass, both load-bearing for Gap 252 above: the "grepped infra/ and found zero `/health/readiness` references" line was true when written but is now stale — that path landed in `infra/modules/compute/invoice-be.bicep` via the Feature 19/20 observability merge — and the claim of a dev-side "CI/CD post-deploy health verification gate" is false; only `deploy-prod.yml` has one, dev has none, which is why Gap 252's outage ran undetected for hours.
- `[x]` **Gap 255 (BE): rule 6d's PostgreSQL form has never been executed against a real PostgreSQL** — opened 2026-08-19, **closed 2026-08-19**. `uv run pytest tests/test_chat_sql_quality.py -k postgres -v` run against Docker Postgres on `localhost:5433` (`invoice-postgres-local` via `docker-compose.yml`): `test_taught_line_item_sql_runs_on_postgres` (both parametrized cases) and `test_recommended_cast_form_runs_on_postgres` all **passed** — rule 6d's Postgres form is now executed evidence, not asserted-only.

- `[x]` **Gap 256 (BE): the Support assistant cannot resolve a conversational follow-up, and cannot be made to without an API contract change** — opened 2026-08-19, **fixed 2026-08-19**. `SupportChatResponse.topic_id` echoes the matched `KNOWLEDGE_TOPICS` entry; `SupportChatRequest.last_topic_id` carries it forward from `SupportChatWindow.tsx`; `evaluate_support_query()` resolves short anaphoric follow-ups (`_FOLLOW_UP_PHRASES` + `_is_anaphoric_follow_up()`) against that id after the direct keyword pass misses. `history` remains accepted but unread — only `last_topic_id` resolves follow-ups. **Verification:** `tests/test_support.py::TestSupportChatEndpoint` 15 passed (4 new Gap 256 cases); FE `SupportChatWindow.tsx` stores `topicId` and sends `last_topic_id`.

- `[x]` **Gap 257 (BE): the Dead-Letter Queue alert rule can never fire — it filters on a metric dimension that doesn't exist** — opened 2026-08-19, **fixed 2026-08-19**, found during the Feature 20 observability merge-readiness review. `alert-rules.bicep`'s `dlqPoisonAlert` filtered `metricName: 'QueueMessageCount'` on a `QueueName` dimension. Confirmed live against Azure (`az rest` on the storage account's metric definitions): `QueueMessageCount` and `QueueCapacity` both report **no dimensions** (`dims: null`). **Fix:** replaced the metric alert with `Microsoft.Insights/scheduledQueryRules@2023-03-15-preview` scoped to the Log Analytics workspace, KQL `ContainerAppConsoleLogs_CL | where Log_s has "POISON MESSAGE ISOLATED"` (same line `queue_worker/main_worker.py::_route_to_dead_letter_queue()` already emits, same query shape as `dashboard.bicep`'s DLQ panel). New resource name `alert-${workerAppName}-dlq-poison-isolated` so an already-deployed metric alert is not a type-change conflict. `09-monitoring.bicep` now passes `logAnalyticsWorkspaceId` + `queryAlertLocation`. **Verified:** `az bicep build --file infra/09-monitoring.bicep` compiles (only pre-existing BCP334 warnings). **Not verified:** Azure deploy of Stage 9, or a live poison-message fire. DLQ *routing* logic is unchanged.
- `[x]` **Gap 258 (BE): dev environment has no post-deploy health check, so a broken revision can hold 100% of traffic undetected indefinitely** — opened 2026-08-19, **fixed 2026-08-19**, found while diagnosing Gap 252's outage. `deploy-dev.yml`/`_deploy-service.yml` had none — confirmed by reading both files. **Fix shipped:** (1) `_deploy-service.yml` now polls the latest revision's `healthState`/`runningState` for up to 4 minutes immediately after `az containerapp update`, failing the job on `ActivationFailed`/`Failed` or timeout — applies to every dev/prod service deploy. (2) `deploy-dev.yml` gained a `verify-deployment` job (Gap 258) matching prod's gate pattern: public website ingress curl, backend `GET /health/readiness` curl, then all four dev Container Apps must be `provisioningState=Succeeded` with their traffic-bearing revision `Healthy`/`Running`. **Not yet live-verified against Azure** — will exercise on the next `deploy-dev.yml` run; code review + parity with prod `verify-deployment` is the close evidence here.
